(6) **Detection ("Found on this host")**, read-only at runtime, never running a program: today's profiles (the config's, or the ones seeded from T3's settings) with their ids, names, models and quota; a wrapper on PATH is *read* (a text script up to 256 KB) for `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and model variables, and for a secret only where it comes from (`$VAR`, `$(cat /path)`); a secret written into the wrapper is dropped and the find says so; the DeepSeek or OpenRouter endpoint is assumed from the quota kind or the name when the wrapper says nothing; `~/.claude` and `~/.claude-*` with a `.credentials.json` (existence only); `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` in fyrd's environment (by name). Import on the owner's click copies a found key into the secret store. (7) **Cut-over.** On the first start with providers and none stored, fyrd imports each find whose id is one of today's profiles (`claudeAlt`, `claudeAcme`, `claudeDeepseek` keep their ids, so threads, handover and quota keep working), marked imported; found keys stay where they were found (no copy without a click). It runs once (`providers.cutover` in kv). From then on the providers are the profiles and the config's or T3's profiles only feed detection. (8) **Status**: ready; needs a login (an account whose wrapper logged in with `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is a *token login*: its folder's login, often the main account's `~/.claude`, never counts and it does not start without its token, "This account logs in with a token. Run `claude setup-token` while logged in to it and paste the token as its key."; any other Claude account whose folder has no login and no stored token: "Log in once in a terminal on this host: run `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=~/.claude-x claude` and type /login. Or paste a token from `claude setup-token` as its key."); needs a key (an endpoint without one: paste it). No login flow in this slice. (9) **Settings.** A gear at the bottom of the desktop rail and "Settings" in the phone menu open Settings: Providers (each with name, kind, models as an editable list of ids, default model and effort, quota line, status and key field; "Found on this host" with Import; "Add provider": a Claude account with a folder from the host folder picker, or an endpoint with name, base URL, key and model ids), Hosts (this host), Notifications (the push control), Appearance (placeholder). Phone: 44 px targets, inside the safe areas.  **Token accounts (found live 14 Nov).** An account that logs in with a token has the token as its quota identity and reads usage with that token only; without it, it shows no numbers (never the folder's login). A provider that cannot run (no token or key yet) carries `unavailable` (the reason) in the state; the new-thread picker shows it disabled ("needs setup") and Hand over does not offer it. Settings shows "token login" instead of a folder. *Amended by V0.122*: a token account's quota comes only from its sessions' `rate_limit_event`. *Amended by V0.119*: Settings is four pages behind one nav (Providers with System agents, Automation, Projects with Hosts, This device) instead of one page of sections; a provider is its own page, its models are rows (reorder, ★, ×, add) instead of a text box, and the key and token fields sit behind Set… / Replace…. |
| V0.51 | → 3.17. **Fronts and hosts, slice 1: one machine, two roles (decision 0016).** (1) **Roles.** `fyrd` runs as `host` (runs threads; serves the host protocol and `/mcp`, never the web client), `front` (serves the web client and the browser API; holds no threads) or `both` (the default, so an unchanged config keeps working). A front is configured with a list of hosts only (`hosts: [{name, url}]`, flag `-hosts name=url,…`). In `both` the front reaches the local host over a real loopback link (an ephemeral `127.0.0.1` port) through the host protocol, the same client it uses for a remote host; it never calls the engine or the store. `/mcp` on the main port goes to the local host. (2) **The host protocol** is the v0 API (docs/API-v0.md) without the web client: `GET /api/state` (snapshot, `?since=REV`), `GET /api/stream` (SSE: `hello`, `thread`, `thread.patch`, `event`, `project`, `deploy`), `POST /api/command`, `GET /api/threads/{id}` and its `events`, `events/{seq}`, `changes`, `pr`, `handover`, the attachments and previews, and the host-wide calls (folders, providers, quota, T3, push, presence). The browser API does not change. (3) **Ids and routing.** Thread ids stay unique; the front keeps an id-to-host map and sends each command and each thread call to the thread's host; a call without a thread goes to `args.host` (or `?host=`) when given, a project command to the project's host, anything else to the first host in the list. The front stamps every thread and project with `host` (the host's name). Its revs are the host's, raised by one when only the front's stamp changes, so the client's keep-the-higher-rev rule (V0.2) and `?since=` (V0.38) hold. (4) **An offline host.** When the link to a host drops, the front keeps that host's last threads and marks each with `hostOffline` (RFC 3339, since when); commands and thread calls for them get `503 {error: "<host> is offline since HH:MM; its threads are read-only"}`. When the link returns the front reloads the host's state and clears the mark. The state carries `hosts: [{name, online, since, error?}]`; Settings → Hosts lists them, and an offline thread's header says "<host> offline since HH:MM · read-only". (5) **A remote host's guard.** A host may also listen on a tailnet address (`hostListen`); there it serves only peers whose Tailscale identity (LocalAPI whois of the connection) is the owner's login, and ignores `Tailscale-User-Login` headers. The loopback guard (V0.14) is unchanged. Not in this slice: a second machine, a front's push and presence (they stay on the host), the cache on disk across a front restart. |
| V0.52 | → 5.14. **The live view stays current during a turn.**  A process with the live view also registers a PostToolUse hook. After a tool call, when the run has gone `ReminderAfter` (4 min) since the latest of its start, the last view_* call and the last reminder, Fyr adds a one-line reminder to the agent's context: update the headline and the items that moved, or call view_unchanged. At most one reminder per 4 min; none without a live view or outside a run. The view prompt asks for updates when an item starts or finishes, not only at the end of the turn. The Stop hook (V0.37) stays. Both are notices of the agent feed (decision 0020, #47). Cost is measured in issue #17. |
| V0.53 | → 3.16. **Login through Fyr (decision 0015, the login row).** *Amends V0.50 (8): "No login flow in this slice".* From Settings → Providers the owner logs a Claude account in without a terminal and without copying a token. (1) **Start.** A Claude account shows **Log in** when it needs a login (**Get a new token** or **Log in again** in its details otherwise); `provider.login {id}` starts the CLI's own login on the host. Only one login runs per provider (a second start gets 409 "A login for X is already running"); an endpoint has none. (2) **Two kinds.** A token account (V0.50 *token login*) runs `claude setup-token` on a pseudo-terminal (it draws only in a terminal) in a private throwaway folder (0700, removed afterwards), never in the account's folder, which may hold another account's login; the token it prints goes straight into the secret store as the provider's key, and the process is ended. Any other Claude account runs `claude auth login --claudeai` on pipes with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to its own folder (made 0700 when missing; none for `~/.claude`), so the CLI keeps and refreshes its own login there; it is done when the CLI exits 0 and the folder has a login. Both run the plain `claude` on PATH with fyrd's environment minus `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_*` and the display, with `BROWSER=true` so nothing opens on the host. (3) **The page.** `GET /api/providers` carries `canLogin` and `login: {kind, state, url?, message?, expires}`; states `starting`, `waiting` (the sign-in link is shown), `checking` (a code was sent), `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `timeout`. The row shows **Open the sign-in page** (the CLI's link, opened in a new tab), a field for the code the sign-in page shows and **Send code** (`provider.loginCode {id, code}`, written to the CLI's input), the time it ends, and **Cancel** (`provider.loginCancel {id}`). A wrong code shows the CLI's error line; setup-token then starts over on Enter, which Fyr presses, and the new link replaces the old one; `auth login` ends (failed, **Try again**). The client polls every 1.5 s while a login runs. A finished login stays visible for 5 min. (4) **Limits.** A login ends after 10 min (`timeout`, "The login timed out after 10 minutes. Start it again."); Cancel and the timeout kill the CLI's process group; removing the provider cancels its login. (5) **Secrets.** The CLI's output is read in memory only and cleared when the login ends: never logged, stored or sent; a client sees only the link and an error line with anything token-like removed. The code is never logged or echoed back. fyrd logs only "login for <id> started/<state>". The status then follows V0.50: a token account is Ready once its key is set, a folder account once its folder has a login. (6) **Fallback.** The V0.50 texts stay as the second way (paste a token from `claude setup-token`; or run the CLI in a terminal). Phone: every target 44 px. Not verified with a real account in tests: a fake CLI (link, code, token) drives the flow; a `live`-tagged test runs the real `claude` in a throwaway HOME up to a refused made-up code. |
| V0.54 | → 3.17. **Fronts and hosts, slice 2: a second machine (decision 0016).** (1) **Push on the front.** The front holds the device data: push keys and subscriptions (in its machine's database; in `both` the same rows the host kept, so subscribed devices keep working) and presence (`POST /api/presence` stays on the front). It follows each host's stream and applies V0.20's plan to every thread of every host: asking and failed push at once, done and blocked after 20 s if still true, never for a thread a device of this front watches or in quiet hours (V0.46); a change the front missed (an outage, its own restart) pushes when it sees the host again. Hosts send no push. Each front pushes to its own devices: two fronts are two sets of devices; watching a thread on one front does not quiet the other. (2) **The cache on disk.** The front writes the last state of each host (threads as the host sent them and as published, projects, profiles, deploy) to `<data>/front/<host>.json`, at most every 2 s and when it stops. After a restart a host it cannot reach shows from the cache at once: its threads marked `hostOffline` (since the front last had it online) and read-only, until the link returns. (3) **Files by host.** An upload goes to `?host=` or `?thread=` (the thread's host; a new thread's project's host), else the first host; an attachment or preview read goes to `?host=` / `?thread=`, else the host the front learned for that id (its upload, or the preview page whose files these are), else the first host. No host is tried in turn. The client sends the thread's host with every attachment, preview and markdown image URL and with each composer's uploads. (4) **State per host.** `hosts[]` adds `profiles` (that host's, decision 0015) and `deploy`; the top-level `profiles` is the merge by id (the first host's wins), so every thread finds its profile; the new-thread dialogs list the project's host's profiles and send `projectId`, which the front routes to the project's host. `actions` are the first host's. `GET /api/quota` merges every online host's (`?host=` for one). (5) **Deploy per host.** The drain and the switch stay on the host (V0.24). A host's `deploy` frames reach the browsers only from the front's local host (its own update, the banner); another host's update shows on its Settings → Hosts row ("online · updating Fyr") and as an offline host while it restarts; nothing else changes on the other front. (6) **Phone.** With more than one host a row names its host and the thread header carries a host chip; a thread on an offline host says "<host> offline" in its row and "<host> offline since HH:MM · read-only" under the header chips, with one host too. (7) **NixOS.** `hostListen` takes `tailnet:PORT` (fyrd reads this machine's tailnet IPv4 from tailscaled; until it is up fyrd exits and the unit restarts it); `hostPort` sets it and opens the port on tailscale0 only; the unit starts after tailscaled when it listens there; `role front` needs hosts and `tailnet.enable` needs a front. docs/API-v0.md has the two machines' config. |
| V0.55 | → 3.15. **A project is one folder (decision 0017).** *Amends V0.23, V0.28, V0.30 and V0.42.* (1) **The model.** A project is `{id, name, color, icon, folder?, profile?, model?, quiet?}`: a name, a colour and one folder on its host (a full path, `~` expanded, that must exist there, else 400 naming the path), or no folder (threads are filed in it by hand). With decision 0016 the project lives on the host of its folder; the front stamps `host` on it. The defaults (profile, model) and quiet hours (V0.46) stay. Removed: `sources` (folder and repo sources), the main folder (`main`, `project.setMain`), `repos` and the automatic repo line, repo matching across hosts, refs and covers, the workspace records and their providers (`workspaceId` on threads and the `workspaces` table), `suggest` and `project.createFrom`, `project.addSource` and `project.removeSource`. (2) **Which threads land in it.** A thread not filed by hand lands in the project whose folder is the thread's folder or contains it; the longest folder wins, ties by project id. Scratch folders, the home folder and `/` have no rule of their own. Matching runs when a thread is made or adopted, at start, and for every thread not filed by hand after `project.create` or a change of a project's folder; it moves a thread to the project it finds and never takes a project away. A thread filed by hand (`thread.setProject`, including `""` for No project, `project.create {threadId}`, `thread.create {projectId}`) keeps its project: nothing matches it again. (3) **Commands.** `project.create {name, folder?, threadId?}`; `project.update {id, …, folder?}` sets the folder (`""` clears it) and then matches. A new thread in the project without `cwd` starts in its folder. (4) **New project from this thread.** The thread DTO's `newProject` is `{name, folder}`: the thread's folder and its last path element; absent for a scratch folder, the home folder, `/`, and a folder a project already has. (5) **Migration at start.** A project stored with `sources` keeps one folder: the one marked main on this host, else its first folder on this host, else its first folder on any host; every other folder and every repo source is dropped, and the host logs each drop with the project's name. Threads keep their projects. The `workspaces` table is dropped. (6) **The project screen** (desktop and phone) shows Name, Folder (the short path with "Threads started in this folder or below land here." and "Change…" through the host folder picker; without a folder "No folder: you file threads here by hand." and "Choose folder…"), Quiet hours, Colour, Icon and the defaults. The create step shows Name and Folder the same way. The "Make project “<name>”" chip and menu item are gone; "Project…" stays. The repo of a project, when a screen needs it later (the project's PR page), is read from the folder's `origin` then, not stored. Archive (decision 0008) is not built yet.  *Amended by V0.119*: the project screen has no Save button; each field saves at once with only its own field, quiet hours 600 ms after the last change. |
| V0.56 | **No live view line in Conversation.** *Amends V0.37.* The headline bar above the conversation that opened the live view is gone. The View tab is the way in; its headline still shows in the inbox row. |
| V0.57 | **The View-tab chat can be dismissed.** *Amends V0.49. Removed by V0.128: there is no View-tab chat.* A round × beside the chat hides it. It stays hidden for that message, per thread and device (local storage), and shows again when a new message is typed on the View tab. 44 px target on touch screens. |
| V0.58 | → 3.15. **Projects in Settings, and Delete project.** *Amends V0.50 and V0.55.*  (1) **Projects in Settings.** Settings (the desktop dialog and the phone screen) has a Projects section after Providers. It lists every project by name, each row with its icon, its folder (the short path, or "No folder: filed by hand") and its thread count. A row opens the project screen (V0.55 (6)). On the desktop, closing the project screen opened from Settings returns to Settings; on the phone, Back does. "New project" opens the create step (V0.30) in the section, without a thread; after it the new project is in the list. No projects: "No projects yet." (2) **Delete project.** The project screen ends with "Delete project…". It asks once: "Delete <name>? Its N threads stay and land in a project whose folder covers them, or in No project. The folder on disk is not touched." `project.delete {id}` removes the project (an unknown id is 404). Every thread in it loses the project and the filed-by-hand mark, then matching runs (V0.55 (2)), so a thread may land in a project whose folder contains its folder. The stream sends `event: project` with `{id, deleted: true}`; a front removes the project from its list and passes the event on; the client removes the project. The device that deleted it closes the project screen (back to Settings when it came from there); on another device an open desktop project screen closes, and the phone's says "This project is not here anymore." There is no undo. Archive (decision 0008) is still not built. *Amended by V0.119*: the desktop Project dialog is gone; a project is a sub-page of Settings → Projects ("Edit …" opens it there), with no Save button. |
| V0.59 | → 3.13, 3.14, 5.12. **Changes are records (decision 0018).** *Amends V0.8, V0.12, V0.25, V0.36.* (1) **The record.** A thread holds `changes`: `{id, key, kind, title, targets[], branch?, revisions[], phase, label?, conditions[], evidence[], group?, after[], needs?, phaseBy, createdAt, updatedAt}`. Kinds: code, infra, host, data, doc, design, other. Phases: `working`, `review`, `landed`, `live`, ends `abandoned`, `reverted`. A condition is `{type, status: ok\|fail\|pending, reason?, source: agent\|observed\|owner, at}`; evidence is `{kind, url?, attachment?, title?}`; a revision is `{id, summary?, url?, by, at}`, the 20 newest kept. At most 50 changes per thread; an ended change older than 30 days is dropped first. (2) **Facts beat claims.** A phase or condition with source `observed` is not changed by the agent; the tool says so. The agent never sets a condition of type `review` or `approval` to ok. (3) **Observed: git.** After each run the host reads the thread folder's branch, base, commits ahead of the base, unpushed commits (ahead of the upstream, or all when there is none) and changed files. When any is non-zero on a branch, the thread has a `code` change for that branch (`working`) with the observed conditions `pushed` (ok when nothing is unpushed) and `clean` (ok when no file is changed). *#35:* that change is a host record, one per repo and branch on the host, read also from each change's `workspace` (decision 0019); every thread in its folder shows it, the Changes board once, and a code change of the thread on the same branch follows it (decision 0018, addendum). It lands when the branch is merged into its base and is abandoned when the branch is gone without a merge. (4) **Observed: GitHub.** Every PR of the thread is a `code` change with the PR as evidence, found by its URL, else by its head branch. Open is `review`, merged `landed`, closed `abandoned`; conditions `checks`, `review` and `conflict` are observed. The poller reads the PR of every code change not ended, not only `pr`. `thread.pr` stays the main PR (the PR tab, the merge gate). (5) **The agent's tools.** `change_open {key, kind, title, targets?, branch?, group?, after?, revision?, pr?}` returns the change with that key or makes it (idempotent). `change_revise {change, revision}` adds a revision (the same id twice is one) and sets the observed and reported conditions to pending. `change_status {change, phase?, label?, conditions?, needs?}`. `change_attach {change, evidence[]}`. `change_request {change, ask: review\|approve\|merge\|apply\|deploy\|revert, args?, reason?}` opens a Decision; `merge` with a PR runs `pr.merge` and `deploy` with `{dir}` runs `fyr.deploy`, every other ask reports the owner's answer back to the agent. `change_list {scope?: thread\|project, id?}` returns changes with their facts. `change` is an id or a key. `pr_link` opens a code change with the PR; `effect_request` stays. Each call adds a `change` event that the conversation shows as one line. (6) **Attention.** After the PR's own reasons, a change not ended blocks the thread when a condition fails ("<title>: <reason>") or it needs the owner ("<title>: needs you"). (7) **Settle.** `settle_when {condition: "change", change, phase}` settles the thread when the change reaches that phase (`landed` also counts as reached by `live`). (8) **The Changes tab** lists the changes above the branch diff, newest first: kind, title, phase rail (working · review · landed · live, or the end), "reported" when the agent set the phase, label, the failing and pending conditions, targets, evidence links and the newest revision's summary. The inbox chip shows the most urgent change that is not a PR when the thread has no open PR. |
| V0.60 | → 5.13. **Actions: one model for changes, the thread and the live view, scripted and agent-driven (decision 0018).** *Amends V0.37 and V0.59.*  (1) **One model.** An action (an *offer*) is `{id, on?, label, run: fyr\|script\|agent, effect?, argv?, cwd?, timeoutSec?, prompt?, in?: this\|new, when?, revision?, onOk?, primary?, by: agent\|observed, digest}`. Its holder is a change, the thread (`on: "thread"`) or the thread's live view (`on: "view"`); each holds one `{offers, runs}` (#31): `changes[].offers` and `changes[].runs`, the thread's `offers` and `runs`, the view's `live.offers` and `live.runs`. At most 12 per holder, the 10 newest runs kept: `{action, label, by, at, running, ok, exit, output (the last 4 KB), endedAt}`. `when`, `revision` (`bind`) and `onOk` exist only on a change; elsewhere they are 400. (2) **Three ways to run.** `fyr`: a built-in Fyr effect (`pr.merge`, `pr.rerunFailed`), only from Fyr. `script`: Fyr runs `argv` exactly, without a shell, in `cwd` (default the thread's folder), with a time limit (default 600 s, at most 3600 s). `agent`: the press sends `prompt` to the thread's agent (`in: this`) or starts a new thread with it in the same folder and project (`in: new`). (3) **Only the owner runs an action.** No agent tool runs one. The press is the command `action.run {threadId, on, action, digest}` (`on`: a change's id or key, `thread`, `view`); `digest` is the fingerprint of every field the owner saw, and a different one is 409 ("The action changed since you saw it"). A change's action is available in the phases of `when` (default every phase not ended) and, when bound, only while its revision is the newest, like a saved Terraform plan; no action while it runs. The client shows a script's `argv` and folder, and a `fyr` action's effect, in a confirmation before it sends the press; an agent action is sent at once. (4) **Results.** On a change, a script that exits 0 sets the condition `action:<id>` ok and moves the change to `onOk`; one that fails sets it to fail ("<label> failed (exit N)"), which blocks the thread (V0.59 (6)). Elsewhere a failed script moves `activityAt`. Every press and result is an `action` event, shown as one conversation line with the output folded; the result of a `fyr` or `script` action is also told to the thread's agent as a `[Fyr]` notice of the agent feed (decision 0020, #47): one sentence with the output's first line, the rest in `fyr_state`, whoever started it; on an archived or settled thread it waits until the thread is revived. (5) **The agent declares** with `offer_action {on, id, label, run, argv?, cwd?, timeoutSec?, bind?, onOk?, prompt?, in?, when?, primary?, remove?}`; ids starting with `fyr.` are Fyr's. A view action needs the live view on, and the view's actions are removed when the owner turns it off. (6) **The live view runs its actions.** The view bridge adds `fyr.run(id)` and `data-action="id"` next to `fyr.send` and `data-send`; they post `ui/action {action}`, and the client presses the view's declared action with that id (with its digest), so a view page cannot run anything the agent did not declare. `data-send` stays: it sends the owner's message. (7) **Built in from observed state** on a code change with a PR in `review`: Merge (`fyr`, primary when nothing fails), Re-run failed checks (`fyr`) and Fix CI (`agent`, primary) while checks fail, Address review (`agent`) while changes are requested, Rebase (`agent`) on a conflict. They follow each poll. (8) **Where they show.** A change's actions on its card in the Changes tab, primary first, agent actions marked ✦, the newest run of each with its output folded; the thread's actions in the thread header above the tabs (desktop and phone); the view's inside the view page. |
| V0.61 | **New project by name, never an existing one by accident.** *Amends V0.30 (1).*  In a project list with a New project row (the new-thread picker, desktop and phone, and the thread's picker): typed text that is not exactly a project's name (any case, spaces trimmed) turns the first row into New project “<text>”, also when other projects match; Enter then opens New project with that name. Enter picks an existing project only when the text is its exact name. Arrow down and Enter, or a click, still pick any match. Without a New project row (the inbox filter) Enter picks the first match as before. |
| V0.62 | → 5.14. **Changes are kept current, like the live view.** *Amends V0.37, V0.52, V0.59.*  (1) **Which changes.** The agent's open changes: not ended, and the phase not set by an observer. Changes Fyr observes (git, GitHub) keep themselves current and never count. (2) **Upkeep.** Every change tool call that succeeds (`change_open`, `change_revise`, `change_status`, `change_attach`, `change_request`, `offer_action` on a change) and the new `change_unchanged {reason}` set the thread's `changesCheckedAt`. The changes are stale when the thread has open agent changes and `changesCheckedAt` is before the run's start. (3) **End of a run.** The Stop hook of V0.37 checks the view and the changes together and blocks once per run when either is stale, with one reason that names what to update ("… or call change_unchanged with a reason, then stop"); the same rules apply (not while background tasks run, not in a forced turn, once per run). A forced turn that only called view tools or `change_status`, `change_revise`, `change_attach`, `change_unchanged`, `change_list` keeps the run's own summary. (4) **Mid-turn.** The reminder of V0.52 also goes out for stale changes after 4 minutes without a change tool call, on its own clock. (5) The Stop and PostToolUse hooks are registered for every process with Fyr's tools, not only threads with a live view. |
| V0.63 | **New thread: the chip shows the project being made.** *Amends V0.30 (4), V0.61.*  While the New project step is open in the new-thread dialog (desktop) and screen (phone), the project chip shows the project being made: a + icon, the name as it is typed (empty: "New project") and "New · <folder>" or "New · no folder"; the folder line follows its folder. Start with the step open makes the project first and then starts the thread in it, so the thread never lands in the project picked before; an empty name is "A project needs a name." Cancel in the step, or opening the picker again, shows the project picked before. |
| V0.64 | **The Changes board across threads (decision 0018).** *Amends V0.59.* (owner chose "Changes board across threads" from the options.) (1) **One screen** with every change of every thread that is not archived (settled threads included): a dialog opened from a Changes button in the desktop rail, above Settings, and a screen on the phone from the More menu (URL `?changes`). (2) **Columns by stage:** Needs you (a change not ended with a failed condition or `needs: owner`, whatever its phase), Working, Review, Landed, Live; Ended (abandoned, reverted) only with "Show ended". Newest first in a column. Only stages with changes show as columns; one line above them gives the count of every stage. (3) **Groups and stacks.** Changes with the same `group` stay together under the group's name; inside a group a change comes after the changes it builds on (`after`). (4) **Filters:** a search over the change's title, key, targets and group and the thread's title; a chip per project that has changes, and All projects. (5) **The card** is the Changes tab's (V0.59 (8), V0.60 (8)) with a line naming its thread, which opens the thread; its actions run on that thread. (6) The rail button and the menu item show how many changes need the owner. The board reads the threads the client has; it adds no host call. |
| V0.65 | → 3.4. **Changes: what changed since you looked.** *Amends V0.25, V0.59.*  (1) **The look.** `GET /api/threads/{id}/changes` adds `head` (the commit, "" without commits) and per file `blob`: the git blob id of its content now ("" when it is deleted or not a file), with uncommitted and untracked files hashed by `git hash-object` without writing them. When the owner leaves the Changes tab (another tab, another thread, or the app goes to the background) after it loaded, the client sends `thread.changesSeen {head, files: {path: blob}}` with the whole-branch list it showed last. It is kept in the owner's View (R19), not on the thread, and is not sent in the thread DTO; Refresh inside the tab keeps the look it opened with. (2) **New since you looked.** With a stored look the reply adds `since: {head, at, mode, commits, files[], rangeDiff?, rangeDiffTruncated?}`. A file is new when its blob differs from the one seen, also when it is new to the branch or no longer changed; uncommitted edits count when their content differs from what was seen. `mode` is `ancestor` when the seen head is an ancestor of `HEAD` (`commits` counts the commits since), `rebased` when it is not (`rangeDiff` is `git range-diff <base> <seenHead> HEAD`, without a base `<seenHead>...HEAD`, cut at 400 KB), and `missing` when the seen commit no longer exists. The new files carry status and +/− from the seen head to the working tree (`missing`: their whole-branch values), sorted by path. `?path=<p>&since=<head>` returns that file's diff from the seen head to the working tree, an untracked file as added; `since` is a hex commit id, else 400. The tab shows "New since you looked · N" and "Whole branch" as two choices, the first chosen when N > 0; a `rebased` look also lists the commits as range-diff shows them (kept, changed, added, dropped), each changed one with its interdiff folded. Without a look, or with nothing new, the tab shows the whole branch only. (3) **Viewed.** Each file row has a Viewed checkbox (44 px on the phone). `thread.fileViewed {path, blob, viewed}` stores the mark with the file's blob; a file counts as viewed only while its blob equals the mark, so the mark resets by itself when the content changes. The reply's files carry `viewed`. The tab says "N of M viewed" for the list shown. A look drops the marks of files no longer in the branch; a look and the marks hold at most 1,000 files each (more is 400). (4) **What this version changes.** When the thread has a `code` change (V0.59) whose branch is the diff's branch, the 3 newest revisions with a summary show above the file list, newest first, with a short id and their age. |
| V0.66 | → 3.13, 7.6. **Merged to live: GitHub Deployments, and CI on the diff lines.** *Amends V0.59 (4) and (8), V0.60 (7); builds the two items decision 0018 left out.* (1) **The merge commit.** The poller's `gh pr view` also reads `headRefOid`, `mergeCommit` and `mergedAt`. When it sees a change's PR merged and the change is not `live`, the change keeps `deployWatch {repo, sha, mergedAt}`. (2) **Deployments.** On the same 2-minute poll, Fyr reads GitHub Deployments of each watched merge commit: the first time the repo's newest deployments (`gh api repos/{o}/{r}/deployments?per_page=30`), then that commit's (`…/deployments?sha=<sha>`), and the newest status of the newest deployment per environment (`…/deployments/{id}/statuses?per_page=1`). A repo with no deployment at all ends the watch on that first call, and nothing changes: one call per merged PR. An environment is production when the deployment says `production_environment`, or its name is production or prod. (3) **Production.** `success` (or `inactive`, a later deploy replaced it) moves the change to `live` (observed), adds the environment URL (else the log URL) as evidence `deployment`, and sets the observed condition `deploy` ok. `failure` or `error` sets `deploy` fail "deploy to production failed", adds the log as evidence `deploy-log`, moves `activityAt`, and so blocks the thread (V0.59 (6)). Anything else (queued, pending, in progress, no status yet) is `deploy` pending "deploying to production". (4) **Other environments** are conditions `deploy:<environment>` with the same ok, fail and pending; they never move the phase. (5) **The watch ends** when the change is live, when production failed, when the change ends, 24 h after the merge when the commit has no deployment, and 7 days after the merge otherwise. (6) **Built-in action.** A landed change whose `deploy` failed offers "Investigate the failed deploy" (`agent`, `in: this`, primary), with the environment and the log in its prompt (V0.60 (7)). (7) **CI on the diff lines.** While a code change's PR is open and its checks fail, the poller reads the head commit's check runs (`gh api repos/{o}/{r}/commits/<sha>/check-runs`) and the annotations of each failed run (`…/check-runs/{id}/annotations`), at most 10 runs and 50 annotations, once per head commit and number of failed checks. The change carries them as `annotations [{path, line, endLine?, level: failure\|warning\|notice, message, title?, check}]`; they are cleared when the checks stop failing or the PR is not open. (8) **In the Changes tab.** A file row shows the number of annotations on that file. The open file's diff shows each annotation under the new-side line it names ("<check>: <message>", with the title first when there is one; failures before warnings and notices); an annotation whose line is not in the diff shows above the first hunk with "line N". |
| V0.67 | **One snooze list.** *Amends 13.1 `s`.* (owner chose the phone's list for both, 2026-11-15.) Desktop and phone offer the same snooze times, from one function, in this order: In 1 hour, In 3 hours, This evening (18:00, only before 17:00), Tomorrow morning (06:00), Monday morning (06:00; a week ahead on a Monday). Each shows its time ("15:41", "today 18:00", "Sun 06:00"). On the desktop, keys `1`–`5` pick them in the order shown, so without This evening `4` is the last. |
| V0.68 | → 5.13. **Confirmations are Fyr's own.** *Amends V0.58 (2) and V0.60 (3).* (owner, with a phone screenshot of a script action: "I want these dialogs to be nicer.") (1) **One confirmation.** Every confirmation in the client is Fyr's own: a bottom sheet on the phone, a dialog on the desktop. The client never calls the browser's `confirm()`, `alert()` or `prompt()`. It asks before a script or `fyr` action (V0.60 (3)), Delete project (V0.58 (2)) and Remove provider. (2) **Title.** The action's label ("Set the Acme token", "Merge"), or the question for a destructive action ("Delete Garden?"). (3) **A script** shows its command in monospace. An absolute path in it is shortened: the home folder as `~`, a path inside a scratch folder as `…/<scratch folder>/<rest>`; every other argument is shown as written, quoted as a shell would need it. Under it, one muted line names the folder ("in ~/…/scratch/<name>"; a folder outside a scratch folder with `~` only). A tap on the command (a hover on the desktop) shows the full command and folder; Copy puts the full command on the clipboard. A bound action adds "Only on revision <id>". (4) **A `fyr` action** says in words what happens, as the plain decision cards do (V0.27): "Fyr merges the pull request on GitHub. The agent gets the result." (5) **A destructive action** (Delete project, Remove provider) states its consequence (V0.58 (2)'s words) and its primary button is red. (6) **Buttons.** The primary button names the action ("Run", "Merge", "Delete project"), never "OK"; Cancel is beside it. Focus starts on the primary button. Cancel and Escape close it, and on the phone so does a tap outside; Enter confirms on the desktop. While it is open, the inbox keys do nothing. (7) **Fits.** The sheet fits the phone sizes of V0.17 above the bottom inset, its buttons at least 44 px tall; a long command wraps inside it. (8) **The live view asks too.** An action the view's page runs (`fyr.run(id)`, `data-action`) goes through the same confirmation as its button; an agent action is sent at once. A page therefore cannot run a script or a Fyr effect without the owner's press. |
| V0.69 | → 5.2. **The agent names its threads.** *Amends V0.5 (rename), V0.22.*  (1) The Fyr tool `thread_rename {title, id?}` renames this thread, or a thread this thread started (`id`, its `parent` is this thread); any other thread is refused ("you can rename this thread or a thread it started"). (2) A title is one line: whitespace and line breaks fold to single spaces, it is trimmed, it is not empty, and it has at most 80 characters. (3) The thread keeps `titleBy` (owner or agent). A name the owner gave (`thread.rename`) is kept: the agent's rename is refused with "the owner named this thread; ask them to rename it" (a row of the field rights, decision 0020). (4) A rename is the event `thread.renamed {title, by}`; the same title again changes nothing. |
| V0.70 | → 3.13. **A landed change is history; one change per branch.** *Amends V0.59 (1) and (6), V0.43 (2).*  (1) **Landed.** A change keeps `landedAt`, set when it first reaches landed and cleared when it goes back to working or review. A failed condition blocks a landed or live change only when it was recorded at or after `landedAt`: in the same call as the landing (a health check after an apply) or later (a deploy that failed). A failure recorded before the landing is shown as history and blocks nothing. `needs: owner` still blocks. A stored landed change without `landedAt` takes its last update. (2) **One change per branch.** `change_open` for a `code` change with a branch, whose key is new, joins the thread's code change on that branch that is in flight (working or review) in the same repo (the same `repo:` target; when either has none, the branch decides). The new key becomes an alias of that change: every tool finds it by either key, and nothing is made twice. (3) **The PR tab after a merge.** On a merged or closed PR, failed checks show muted, under "Checks at merge", with no "Ask the agent to fix this check". |
| V0.71 | → 3.16, 5.13. **A thread another thread starts follows it; a press counts once.** *Amends V0.22, V0.60 (2) and (3).*  (1) A thread that `thread_new` or an agent action with `in: "new"` starts takes the starting thread's provider, model and effort unless the call names them, and has it as `parent`. (2) Every press runs until its result lands, agent actions included: an agent action to this thread ends when the message is sent, one with `in: "new"` when the thread exists ("Started thread <id>." is its result, and the pressing thread's agent hears it); a second press while it runs is "It is running". (3) A press of the same action within 2 s of the last one is the same press and is refused ("It was just pressed"), whatever sent it (a double tap, a view page that calls `fyr.run` twice). |
| V0.72 | → 3.13, 5.2, 5.3. **A PR that does not exist ends its change; the owner ends any change.** *Amends V0.59 (4), V0.60 (7), V0.66, V0.70.*  (1) **Does not exist.** When `gh pr view` answers that the PR's repository or the PR does not exist (GraphQL "Could not resolve to a Repository" or "Could not resolve to a PullRequest", or HTTP 404), the poller reports the PR with state `MISSING` as an observed fact (`pr.observed`). The change moves to `abandoned` (observed) with the label "the PR does not exist on GitHub"; it is no longer in flight, so nothing polls its URL, the built-in actions go (Merge among them) and the PR effects' guard says "The PR is not open". The agent hears "PR #88: does not exist on GitHub." No change is made for a missing PR that no change has. (2) **Two answers.** The poller reports it at the second such answer that comes at least 1 minute after the first, so a poll later: the text is unambiguous for this token, but a PR GitHub has only just made may not resolve on a kick right after it opened, and one poll costs 2 minutes. An answer in between starts the count again. A transient error (network, rate limit, 5xx, a failed login, a time-out) changes nothing. A repository the token can no longer read also answers "Could not resolve"; the owner sees the label and the agent can open a new change. (3) **End this change.** The owner command `change.end {change, phase?, reason?}` ends any change of the thread that has not ended, an observed one too (the owner decides): `phase` is `abandoned` (the default) or `reverted`; the reason (one line, at most 200 characters) becomes the label. The change's actions go, its PR is no longer polled, and the agent hears "The owner ended PR #88 as abandoned: <reason>." The agent cannot send it, and the field rights keep the owner's end against the agent and against later facts (a link or a digest of the same PR does not reopen it). More work is a new change with another key (V0.70 rules). (4) **Where.** A small text button "End this change" on every change card that has not ended, in the Changes tab and on the Changes board (not on a host record), 44 px on the phone. It asks in Fyr's own confirmation (V0.68): title "End this change?", the text "Fyr stops watching its PR and removes its actions. The agent is told; more work on it becomes a new change." ("watching it" without a PR), a choice Abandoned or Reverted, an optional reason, and the button "End change". The PR chip of a missing PR says "missing" ("Does not exist on GitHub"), and Dismiss PR is offered as for a closed one. |
| V0.73 | → 5.14. **A live change needs no upkeep.** *Amends V0.62 (1).* (owner chose "Live needs no upkeep", 15 Nov, after ending four finished deploy cards by hand to stop the end-of-run reminder.) The upkeep rules (the Stop reason and the mid-run reminder) count the agent's changes that are not ended and not live. A live change counts again only while something about it fails after its landing (V0.70 (1): a deploy or a health check), because then the agent has work on it. A live change stays on the board as done. |
| V0.74 | → 3.15. **A quiet project stays out of New thread; a 30 s hold shows it for 30 minutes.** *Amends V0.46 (4).*  (1) **Hidden.** While a project is quiet (V0.46 (2): it carries `quietUntil`), the project list of New thread (desktop dialog and phone screen) leaves it out, its search does not find it, and the form never starts on it (the project filter or the remembered project picks it only when it is not quiet). The project the form already has picked stays in the list when its window starts. (2) **The hold.** Below the list, while any project is quiet, one row says how many quiet projects are hidden and "Hold here for 30 s to show them for 30 minutes". The owner keeps a finger, or the mouse button, on the row for 30 s; a fill and a countdown show the progress. Lifting the finger, moving off the row or leaving the list before 30 s resets it to zero. There is no key and no tap for it, on purpose. (3) **Shown.** After the hold, quiet projects are in the list for 30 minutes on that device (browser storage), and the row says "Quiet projects show until 21:14" with "Hide" to end it at once. When the 30 minutes end, the list hides them again without a reload. The inbox, the counts and the notifications keep V0.46; only New thread changes. |
| V0.75 | → 5.13, 13.5. **A used action says so where it was pressed; the filmstrip scrolls with the conversation.** *Amends V0.60 (4) and 13.5.* (1) **The result on the button.** An action carries its newest run: `fresh` (never run), `running…`, `✓ <label> · HH:MM` after a run that succeeded, or `✕ <label> · failed (exit N)`. A used action loses its primary weight and turns muted, and it stays pressable, because a second run is legitimate; its tooltip says what it does and how the last run ended. A failure keeps its weight and takes the signal colour, and only a failure keeps a line of its own under the bar. The last run's output is one control away, not a row. (2) **The filmstrip is inside the conversation.** The thread's previews scroll with its messages instead of holding a band under the tab row, so a thread without previews has no band at all; on the phone the strip is the same one line it was (13.5). (3) **The header's gaps.** The offers block takes 12 px from the chips row and 6 px between a bar and its lines, and no block adds a fixed gap under the tab row (which is what left an empty 66 px band). |
| V0.76 | → 3.13, decision 0019. **A branch behind its base is not merged into it; the facts say where the base is.** *Amends V0.59 (3).* (issue 51) (1) **The guard.** A host record's branch ends as merged only when it is ahead of its base and its head is an ancestor of it. A branch that is behind or level with its base has nothing of its own to merge, and makes no record: a checkout on `main` that is behind `origin/main` is not a merge, and neither is `main` at the head. `Git.End` reads `rev-list --count <base>..<branch>` and answers `merged: false` when it is 0, in front of the ancestor test. (2) **The base's head.** `GitFacts` carries `BaseHead`, where the base points (`origin/main`). A record that has nothing ahead keeps its place in flight and takes the next revision when its branch catches up, so the record of a branch that keeps working shows each landed commit instead of one revision and a landing at the first look. |
| V0.77 | → 5.2, the provider screen. **A provider card says each thing once, and its badge tells the truth.** (1) **One statement.** The card draws the error once: in the line when the account has no data, in the detail when it has, and never in both. The next try is one line under it. (2) **The badge.** Ready means nothing is wrong. A card whose quota failed says Rate limited or Login expired, one whose data is older than its last good read says Stale, one never read says Not read yet. The status of the account (V0.50) still decides whether it needs a key or a login, and wins when it is not ready. (3) **The reason, in words the owner acts on.** A rate limit says when the next try is; an expired login says to paste a new token or run the profile, never "run this profile once" to a token account (issue 65). |
| V0.78 | → V0.64, the Changes board. **Settled work leaves the board.** (issue 54) (1) The board leaves out the changes of a thread that is settled or snoozed (archived threads were already out). (2) **Except what needs the owner:** a change of such a thread that is in Needs you (a failed condition, or it waits for the owner) stays, because V0.59 (6) blocks the thread on it; the badge counts it. (3) A host record shown by an open thread stays, under that thread. (4) A **Show settled** tick beside Show ended brings them all back; it is off by default and remembered per device. The conversation's own change list (V0.59 (8)) is unaffected. |
| V0.79 | → 5.2, 13. **Start a skill or a slash command from the composer.** (issue 70) (1) **The list.** The CLI answers `initialize` with `commands` (name, description, argument hint): its built-in commands, the custom commands and the skills of the folder and its plugins. The host keeps the list of each session's answer for its thread, and the newest list per provider and folder (stored, so it survives a restart). Commands that need the interactive terminal are left out: `clear`, `login`, `logout`, `config`, `exit`, `quit`, `help`, `resume`, `theme`, `vim`, `terminal-setup`, `ide`, `doctor`, `status`, `statusline`, `permissions`, `model`, `mcp`, `agents`, `hooks`, `memory`, `export`, `bug`, `feedback`, `upgrade`, `release-notes`, `privacy-settings`, `install-github-app`, `migrate-installer`, `rewind`, `plugin`, `plugins`, `login-method`, `add-dir`, `output-style`, `fast`, `usage`. (2) **Served.** `GET /api/threads/{id}/commands` answers `{commands, from}`: the thread's own list (`from: "thread"`), else the newest list seen on the host for the thread's provider and folder (`"folder"`), else none (`""`). `GET /api/commands?cwd=&profile=` answers the newest list for a provider and folder, for New thread. Neither is in the thread DTO. (3) **Autocomplete.** Typing `/` at the start of the composer (desktop, phone and New thread) opens a list above it of the commands whose name starts with the typed text, then those that contain it, at most 8, each with its description. Arrow keys move, Tab or Enter picks, Escape closes; a tap picks. A typed space or a text that no command matches closes it. (4) **Picked.** A pick fills `/name ` and, while nothing follows the name, the composer shows the command and its argument hint in a muted line above the field (`/review <pr-number>`), since a field with text shows no placeholder. A send sends the text unchanged, so the CLI runs the command or the skill. |
| V0.80 | → 8, 12.6, 13. **Each thread in its own cgroup under one budget for agent work; the client shows the load.** (issue 74) (1) **Split.** The unit delegates `cpu memory` (`Delegate=`). Before it starts anything, fyrd moves every process of the unit's cgroup into `server/` (cpu.weight 1000, memory.low from the module), then enables `+cpu +memory` for the unit's children. (2) **Per thread.** Every agent CLI starts directly in `agents/<thread id>/` (clone3 `CLONE_INTO_CGROUP`, no sweep), so everything it starts is there too; the deploy's build and test run in `agents/self-deploy/`. A thread's cgroup is removed when its process ends and it is empty. (3) **Budget.** `agents/` carries cpu.weight 100, cpu.max (4 cores), memory.high, memory.max and memory.swap.max from module options, with T3's defaults. `agents-lock-cap` (the machine's QoS) may rewrite `agents/cpu.max` at `/sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/fyr.service/agents/cpu.max`. (4) **Off.** Without the module's cgroup settings, or without a writable delegated cgroup v2 with `cpu` and `memory` (dev runs, tests, other systems), fyrd runs every process as before and says why once in the log. (5) **Load.** `GET /api/load` answers each host's load average, cores, CPU pressure (`/proc/pressure/cpu` some avg10) and, with cgroups, the agents' total and each thread cgroup's CPU (cores, from the `cpu.stat` usage_usec between two reads) and `memory.current`; the front merges its hosts. The host reads them only when asked, at most every 2 s. The client asks every 5 s while the page is visible. (6) **Shown.** The desktop rail shows the load average above the quota, coloured when the 1-minute load passes the cores or CPU pressure passes 40 %; the tooltip has the three averages, the pressure and the agents' share. A working thread's row (desktop and phone) adds its CPU ("1.6 cores"), coloured from 2 cores. |
| V0.81 | → V0.13, V0.39. **A deploy that changes only the web client switches without a restart.** (issue 75) (1) **Same fyrd.** Before it builds, the deployer hashes the sources that go into `fyrd` (SHA-256 over the path and content of every file of the checkout except hidden entries, symlinks, `node_modules`, `bin`, `docs`, `research`, `design`, `spikes`, `testdata`, `*_test.go`, `*.md`, and everything under `web/` but its `*.go` files) and writes the sum to the build's `server.sum`. The running daemon's sum is the `server.sum` beside its own executable; a daemon without one (the seed, a build from before V0.81, `fyr install`) always takes the full path. (2) **Client-only path.** When the sums are equal it still builds and runs `make test` (red stops it as before), copies the built client (`web/dist`) into the build as `web/`, and then, with no snapshot, no drain and no restart: points `previous` at the build `current` named and `current` at the new build (so a later restart runs it, and the launcher's rollback has `previous`), makes the front serve the new client and report the new build's id (`fyrd version`) as `buildId`, and closes every browser stream, so each open page reconnects, sees the new `buildId` in `hello` and reloads (V0.13). Running threads are not touched. The deploy's result reaches the requesting thread at once ("Fyr now runs build <id>; only the web client changed, so nothing restarted"). (3) **Failure.** A switch that fails (the built client cannot be copied or has no `index.html`, a link cannot be written, the front refuses it) puts the links back, keeps serving the old client and reports `failed`; nothing restarts. (4) **Banner.** The phases are `build`, `test`, `client` ("Updating the web client · this page reloads by itself"), then `done` ("Fyr is updated · build <id>") or `failed`; no drain and no restart phase. (5) Any other difference runs the full path of V0.13 unchanged. |
| V0.82 | → V0.59 (4), V0.66, V0.13. **Change records do not go stale after a merge.** *Amends V0.59 (4) and V0.66.* (issue 83) (1) **Checks after the merge.** The poller also reads the PR of a `landed` or `live` code change while its snapshot is not final, for at most 24 hours after the merge (`mergedAt`, else when the change landed); then it stops. Not final: the PR's state is not yet merged or closed, or its checks are `pending` or `none` (none read yet). Each read replaces the PR snapshot (state, checks, merge commit); the phase does not move back, and a `live` change stays `live`. A merged PR whose deploy watch (V0.66) a repo without deployments ended does not arm it again on a later read. (2) **A self-deploy makes changes live.** Before it builds, the deployer records the checkout's `HEAD` and its `origin` remote, and stores them with the pending deploy, so the success report of the new daemon (V0.13) has them; the client-only path (V0.81) uses them when it succeeds. On success, every `landed` code change of any thread whose targets name that repo and whose PR has a merge commit that is an ancestor of the built commit (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <merge> <HEAD>` in the checkout) moves to `live` (observed), with the observed condition `deploy` ok and the build id as evidence `build`. A change whose merge commit is not in the build, or that the owner ended, stays as it is. The move goes through the Input path as an observation of the deploy observer (`deploy.self`). |
| V0.83 | → V0.13, V0.39. **A deploy that waits for threads can be forced; the cut turns resume.** (issue 76, decision 0022) (1) **Button.** While a deploy is in phase `drain` with at least one blocking thread and the host is live, the update banner has **Restart now**, on the desktop and on the phone (44 px target there). Every other phase, and a drain with no blocking thread, shows no button. (2) **Confirmation.** A press opens Fyr's confirmation (V0.68): "Restart Fyr now?", a text that names the threads whose turns end ("“<thread>” ends its turn" or "These N threads end their turns: …") and says each gets the message “continue” after the restart, a red primary **Restart now**, and Cancel, which sends nothing. (3) **Command.** Restart now sends `host.deployNow` (no `threadId`), an owner-only host kind (an agent cannot send it) whose reply waits for the deployer (Await, 5.1): `409` "No update is waiting for running threads" outside a drain. (4) **Switch.** The deployer's drain loop stops at once: it stores the threads running at that moment (the requester's own thread included when it runs) as `cut` in `pending_deploy`, links `next` and exits with code 75 as V0.13 (5) says; the running processes end by the normal stop (SIGINT to the process group). A drain that ends by itself cuts nothing. (5) **Continue.** When the new daemon is up (where it reports the pending deploy, also after a rollback), each cut thread gets the text "continue" as the owner's message through the one send path, so it shows in the conversation and starts a run that resumes the session (`--resume`). Its command id is `deploy-<build>-continue-<thread>`, so a second start sends nothing twice (I7). A thread settled or archived since gets nothing, and one that cannot take a message (an open question, a handover) keeps waiting for the owner. (6) The client-only path (V0.81) has no drain and no button. |
| V0.84 | → V0.59 (6), V0.64, V0.78, 5.2. **Not now: the owner parks a change that needs them until something on it changes.** *Amends V0.59 (6) and V0.64.* (issue 93) (1) **Command.** `change.park {change}` is an owner-only command (an agent's call is refused, like `change.end`). It applies to a change of the thread that has not ended and blocks (V0.59 (6): a failed condition, or `needs: owner`), and stores `parked {at, seen}` on the change: the time and a fingerprint of what the owner saw (needs, label, phase, the failed and pending conditions with their reasons, the newest revision id). `change.unpark {change}` is its Undo. (2) **Not blocking.** A parked change whose fingerprint still matches does not block its thread and is not in Needs you: not in the thread's attention (the inbox section), not in the board's Needs you column and not in the needs count; on the board it sits in its phase column. Another change of the thread that blocks still blocks. (3) **Expiry.** Any change to the fingerprinted fields (a new reason or label, a new or changed condition, a new phase, a new revision) makes the change block again by itself; Fyr clears `parked` at that reduce, so the agent and the client see it is back. The same report again is no new fact. (4) **Card.** A change card that blocks shows **Not now** next to End this change (44 px on the phone, inside the card); a parked card shows a quiet line "Not now · hh:mm" with **Undo**. (5) **The agent.** The agent is told when the owner parks or unparks a change, and `fyr_state` and `change_list` say the owner parked it, so it stops asking. A host record (an observed branch shown by several threads) is not the thread's to park. |
| V0.85 | → V0.80, V0.20, V0.54. **The thread the owner has open gets a higher CPU weight.** (issue 78, the part of issue 74 that V0.80 left: the work being watched goes first under contention.) (1) **Forward.** The front keeps presence (V0.20, V0.54). On every beat that names a thread it sends `POST /api/threads/{id}/focus {device}` to that thread's host, the same per-host forwarding as every thread route, in role both too. When a device's beat names another thread or none, the front sends `{device, off: true}` for the thread it showed before. The send is in the background with a 5 s timeout and is skipped for an offline host; the beat's answer does not wait for it. (2) **Weight.** The host keeps, per device, the thread it focuses and when. A thread focused by any device within 45 s (`notify.Watch`, the same window that keeps it watched) gets `agents/<id>/cpu.weight` 300; every other thread cgroup has 100. A thread whose cgroup is made while it is focused starts at 300. (3) **Expiry.** The weight goes back to 100 when the last device's beat for the thread is older than 45 s (the host checks every 5 s and on each focus), or at once on `off`. (4) **Off.** Without cgroups (a nil tree, V0.80 (4)) the route answers 204 and changes nothing. |
| V0.86 | → V0.80, V0.13, 16.2. **Slots for heavy jobs: at most N test suites, builds or e2e runs at once across a host's threads.** (issue 79) (1) **Pool.** Each host has one pool of N slots, `slots` in config.json, default 2 when absent or negative. `slots: 0` turns slots off: every `slot_acquire` answers at once with "no limit", nothing queues, the deploy's `make test` takes no slot, and the rail shows no slots line. Every kind (`test`, `build`, `e2e`) takes from the same pool. A thread holds at most one slot. The queue is first come, first served; a priority waiter goes ahead of every waiter without priority and behind earlier priority waiters; nobody takes a slot from its holder. (2) **Tools.** `slot_acquire {kind}` answers at once and never blocks a tool call: either the thread now holds the slot ("You hold a test slot. Run the job now, then call slot_release."), or it is queued with its position ("Queued for a test slot: 2 ahead. Do not start the job; Fyr tells you in a [Fyr] message when the slot is yours."). A second call answers the same way and does not queue twice. `slot_release` gives the slot back or leaves the queue. When a queued thread gets the slot, Fyr tells its agent with a notice that wakes it ("A test slot is yours now: run the job, then call slot_release."); the thread records event `slot` with the kind. (3) **Release.** A held slot is released when the thread's run ends, however it ends. A place in the queue survives the run's end, since the agent was told to end its turn while it waits. A held slot of a thread with no live run for 2 minutes after the grant is released (the grant's wake did not start a run), and a thread that no longer exists leaves the pool; the engine's 10 s tick checks. **Restart.** The holders and the queue (not the deploy's priority wait, which ends with its daemon) are stored in the store's KV table under `slots`, written on every change and loaded at start. After a restart a holder without a live run follows the 2-minute rule, and a queued thread keeps its place and gets the grant notice when its turn comes. (4) **Deploy.** The self-deploy's `make test` waits for a slot with priority, holds it while the tests run and releases it after; the wait ends when the deploy is replaced. Its owner in the pool is `self-deploy`. The build does not take a slot. (5) **Prompt.** The prompt Fyr gives every agent says to call `slot_acquire` before a full test suite, a build or an e2e run and `slot_release` after, to run the job only while holding the slot, and that the slot goes back when the run ends. (6) **Shown.** `GET /api/load` adds per host `slots {n, held: [{owner, kind, since}], waiting: [{owner, kind, priority}]}`, with cgroups or without, and leaves it out while slots are off. A thread's row (desktop and phone) shows "waiting for a test slot (2 ahead)" while queued and "test slot" while it holds one. The desktop rail adds "1/2 slots" under the load figure, coloured while anyone waits; the tooltip says how many hold and wait. |
| V0.87 | → V0.59 (3), (4), V0.70 (2), V0.76, V0.64, decision 0018 addendum. **One work item, one card: a change on a branch follows its record, and a PR joins the change of its work.** *Amends V0.59 (3) and (4).* (issue 69) (1) **Follow.** A change of the thread of any kind with a branch, not only `code`, follows the host record of its repo and branch (`observed` is the record's id), and the thread shows it instead of the record. It takes the record's workspace, `pushed` and `clean`, and the end of the branch as before, and shows the record's revisions until the agent writes one of its own; after that it adds the record's newest. (2) **A PR joins the change of its work.** A PR found by its head branch attaches to the thread's change on that branch of any kind that has no PR, the one that follows a record first. When the agent gives a change a PR (`change_open {pr}`, `change_attach` with `pr` evidence) that a change Fyr made for it (key `pr:<repo>#<n>`) already holds, or opens a change that is not `code` on the branch of such a change in flight, Fyr's change folds into the agent's: its key becomes an alias, and its PR snapshot, observed phase and conditions, evidence, revisions, actions and Rules move over; the thread's main change follows. The next PR poll folds two changes that already hold one PR the same way. (3) **The client** shows one card per change id in a thread's Changes tab, the newest, and one card on the board for a host record and the change that follows it. |
| V0.88 | → V0.59 (3), V0.76, V0.87. **A merged branch that stays checked out makes no new record.** *Amends V0.59 (3).* (1) **Same head, same work.** When a branch has no record in flight, the observer makes a new one only when no ended record of that repo and branch has the checkout's head as its newest revision. A merged branch that is still checked out, with or without changed files, is the work that ended; a new commit on it is new work and gets a new record. (2) **A merged PR ends only its own head.** A merged PR on the record's branch ends a record as merged only when the PR's head commit is the record's newest revision, or when either is unknown. New commits on a branch after its PR merged are not that PR's merge. |
| V0.89 | → 5.10 (`owner`, Loop guard), V0.72, 13.1 `e`. **The owner's settle never wakes the agent.** *Amends 5.10.* The cause: Settle on an idle thread with an open PR sets *settle when merged*, which leaves the thread awake, and the `owner` notice "The owner set this thread: settles when its PR merges." is `next`, which wakes an idle agent. The agent then starts a turn only to acknowledge it. (1) An `owner:settle` notice whose new state is a settle (settled, settles when its PR merges, settles when its condition holds) is `self`: it never wakes the agent and goes out with the next tool call or delivery, as the loop guard does. (2) Unsettle still wakes: when that notice merges into a queued settle notice, the merged notice is not `self` (Coalescing). |
| V0.90 | → V0.13 (4), V0.6, V0.83, 16.2. **The agent processes outlive fyrd: a deploy or a crash does not stop a turn.** *Amends V0.13 (4), V0.6 and V0.83.* (decision 0023) (1) **Runner.** The launcher runs a runner on a Unix socket (mode 0600, `<data>/runner.sock`, or a short path in the runtime folder when that one is too long) and passes it to fyrd as `FYR_RUNNER`. fyrd starts every agent process through it with the thread id as the key. The runner keeps at most one live process per key: a second spawn for a key fails with the id of the one that runs, and fyrd links to that one. (2) **No smarts.** The runner numbers each stdout line and stores it until fyrd acks it; it writes what fyrd sends, closes stdin, kills, lists and forgets on request, and never reads a line's content. An ack names the last line fyrd is done with and the lines up to it that the runner keeps; fyrd keeps every request of the CLI that it has not answered. (3) **Adoption.** At start fyrd lists the runner's processes (retried for 10 s) before the restart repair. A thread with a live process keeps its run, its unread messages and its questions (V0.6 does not apply to it; a handover in flight still fails). fyrd adopts one process per thread (a live one before an exited one, then the newest) and ends the others, before it resumes effects or serves. The adopted process gets the stored lines first, then the new ones; its MCP token, effort and flags come from the note fyrd gave at the spawn. (4) **Questions.** A Decision's id derives from the process and the CLI's request id, so a request that comes again finds its Decision and opens no second one. An answer given before the request comes again waits for it. A question the CLI withdrew while no fyrd listened is withdrawn when the turn ends. (5) **Abandoned.** A process with no fyrd linked for 1 hour gets stdin closed and is killed 10 s later; an exited process that no fyrd collected within 1 hour is dropped. A linked process is never ended by the runner. (6) **Deploy.** With a runner, a deploy does not drain: it switches with threads running, cuts no turn, shows no drain phase and no Restart now. Without one (no launcher, or a runner with another protocol version, which fyrd logs) the agents are fyrd's children and V0.13 (4) and V0.83 apply. (7) **Shutdown.** fyrd's shutdown lets go of each process and ends none; the launcher's shutdown ends them all (stdin closes, a kill after 5 s). |
| V0.91 | → V0.4, #56. **An inbox row puts the project first and the chips under the title.** *Amended by V0.135: the project is its icon before a two-line title; "from <thread>" is a quiet ↳.* On the desktop list, a row in every section has the same order. (1) **First line:** the project tag (icon and name, at most 45 % of the line) and then the title, cut with an ellipsis; the age at the end. Nothing else shares this line. (2) **Second line:** Needs you shows the state word, "from <thread>" and the row's details; the other sections show "from <thread>", what the agent does (working) or the snooze end (snoozed), then the CPU and the slot. A row with nothing for the second line has none. (3) **Chips:** the PR or change chip and the subagent count sit under the title, never beside it: on a third line in Needs you, at the end of the second line elsewhere (at its start when the line has no text). "0 sub" is not shown. |

| V0.93 | → V0.92. **The sidebar logo is a rendered isometric lighthouse.** *Amends V0.92.* The rail's top shows `/logo.png`, a 128 px PNG with a transparent background rendered with Blender Cycles through a true isometric orthographic camera: a white tower with two slate bands on a rock base, a dark gallery and dome, an amber lamp, and two amber beams that fade outward. It shows at 32 × 32 px, with the accessible name "Fyr", and is precached by the service worker. The scene script and the 512 px master are in `design/logo/`.
