| V0.95 | → 0020 (feed), V0.88, V0.89, decision 0024. **An observation never starts a loop: `next` does not wake, a fact is said once per head, observers wake a thread at most 5 times in 10 minutes, and a branch with nothing ahead makes no record.** *Amends 0020 (the wake rule), V0.59 (3), V0.88 (1) and V0.89 (2).* (decision 0024, V0.88 (1)) (1) **Wake.** Only a `wake` notice starts a turn of an idle agent; a `next` notice goes out with the next turn (the pending ones go before the words of the message that starts it, and are taken then, so a tool call does not repeat them) or at the next tool call. Wake: a failure appears on a change (CI failed, conflicting, changes requested, a deploy failed), the result of an ask, an effect or an action, the agent's watch that fired, a slot granted. Next: the owner's corrections and settings (unsettle too, which amends V0.89 (2)), merged, live, closed, gone, ended, CI passed, approved. Silent: Fyr made or joined a card. (2) **Said once per head.** A fact notice about a change is keyed (change, field, head); the head is the PR's head commit, else the change's newest revision, else the head the observer stored on the record. The thread's `Feed.Said` keeps the last state said per key, at most 200 keys, and a run does not clear it. The same state on the same head again is dropped; a new head is a new notice. (3) **Wake budget.** Notices from observers and Rules (facts, records, watches) wake a thread at most 5 times per 10 minutes. A further wake stays queued and is taken at the next tool call or turn; when the oldest of the 5 leaves the window, that time is in the thread's NextCheck, and the clock delivers the queued wake then. A message the owner sends resets the budget. The owner's and the agent's own Inputs, results and slots do not count. (4) **Records.** The git observer makes a record only for a branch with commits ahead of its base that is not the repo's default branch (`origin/HEAD`, else the base's branch); changed files or unpushed commits alone make none (amends V0.59 (3)). The record's workspace stores the head the observer read, so V0.88 (1) finds an ended record by that head even without a revision, and V0.88 (2) compares a merged PR's head with it. A branch whose last record ended at an unknown head makes no new record until it has a head. (5) **Migration.** At load, Fyr deletes the records with a key `git:<repo>#<branch>~N` that are not in flight and that no thread change follows, with the threads' references to them, and removes the change of `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/88` (a test fixture that leaked into live threads) from every thread. Both are idempotent. |
| V0.96 | → V0.12 (tools), 13.5, decision 0024. **`change_list` lists open changes, one line each, in at most 4,000 characters; handover and compaction carry those lines.** *Amends V0.12 (tools) and V0.59 (5).* (decision 0024 section 7 and slice 2: on 15 and 16 Nov `change_list` returned 83,000 to 100,000 characters three times and `fyr_state` 115,000, each failed call cost about 25,000 tokens, and after a handover or a compaction agents lost their change ids.) (1) **Open.** A change is open when it has not ended (abandoned or reverted) and is not at landed or live. A landed or live change with a failed condition counts as open. (2) **change_list `{scope?, id?, done?}`.** By default it lists the open changes, one line each: the full id, the PR as `owner/repo#N` (else the key), the title in quotes, the phase, and the failed or pending conditions in a few words (`checks failed`, `review pending`), with "needs the owner" and "parked" when they hold. A first line says how many changes are not shown because they are done. `done: true` lists every change. `scope: project` uses the same lines, each with the other thread's id. The whole reply is at most 4,000 characters; when lines are cut it ends with "+N more; pass id for one change". `id` returns that one change of this thread in full, as JSON. (3) **fyr_state.** Its changes part (`about` empty or `changes`) shows only the open changes, in its own form, within the same 4,000 characters and with the same tail; a line says how many done changes are left out. `about: <id>` still shows one change in full. (4) **Handover.** The first message to the new agent (rungs 2 and 3) ends with one line per open change of the thread: full id, title and phase. (5) **Compaction.** A process with Fyr's tools registers a `SessionStart` hook with matcher `compact`; it returns the same lines as `additionalContext`, or nothing when the thread has no open change. |
| V0.97 | → V0.43, 5.10, decision 0024. **A merge is bound to the head the owner saw, and the board merges every green PR at once.** *Amends V0.43.* (decision 0024) (1) **Head.** Every merge carries the head commit: the PR tab's Merge… sends the head it shows (from the PR detail, else the PR chip), a change's Merge button sends its PR's head, and an agent's merge ask (`change_request {ask: merge}` or `effect_request pr.merge`) gets the head Fyr reads when the ask is made, unless the agent names one; the head is stored in the decision's args and shown on its card ("Merge a1b2c3d"). Fyr refuses a head it no longer reads with "The head moved. Look again." when the button is pressed or the ask approved, then reads the PR again and refuses the same way before gh runs; otherwise it runs `gh pr merge --<method> --match-head-commit <sha>`, so GitHub refuses a head that moves in between. A merge without a head is refused. Each Merge button shows the short SHA ("Merge a1b2c3d", "Merge a1b2c3d…" on the PR tab). (2) **Poller.** The PR read adds `isDraft`, `mergeStateStatus` and `autoMergeRequest`; the PR snapshot carries `draft`, `mergeState` and `autoMerge`. (3) **Ready.** `core.Ready(pr, threadRunning)` is "Merge" when the PR is open, not a draft, its checks pass (none is not pass), it has no conflict, `mergeStateStatus` is CLEAN or HAS_HOOKS, no auto-merge is set and the thread has no run and no task running; "Review and merge" when all of that holds except that the state is BLOCKED and the review decision is REVIEW_REQUIRED; empty otherwise. Each change in flight carries it in the thread DTO as `ready` (derived, not stored). (4) **Merge all green (n).** The Changes board shows the button when n changes it lists have `ready` "Merge", a PR head and a Merge action. After one confirmation the client presses each one's Merge, one PR once (the first card of a PR in board order), one after another, each bound to the head the board showed. A refusal shows under its card ("Not merged:...") and the rest go on. |
| V0.98 | → V0.59, V0.64, V0.70, V0.87, decision 0024 (slice 4). **A finished change leaves the board.** *Amends V0.59 (3), (4) and (8), V0.64, V0.70 (2) and V0.87 (2).* (1) **Done.** A change, of a thread or a host record, carries `done {at, by, reason}` once it is finished; `ended` (abandoned, reverted) keeps its meaning for rules and settle. Fyr marks it at the reduce that finishes it: code at `landed` (reason `merged` for a merged PR); a PR merged into a branch that is not the repo's default branch (stacked) when its merge commit is an ancestor of the default branch, which the poller reads on each poll through GitHub's compare (when that read fails: when the change of its base branch is done); a change whose deploy watch (V0.66) is armed when it is `live` or the watch ends; not code at `landed` or `live` as reported; any change at `abandoned` or `reverted`. A change that goes back in flight (a reopened PR) is no longer done, unless the owner marked it. (2) **Out of sight.** The board and the thread's open list leave done changes out; a done change that still blocks (V0.70) stays in Needs you. Nothing polls the PR of a done change once its checks settle (V0.82), and the thread keeps a done change 30 days. (3) **Finished since your last visit.** The board shows a folded group "Finished since your last visit · N": the changes done after the board was last opened, newest first, each with its reason ("merged 10:42", "abandoned by you", "marked done by you"). Opening the board is a visit; the host stores the time as the owner's `boardSeenAt` and returns the one before. The thread's Changes tab lists its done changes under "Done here", folded. (4) **Untouched for 3 days.** An open change that does not need the owner, with no push, PR event, report or owner action for 3 days (`touchedAt`, moved when any fact of the change moves; a poll that reads the same PR again is no touch), sits in the board's group "Untouched for 3 days" with **Done**. Done is the owner's `change.done {change}` with a receipt, on a thread change and on a host record (the record's reduce takes this one owner Input); the card says "marked done by you". (5) **One card for a `gh pr create` PR.** A PR found in the agent's output has no branch, so Fyr makes `pr:<repo>#<n>`; the first poll reads its head branch, and that change folds (V0.87 (2)) into the thread's open change on that branch in the same repo, of any kind, that has no PR. A PR found by its branch also matches the repo. (6) **The header** shows the PR of the thread's newest open change that has one, never a done one, and nothing when none is open. (7) **Migration**, once on load: in each thread a `pr:` change and an open change on the same repo and branch fold into one, then every change at or past `landed` is marked done (reason "landed before v2") unless its deploy watch is armed, and every ended change and every landed host record is marked done. |
| V0.99 | → V0.80 (6), V0.86 (6). *Amended by V0.166: the phone shows the load too, a strip in the inbox header and a sheet.* *Amended by V0.167: each host is two labelled meters ("ld", "job"), the same on both fronts; the rail's middle has no scrollbar and the rail is compact under 940 px.* **The rail shows the load as a ring and the slots as pips.** *Amended by V0.152: the rail's middle is one card per host (the load as a share of the cores, a short host name, a slot meter with a word only when it matters) and a quota grid with provider initials.* *Amended by V0.141: the rings sit side by side in the rail's middle, its own scroll area.* *Amends V0.80 (6) and V0.86 (6).* (1) **Ring.** Each host's 1-minute load is a 30 px ring with the figure inside. The arc is the load over the cores, full past the cores. Its colour is calm under 70 % of the cores, amber from 70 % up to the cores, and the hot colour (with the figure) when V0.80's rule holds (load past the cores or CPU pressure at 40 %). (2) **Pips.** Under the ring, one pip per slot: filled while a thread holds it, empty while free. While anyone waits, the filled pips take the hot colour and "+n" follows with the number waiting. Above 6 slots the rail writes "3/8" in place of the pips. (3) **Unchanged.** The tooltip keeps the exact values (the three averages, the pressure, the agents' share, the slots in use and waiting); the screen reader gets one label with the load, the cores and the slots. The phone shows no machine load (only each thread's CPU and slot in its row), so it does not change. |
| V0.100 | → V0.91, V0.17, V0.23. **The phone rows are the desktop rows, and the phone shows the project filter as chips; the thread header names the provider.** *Amended by V0.135* (1) **One row.** Both lists render the same row (`web/src/row-ui.tsx`) in the V0.91 shape: project tag and title first, the age at the end, the details on the second line, the chips under the title. The phone sets only larger sizes for touch. It differs in three ways, each for a reason: it names no keys (no keyboard), "from <thread>" is not a link (a tap opens the row), and it names the host before the details when the front has more than one host (V0.54). (2) **Project filter.** The phone shows the desktop chip row of projects under the section strip, with 32 px chips; a tap filters, the × clears, and a quiet project picked shows its note in its chip. It replaces the "Only <project>" line. The menu item "Filter by project…" stays. (3) **Provider.** The desktop thread header shows a chip with the provider's colour dot and name, before the model; its tooltip gives the reason when the provider cannot run. The phone already shows it in the thread details. |
| V0.101 | → V0.59, V0.62, V0.84, V0.91, 6.2, decision 0024 (slice 5; V0.99 and V0.100 were taken). **Needs you is computed on the host; the agent reports only what Fyr cannot read.** *Amends V0.59 (5) and (6), V0.62, V0.84 (1) and (3), V0.91 and 6.2.* (decision 0024) (1) **Computed Needs you.** Each change a thread shows that is not done or ended carries `need {cause, head, text, ask, action, muted}` in the thread DTO, never stored; the web client only reads it and derives nothing from conditions. The causes: an open ask of the thread (a `change_request` on the change, or an `effect_request` on its PR) that is not answered, declined or void; **Ready** by V0.97's rule (`Merge`, `Review and merge`), with the PR's head; **Stopped on a failure**: a failed condition and no run or task in progress `StopAfter` (5 min) after the last run ended. The thread stores `runEndedAt` when a run ends and `stopped`, the 5-minute rule at the last reduce; `NextCheck` adds `runEndedAt + 5 min` while a failure waits, so the clock's reduce turns it on, with no new effect. A failure while a run goes is `fixing` ("Agent is fixing CI"), muted, never in Needs you. A done or ended change, and every change of an archived thread, is never a cause. Attention: a cause puts the thread in Needs you as blocked; a finished run whose result the owner has not seen is `done` in Seen (unread), not in Needs you; a run failure stays in Needs you. Asks are void (V0.100's `voidAsks`, extended) when their change is done (a deploy ask stays), their PR is no longer open, or the thread is archived; an agent's open ask no longer blocks Archive. (2) **Not now by head.** A park holds while the change's head (the PR's head, else its newest revision) and the sorted set of its failing condition types are as the owner saw them; a new reason, label or phase does not bring it back. Not now applies to Ready and a failure; on an ask it is the decline (`thread.answer {allow: false}`). The card says "back when the head changes". (3) **Owner corrections**, each an owner Input with a receipt: **Refresh now** (`change.refresh {change}`, on a thread change or a host record the thread shows) reads GitHub (the poller's kick) and git (the observer) now, and the card says "checked N s ago" from the PR's `checkedAt`; **Abandon** (`change.end`) on a thread change and, through the new `record.end` owner Input of `ReduceRecord`, on a host record (ended by the owner and done); **Done** (V0.98). An owner end or Done refuses a later agent write to the phase. (4) **Four agent tools** with the descriptions of decision 0024 section 7: `change_open` (key optional with pr), `change_update {change, phase?, label?, conditions?, revision?, evidence?}`, `change_request`, `change_list`. `change_status`, `change_revise`, `change_attach` and `pr_link` are aliases for one release, callable and not listed (`mcp.Tool.Hidden`); `change_unchanged` is a no-op. `needs` is gone from the tools and the model. A change reference is an id, a key, a PR URL, `owner/repo#N`, or `#N` when one change of the thread has that PR. An agent value for a field Fyr observes is ignored, not refused, and the reply names what Fyr reads ("Fyr reads #13219 from GitHub: merged 10:42."); `abandoned` on a PR GitHub shows open is taken, the card leaves, and a later poll of the open PR does not bring it back ("Still open on GitHub. Close it with gh pr close if it is dead."). The system prompt carries section 7's paragraph. (5) **No change upkeep.** The changes parts of the Stop reason and the PostToolUse reminder (V0.62) go; the hooks stay registered for the live view's upkeep and the notices, and the runner meta keeps `changeTools`. (6) **Inbox row** (V0.91's shape): a thread in Needs you whose change has a cause shows the beam dot and its button after the change chip: "Merge a1b2c3d", bound to the head (V0.97), for Ready; "Answer" for an ask, which opens the thread. The thread's Changes tab shows the cause inline on the card: the ask with its answer ("Merge a1b2c3d" for a merge ask, else Yes) and Not now; Ready with Merge bound to the head and Not now; a failure with Not now; fixing muted without the beam. |
| V0.102 | → V0.97, V0.43, 5.10. **In a repo the owner opted in, Fyr approves an agent's merge ask itself once the checks pass on the head the ask named.** (1) **Setting.** Per GitHub repo (`owner/name`, compared in lower case), stored on the host (kv `autoMerge.repos`). Only the owner changes it: the host command `host.autoMerge {repo, on}` (owner only, with a receipt). No agent tool sends it. Settings → Auto-merge lists the repos of the threads' PRs and the repos turned on, each with an On/Off button, and a field to turn on a repo by name; `GET /api/autoMerge` serves the list. (2) **Approval.** After every Input of a thread, Fyr looks at its open merge asks (`change_request {ask: merge}` or `effect_request pr.merge`). An ask whose PR is in a repo turned on is approved when the PR's head is the head the ask named and `Ready` (V0.97 (3)) is "Merge": open, not a draft, checks pass, no conflict, merge state CLEAN or HAS_HOOKS, no GitHub auto-merge, and no run or task running in the thread. The approval is an observer's Input (`merge.autoApproved`); neither the agent nor a client can send it. It closes the ask and runs the same merge as the owner's yes, bound to the ask's head: V0.97 (1) holds, and a push after the ask leaves the ask open for the owner, who gets "The head moved. Look again." on a press. Turning a repo on approves the asks that already wait in it. A repo that is off waits for the owner as before. (3) **Shown.** The thread shows the ask closed as "Auto-approved: <repo> merges without a press (head a1b2c3d)." and the result starts with "Auto-approved by the auto-merge setting of <repo>."; the agent hears that result. The change keeps `autoApproved {repo, head, at}`, and its card on the board and in the thread says "Merge auto-approved by the repo setting (<repo>, a1b2c3d)". |
| V0.103 | → V0.93. **The phone's inbox header shows the logo.** *Amends V0.93* ("the phone header is unchanged"). The phone's inbox header shows the same `/logo.png` as the rail, at 28 × 28 px, before the name "Fyr". The rail has no room for the name; the phone header has, so the name stays, and the image has an empty accessible name there so a screen reader says "Fyr" once. The rail and the phone share one component. The thread, search and selection headers on the phone do not show the logo: the desktop shows it only in the rail, never in a thread header. The touch targets in the header do not change. |
| V0.104 | → V0.100, 13.1, 7.5. **The owner pins a thread; a pinned thread sorts above every other thread, and snooze, settle and archive remove the pin.** (1) **Actions.** `thread.pin` and `thread.unpin` are owner actions on the one path, with a receipt; an agent cannot send them. The thread stores `pinnedAt`, and the DTO carries it. Pin is unavailable on a thread that is pinned, settled, snoozed or archived; Unpin on a thread that is not pinned. (2) **Order.** Both lists draw a Pinned block above every section, always open, when a thread is pinned. Its rows are in the normal order: the section order, then the section's own order. A pinned row keeps the row shape of its section (V0.91), shows a pin mark before the project, and shows once (not again in its section). The rail, the phone strip and the badges still count it in its section, because its attention did not change. j/k start at the Pinned block. (3) **Removal.** A snooze removes the pin. A settle removes it when the thread settles: at once, or when a deferred settle (when done, when merged, the agent's settle_when) fires; until then the pin stays. Archive removes it too. Unsnooze, unsettle, unarchive and a new run do not restore it. (4) **Controls.** Desktop: Pin or Unpin in the row's right-click menu, and a pin toggle left of Snooze in the thread header. Phone: Pin or Unpin in the thread's ⋯ sheet (the phone has no right-click and no room in its header, so the sheet holds it). (5) **Agents.** The `threads` tool writes "pinned" after the id of a pinned thread. |
| V0.105 | → V0.98 (1), V0.95 (4), V0.66, V0.82, decision 0024 (3). **A merged change is done in every repo, and a record on the default branch is done.** *Amends V0.98 (1) and decision 0024 (3), the row for Fyr's own repo.* (Live data after the V0.101 deploy, 16 Nov: `hosting:p15-buckets`, merged as AcmeHosting#1547, stayed on the board because AcmeHosting has GitHub deployments and its V0.66 deploy watch held the change open, against the owner's decision that code is done at merged; a record `git:github.com/meros/fyr#main~2` from before V0.95 stayed in flight and the observer kept it.) (1) **Merged is done.** A change whose phase reached `landed` is done; an armed deploy watch no longer holds it. The deploy watch (V0.66) and a self-deploy (V0.82) still move it to `live`, shown as a fact on the done card. (2) **The default branch.** When the git observer reads a repo's default branch and an open record of that branch exists, the record is done with the reason "default branch"; the load pass does the same for an open record whose branch is the default one (by its workspace base, else main or master). A feature branch is untouched. Tests: `TestIsDoneByKindAndPhase`, `TestRecordOnDefaultBranchIsDone`. |
| V0.106 | → V0.104 (3), 7.4. **A pinned thread never settles by itself; only the owner's Settle settles it.** *Amends V0.104 (3).* (1) **Waits.** A settle Rule that the owner did not arm (the agent's `settle_when` done, merged or change; the agent's `watch` with then "settle"; any settle Rule Fyr arms) is suspended with the reason `pinned` while the thread is pinned. It stays recorded and does not fire. (2) **Unpin drops them.** Unpin disarms those Rules; they do not fire after it, also when their condition holds by then. (3) **The owner's Settle.** The owner's Settle (now, or when done or when merged, which the owner armed) still settles a pinned thread and removes the pin, as in V0.104. Settle stays available on a pinned thread that has only the agent's "settle when done"; the press replaces it with the owner's. (4) **The agent is told.** On a pinned thread, the reply to `settle_when` and to `watch` with then "settle" adds: "The owner pinned this thread: it does not settle while pinned, and unpin drops this condition." (5) **Unchanged.** Snooze and archive still remove the pin. |
| V0.107 | → V0.37 (1), V0.52. **Every agent process lists the view tools, so a view the owner turns on during a run is filled in that run.** *Amends V0.37 (1) and (4).* (Bug from the Acme coordinator thread, 16 Nov: the owner turned the view on while a long run went; `fyr_state` said "view: on, no page yet", the running process had no `view_*` tools, and the notice said "it takes effect with your next process", so the view stayed empty with nothing shown to the owner.) (1) **Tools.** Fyr's MCP server lists `view_publish`, `view_state` and `view_unchanged` for every thread. The CLI reads the tool list once, when the process starts; Fyr's MCP transport answers JSON over POST only and has no stream for `notifications/tools/list_changed`. While the view is off, a call fails with "the live view is off in this thread; only the owner turns it on, and Fyr tells you when they do". (2) **Notice.** When the owner turns the view on, the agent's notice asks it to publish now with `view_publish {html or path, state}`, says what the view is and that `state.headline` is its status line, and asks for `view_state` as items move. (3) **Upkeep.** Every process with Fyr's MCP tools counts as having the view tools, so the Stop hook (V0.37 (4)) and the reminder (V0.52) apply in the run where the owner turned the view on. The view prompt still comes with the next process, which starts after the run as before. Tests: `TestViewToolsOnlyWithFlag`, `TestViewOnNoticeAsksForPublish`. |
| V0.108 | → V0.69, 5.10, V0.104. **Regenerate title: Fyr writes a new thread title from the conversation so far, on the owner's press or the agent's request.** (V0.107, #123) Today a thread's first title is the first line of its first message (`TitleFrom`, cut to 80 characters) or the `title` that `thread_new` gave; after that only `thread.rename` (owner) and `thread_rename` (agent, V0.69) change it, and a name the owner gave is kept against the agent. A thread that drifted from its first message keeps a name that says what it was. (1) **Owner.** `thread.regenerateTitle` is an owner action with a receipt; an agent cannot send it. It returns the effect `title`; the engine makes the title in the background and lands it as the observer Input `title.made`. The new title replaces any title, also one the owner typed, because the owner asked for it, and it is stored as the owner's (`titleBy: owner`), so the agent's `thread_rename` keeps it after. The thread log gets `thread.renamed {by: owner, regenerated: true}`. Desktop: "Regenerate title" in the thread header's ⋯ menu and in the row's right-click menu. Phone: "Regenerate title" in the thread's ⋯ sheet (the phone has no right-click and no ⋯ menu in its header, so the sheet holds it). Both send the same command through one function (`regenerateTitle` in `web/src/commands.ts`) and show the toast "Fyr is writing a new title"; the title changes when the host answers. (2) **Agent.** `thread_rename {regenerate: true}` (no `title`) on this thread or a thread it started makes the title in the tool call and applies it as the agent's rename. A name the owner gave is kept: the tool refuses with the V0.69 reason before any model call. (3) **How the title is made.** One `claude -p` run of the thread's own provider (its auth, key and endpoint), in the system temp folder so no project instructions load, with `--tools ""`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--setting-sources ""`, `--no-session-persistence` and a short system prompt that replaces Claude Code's (`--bare` is not used: it never reads an OAuth login). The model is the provider's Haiku model (`claude-haiku-5-5` on a Claude login), else the model its endpoint sets in `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`, else the thread's model (*amended by V0.115*: the System agents setting picks the provider and the model, and there is no fallback to the thread's model). The prompt is the conversation: the owner's words and the agent's text, no tool calls; a long one keeps its first 2 000 characters (why the thread started) and its newest 10 000 (what it is about now). Fyr keeps the first line of the answer, drops quotes, Markdown emphasis, a "Title:" prefix and a final full stop, and cuts it to 80 characters. At most one call per thread runs at a time; a call stops after 90 s. (4) **Cost.** Measured on a short conversation: 945 input and 15 output tokens, $0.0002 at list price, about 2 s. A full 12 000-character prompt is about 3 000 to 4 000 tokens, so about $0.001 a call (estimated from the measured rate). A Claude login spends it from the subscription's allowance. Fyr never calls on its own: only a press or an agent's request starts a call. (5) **Failure.** When the call fails (no login or key, no answer in 90 s, an empty answer, a thread with no delivered message yet), the title stays. The owner's thread shows the failed line "Fyr could not regenerate the title: <reason>. The title stays."; the agent's tool returns the same line as its error. Tests: `TestRegenerateTitleIsTheOwners`, `TestOwnerRegeneratedTitleReplacesTheOwnersName`, `TestFailedTitleKeepsTheTitle`, `TestCleanTitle`, `TestOwnerRegeneratesTheTitle`, `TestFailedRegenerateKeepsTheTitle`, `TestAgentRegeneratesTheTitle`, `TestHaikuModel` (was `TestTitleModel`; V0.115), `TestPrintArgs`, `TestPrint`, the layout test's V0.108 block. |
| V0.109 | → 6.1, V0.48, V0.104. **The marked row in the desktop thread list is the open thread, whatever path opened it, and the row is in view.** (1) **One source.** The list keeps no cursor of its own. The marked row is the open thread; with no thread open, the last thread opened stays marked, and j/k start there. (2) **Paths.** A row click, a link from another thread ("From thread", "Started by"), a notification, `?open=`, a new thread and a reload open the thread, so the row is marked. j/k and g1–g5 open the row they move to when a thread is open (a peek stays a peek); with nothing open they only mark it. Settle, snooze and archive from the list open the next row (else the one before); from the thread pane the thread stays open. (3) **Drawn and in view.** When the open thread's row is in a closed section, that section opens; past the section's row limit, the section shows all its rows. This holds when the row moves to another section while it is open (Working → Settled). Settled opened this way closes 30 s after the owner leaves it (V0.48). The row scrolls into view when it changes, moves to another section or the Pinned block, or is drawn again. (4) **Hidden rows.** When search, the project filter or a quiet project hides the open thread's row, no row is marked and the filter stays as the owner set it. (5) **Phone.** The phone shows the inbox or one thread, never both, so it marks no row. Tests: `test/selection.test.ts`; layout e2e "V0.109 desktop" (a "From thread" link, `?open=` of a Settled thread, k in the list). |
| V0.110 | → V0.15, V0.92, V0.93. **The favicon, the PWA icons, the Apple touch icon and the push badge are the rendered lighthouse.** (V0.93) (1) `design/logo/icons.sh` cuts every icon from `design/logo/lighthouse-512.png` once, and they are committed in `web/assets/icons/`; the build copies them and draws nothing. (2) The "any" icons (192, 512) and the favicon (32) are the render on transparent. The maskable icon (512) and the Apple touch icon (180) are the render at 80 % (the maskable safe zone) on a full-bleed `--night` tile (`#14212d`), because the white tower and the amber beams need a dark ground. The push badge (96) is a white silhouette from the render's alpha. (3) The icons live at `/icons/lighthouse/` (`web/src/icon-paths.ts`): the service worker serves `/icons/` cache-first as immutable, so new icons take a new folder. (4) The hand-drawn mark and its PNG encoder (`web/scripts/icons.ts`) are removed. An installed PWA can keep its old home-screen icon until the owner reinstalls it. |
| V0.111 | → V0.102, V0.13, V0.90, 5.10. **Auto-deploy: Fyr approves an agent's deploy ask itself when the checkout is clean and its commit is on origin's default branch; the setting is on by default.** Since V0.90 a deploy cuts no turn, and a failed build or test switches nothing. (1) **Setting.** One host setting, not per checkout, stored in kv `autoDeploy` (`on`, `off`; empty is on, the owner's default). Only the owner changes it: the host command `host.autoDeploy {on}` (owner only, with a receipt); no agent tool sends it. Settings → Auto-deploy has an On/Off button on desktop and phone (the same component); `GET /api/autoDeploy` serves `{on}`. (2) **Gate.** After every Input of a thread, Fyr looks at its open `fyr.deploy` asks (`change_request {ask: deploy}` or `effect_request fyr.deploy`). For each ask it checks the checkout once: `git status --porcelain` is empty (no uncommitted or untracked file, which the build would include), and HEAD is the commit `git ls-remote origin HEAD` reports or an ancestor of it. Code that passes is code a person already merged, so the press adds nothing. An ask that fails the gate stays open for the owner, and the thread shows "Not auto-approved: <why>. The deploy waits for your press." (3) **Approval.** The approval is an observer's Input (`deploy.autoApproved`); neither the agent nor a client can send it. It closes the ask as "Auto-approved: Fyr deploys a1b2c3d without a press." and runs the same deploy as the owner's yes, bound to the commit the gate read (`args.commit`). The build, the tests, the older-than-live check (V0.24), the rollback and the reports are as before. (4) **One at a time.** While a deploy builds, tests, drains, or waits out its probation (`pending_deploy`), Fyr approves no other deploy ask: the thread shows "Another Fyr deploy runs; Fyr approves this one when it ends." once, and the ask is approved when the running deploy ends (a failure, or the new build's probation). A deploy the owner presses still replaces one that has not switched, as before. (5) **The commit.** A deploy builds the checkout's HEAD when it starts. An auto-approved deploy refuses to build when HEAD moved after the gate: "The checkout moved from a1b2c3d to e4f5a6b after the approval; nothing was built. Ask again." An ask the owner presses builds the HEAD at the press; the tool description tells the agent to pull before it asks and to leave the checkout until the result. (6) **Shown.** The result starts with "Auto-approved by the auto-deploy setting (a1b2c3d)."; the agent hears that result. The change keeps `autoApproved {deploy: true, head, at}`, and its card says "Deploy auto-approved by the setting (a1b2c3d)". Limit: the gate trusts the checkout's `origin` remote; an agent that points `origin` at another repo passes it. Tests: `TestAutoDeployApprovesTheAskAndBindsTheCommit`, `TestOnlyAnObserverAutoDeploys`, `TestAutoDeployHeldAsk`, `TestAutoDeploySettingIsTheOwners`, `TestAutoDeployByTheSetting`, `TestAutoDeployOneAtATime`, `TestDeployRefusesAMovedCommit`, `TestDeployBusy`, `TestGate`, `web/test/automerge.test.ts`, the layout test (the section on desktop, the 44 px toggle on the phone). |
| V0.112 | → V0.43, V0.97, V0.102, 5.10. **A merge waits for CI: no check reported on a repo with CI is pending, never green.** *Amends V0.43 (2) and (4), V0.97 (3).* (Bug from the coordinator thread, 16 Nov: Fyr merged meros/fyr#126 at 16:05:33 on the owner's yes to the agent's merge ask, 8 s after the ask; the PR's check list was empty, and its pull_request run started 18 s after the merge. Auto-merge was off for meros/fyr. The guard of `pr.merge` read no checks, and the host's last read refused failed checks only on a BLOCKED merge state, which an unprotected repo never has.) (1) **One summary.** `github.Summarize` is the one place a PR's check list becomes its checks, for the poller and for the PR detail: `fail` when a check failed, `pending` while one runs (queued or in progress, or a commit status PENDING or EXPECTED), `pass` when at least one check reported and none failed or runs (skipped counts as reported). An empty list on an open PR is `pending` on a repo with CI, because GitHub lists a run only after it is queued; it is `none` only on a repo with no CI. The PR detail carries the summary as `checked`. (2) **A repo with CI.** A repo has CI when a check reported on the head of any of its 20 newest PRs (one GraphQL read), or on any PR of it that Fyr read since the host started. A yes is kept for the life of the host process; a no is read again after 10 minutes. A read that fails counts as CI, so a merge waits and never runs on a guess. A new repo whose first PR adds its first workflow therefore reads as no CI until a check reports on a PR; GitHub branch rules still apply. (3) **The gate.** A `FyrEffect` has a `Gate` beside its `Guard`: what must also hold when the owner's yes, the owner's press or Fyr's auto-approval runs it, but not when the agent asks for it. The merge's Gate refuses while Fyr reads the PR's checks as `pending`: "CI has not passed on a1b2c3d yet: Merge waits for the checks". The agent may still ask for a merge while CI runs; the owner's yes on that ask is refused with that reason and the ask stays open. Before gh runs, the host reads the PR again and refuses the same way ("No check has reported on a1b2c3d yet: Merge waits for CI", or "CI has not passed on a1b2c3d yet" while a check runs), whatever GitHub's merge state is. A repo with no CI (`none`) does not wait. Failed checks keep V0.43's rule: a failing check that GitHub does not require (UNSTABLE) does not block, and BLOCKED with a failing check refuses. Auto-merge (V0.102) needs `pass` through Ready, as before. (4) **Shown.** A merge ask whose yes waits keeps its card and its place in Needs you (an open ask always is, 7.4); its `need` carries `wait`, the line adds it after the reason, and "Merge a1b2c3d" is disabled with the reason as its title. The PR tab's status line is "Checks running: N" or "No check has reported yet", and Merge… is disabled, on any merge state; the PR chip's "Checks running" no longer allows Merge. Desktop and phone share the card (`NeedLine`) and the tab. Tests: `TestOwnerMergeWaitsForPendingChecks`, `TestOwnerMergeOnNoCIOrFailedChecks`, `TestMergeCheckedWaitsForCI`, `TestFetchEmptyChecksByRepoCI`, `TestMergeBlock`, `test/merge-wait.test.ts`. |
| V0.113 | → V0.103, V0.93. **The phone's inbox header shows the logo alone, without the word "Fyr".** *Amends V0.103* ("before the name \"Fyr\"", "the name stays"). The phone's inbox header shows the 28 × 28 px `/logo.png` and no visible name, as the desktop rail has since V0.93; the desktop does not change. The image's alt is "Fyr", so a screen reader still says "Fyr" once. The connection dot and host name follow the logo; the touch targets in the header do not change. Test: the layout test (the logo is there with alt "Fyr", and the header's brand has no text "Fyr"). |
| V0.114 | → V0.46, V0.74, V0.100, 3.15. **Every way into a quiet project goes through the same 30 s hold; one unlock opens them all.** *Amends V0.46 (4) ("Picking the project in the project filter shows them", "Search (desktop and phone) still finds them") and V0.74 (3) ("only New thread changes").* (1) **One unlock.** V0.74's hold (30 s, a finger or the mouse button, no key and no tap) and its 30 minutes on that device (browser storage) now open every path below at once, and "Hide" in New thread shuts them all; another tab of the same browser follows. The unquiet inbox, its counts, the title's Needs you count and the notifications keep V0.46 (2)–(4) during the unlock: the unlock opens paths, it does not end the window. (2) **The project filter** (the desktop chip, the phone chips): picking a quiet project shows none of its threads; under the chips a box says "<project> is quiet until 06:00." and "Its threads are hidden in its quiet hours.", with the hold row ("Hold here for 30 s to open quiet projects for 30 minutes."). After the hold its threads show, as V0.46 (4) had them. (3) **Search** (desktop ⌘K, phone): a quiet project's threads are not found; when a search would have found any, the same box says "Threads of quiet projects are left out of search." with the hold. (4) **A quiet thread opened any other way** (a "From thread" or "Started by" link, a [Fyr] notice, a board card or Needs you, `?open=`, back and forward, a push tap) shows, in place of the thread (desktop: the thread pane; phone: a screen titled "Quiet hours" with Back), "<project> is quiet until 06:00." and "This thread is in its quiet hours." with the hold; the hold opens it where it stands. Behind the gate the thread is not loaded, not marked seen and not reported as watched (presence). (5) **The open thread stays open.** A thread opened while its project was not quiet, or during the unlock, keeps working when the window starts or the unlock ends (V0.46 (6)); leaving it and coming back (another thread, the inbox, back and forward) goes through the gate again. (6) **Not gated, on purpose:** j/k and g1–g5 walk the inbox order, which holds no quiet thread (V0.46 (4)); the board shows a quiet project's cards, but opening one goes through (4); no push is sent in quiet hours (V0.46 (3)), and a push tapped from before the window goes through (4); the project screen (quiet hours, name, folder) stays open so the owner can change the hours, and lists no threads; the agents' `threads` tool is not the owner's way in and lists every thread as before. Tests: `web/test/quiet-gate.test.ts` (one test per path) and the layout test (the phone chip shows the gate, not the thread, without the unlock; with it, the thread; `?open=` to a quiet thread shows the gate). |
| V0.115 | → V0.108, V0.50, V0.102, decision 0016. **System agents: the model calls Fyr starts itself run on the provider and model the owner picks in Settings; the default is Haiku on the host's default provider.** *Amends V0.108 (3) and (4).* A system agent is a call Fyr makes on its own behalf, not an owner's thread. There are two jobs: `title` (Regenerate title, V0.108) and `setup` (the project setup run, which sets the icon, colour and name). Before this row the only one was the title call, and it ran on the thread's own provider. (1) **Setting.** It is stored per host (kv `systemAgents`), because a provider and its keys never leave their host (decision 0016): a host's system agents run on that host with its providers. The setting has a default `{profile, model}` and an optional override per job. No provider means the host's default provider (its first). No model means that provider's Haiku model: one in its model list, else the model its endpoint sets in `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`; on a Claude login that is `claude-haiku-5-5`. An override that names a provider is taken whole: its model, or that provider's Haiku. An override that names only a model runs it on the default's provider. Only the owner changes the setting, with the host command `host.systemAgents {job?, profile, model}` or `{job, clear: true}` (owner only, with a receipt); a provider the host does not have is refused. No agent tool sends it. `GET /api/systemAgents?host=` serves the setting, the host's providers with their models and Haiku model, and, for each job, the provider and model it runs on now or why it cannot run. (2) **Settings → System agents**, on desktop and phone (the shared Settings body). When the front has more than one host, a Host picker comes first. Then come a Provider and a Model picker for all jobs, and for each job ("Thread titles", "Project setup") a picker that reads "Same as above" or names its own provider, with a Model picker once it has one. Under each job a line reads "Runs on Claude · claude-haiku-5-5", or gives the reason it cannot run, in the error colour. A provider that cannot run is listed with its reason. On the phone the pickers are 44 px. (3) **One interface.** Every system job reads the setting through `(*agent.Manager).SystemAgent(job)`, which returns `{Host, Profile, Model}` or an error. A print job runs with `SystemPrint(ctx, job, system, prompt)`, the V0.108 launch: in the system temp folder, with a system prompt that replaces Claude Code's. No model id is hardcoded in a system job. The title call (V0.108 (3)) now runs on the setting, no longer on the thread's provider. (4) **Failure is visible.** If the provider is missing, cannot run (no login or key), or has no Haiku model while no model is set, the job fails with a reason that names where to fix it ("Z.ai has no Haiku model: pick a model in Settings → System agents"). A title then stays and the V0.108 (5) failed line shows. A job never falls back to another provider, to the thread's model or to a bigger model. *Amends V0.108 (3)*, which fell back to the thread's model. *Cost (amends V0.108 (4))*: the default is Haiku, so a title costs as before (about $0.001 a call); a bigger model the owner picks costs more. Tests: `TestSystemAgentsSetIsTheOwners`, `TestSystemAgentsFor`, `TestSystemAgentsSetting`, `TestSystemAgent`, `TestSystemAgentFailsVisibly`, `TestHaikuModel` (replaces `TestTitleModel`), `web/test/system-agents.test.ts`, and the layout test's V0.115 checks (desktop and phone). |
| V0.116 | → 3.15, V0.23, V0.55, V0.69. **Project setup: an agent fills in a project's image icon, colour and name; the owner's values are kept.** *Amends V0.23 ("the icon is a monogram, an emoji or a line icon"): a project may also have an image, and the glyph is its fallback.* (1) **The image icon.** A project may carry `image`, the version of its stored icon. The host keeps it under its data folder (`project-icons/<id>/64.png` and `128.png`) and serves it at `GET /api/projects/<id>/icon?size=64\|128&v=<image>` (PNG, `nosniff`, kept by the browser when `v` is given); a front sends `/api/projects/<id>/…` to the project's host. Fyr reads PNG, JPEG and GIF only, at most 2 MB and 4096 × 4096 pixels; a picture up to 5:4 is cut square in the middle, a wider or taller one (a wordmark) is padded with transparency; it is scaled to 64 and 128 px and written again as PNG, which drops every metadata chunk. Every place that draws the project's icon (the shared `ProjectIcon`: the project chip, the row tag, the filter, the thread header, the project screen, Settings → Projects, New thread, the board; desktop and phone) draws the image when there is one and the glyph in the project colour when there is none or it does not load. (2) **Who set it.** A project carries `by: {name, color, image}`: `owner`, `agent`, or `""` for a placeholder. A name equal to the folder's last element and the colour picked from the name are placeholders; for a project from before V0.116 that is derived the same way. A value the owner changes (`project.update`, the upload, Remove image) is the owner's; saving an unchanged value does not change who set it. Three rows in the rights table (`project.name`, `project.color`, `project.image`) keep the owner's value against an agent, as `thread_rename` keeps the owner's title (V0.69): the agent may fill a placeholder and change its own values, never the owner's. An image the owner removed is the owner's choice of no image: the agent does not put one back. (3) **The owner.** The project screen (the shared `ProjectEditor`, desktop and phone) has an Image field with Upload image… (Replace image… when there is one; the browser checks PNG, JPEG or GIF and 2 MB first, the host again: `POST /api/projects/<id>/icon`, X-Fyr, the body is the picture), Remove image (`project.update {id, image: ""}`, which also deletes the files), and Set up automatically (only with a folder). The field under it is "Icon without the image" when there is an image. Name, Colour and Image show "Set by agent" while the agent's value stands. The setup line says "Set up automatically: an agent filled in what you had not set.", or "Automatic setup could not start: <why>". On the phone the image buttons are 44 px high (touch). (4) **The agent's tool.** `project_setup {project?, icon?, name?, color?}` (`project`: an id or a name, default the calling thread's project; `icon`: a full path or an http(s) URL). It is one tool for every field the setup fills, so a later field is a new argument, not a new mechanism. Fyr reads the file or fetches the URL (http and https only, at most 3 redirects, 20 s, 2 MB; every connection, after each redirect too, must go to a public address: loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT and Tailscale addresses are refused), checks and stores it as in (1), and without `color` takes the colour from the icon: the most common hue among its opaque, coloured pixels, mapped to the nearest palette hue (an icon with too little colour gives slate). The Input `project.setup` (agent only) fills each field the owner has not set, marks it the agent's, and the reply names what it set and what it kept ("Kept the name: the owner set it."). (5) **The setup run.** `project.setupRun {id}` (the owner's command, or the host itself) starts a thread titled "Set up <name>" in the project's folder, filed in it, with a fixed brief: look in the folder for the project's own image (favicon, apple-touch-icon, logo files, `public/`, `static/`, `assets/`, the web manifest's icons, the README's image; a square mark over a wordmark); if there is none, fetch only the project's own branding online (its website's icon, or its GitHub owner's avatar from the folder's remote), nothing whose licence does not allow it; convert SVG, WebP or ICO to PNG outside the folder; give the real name (package.json, go.mod, Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, the README title, the repo name) only when the name is the folder's; ask the owner nothing, change no files, and end with one line. The thread runs on the system agent of the job `setup` (V0.115: Settings → System agents, "Project setup"; by default the host's default provider with its Haiku), never a hardcoded model. The project records the run as `setup: {thread, at}`; a system agent that cannot run, or a thread that cannot start, is recorded as `setup: {at, error}` and answered 409, and the project screen shows it. A project without a folder is 400. (6) **Automatic.** A project made with a folder gets one setup run at once. Each project from before with a folder, no image and no run gets one, once, a minute after the host starts and 30 s apart; a run that started (it has a thread) is never repeated by itself, also when the agent set nothing: the owner presses Set up automatically. *Amended by V0.117*: a project whose run never started (setup.error, no thread) gets another try at the next start. Online fetching needs no yes per project (the owner's choice, 16 Nov: own branding only). (7) **Not built, for later fields of the same tool:** the default provider and model, quiet hours, the repo link, a short description, the project's website. Tests: `internal/projicon` (types, size and pixel limits, square and scale, metadata dropped, main colour, the local-network block, redirects), `internal/core` (who set what, placeholders, the owner's kept, the run recorded), `internal/engine/setup_test.go` (the tool from a file and a URL, the owner's upload and remove, the run, AutoSetup on create, the one pass for projects from before, delete removes the files), `internal/server/icon_test.go`, `internal/front/icon_test.go`, `web/test/project-icon.test.ts`, and the layout test (Settings → Projects draws Fyr's image at 28 px and a glyph where an image does not load; Fyr's project screen on the desktop and the phone, its buttons 44 px on the phone). |
| V0.117 | → V0.115, V0.116, V0.108. **On a Claude login the default system agent is claude-haiku-5-5, also when the stored model list has no Haiku; a setup run that never started runs again at the next start.** *Amends V0.115 (1) and V0.116 (6).* (Bug on the live build db1211d, 16 Nov: daemon.log said "Project setup cannot start: Claude has no Haiku model: pick a model in Settings → System agents", with no model set. The host's Claude login stores Opus, Sonnet and Fable only, and V0.115 read the Haiku model from that list alone. Regenerate title failed the same way.) (1) **Haiku.** A provider's Haiku model is one in its model list or its model caps, else the endpoint's `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`. Else, on a Claude login (the claude CLI with no endpoint and no API key; a login by token included), it is the claude CLI's own Haiku (`claude-haiku-5-5`). An endpoint provider with no Haiku still fails the job visibly (V0.115 (4)). The title job, the setup job and the Settings line read the same rule. (2) **Retry.** A setup run that could not start records its error with no thread. The once-per-project pass at host start (V0.116 (6)) gives such a project another try, and the error goes when the run starts. A run that started is never repeated by itself. The pass runs only at host start, so a failing system agent is tried at most once per restart. Tests: `TestHaikuModelOnAStoredClaudeLogin` (the live provider record), `TestSystemAgentDefaultOnAStoredClaudeLogin`, `TestSetupThatCouldNotStartRunsAgain`. |
| V0.118 | → V0.39, V0.81, V0.83, V0.111. **The update status is a line in the layout and never covers a control; it is calm now that deploys are frequent.** *Amends V0.39 (where the banner sits, how long a success shows), #81 (the fold) and #85 (the dismissed key also takes a success that went by itself).* (V0.111) (1) **Place.** *Amended by V0.141: the shell is the window's height, clips, and the document never scrolls.* One component on both fronts (`DeployBanner`, `web/src/deploy-ui.tsx`). On the desktop it is the first line of the page, above the rail, the list and the thread (`.shell` is a column; the app takes the rest), so it pushes the content down. On the phone it is the first line of the screen, below the top safe area, above the header (`PhoneApp`'s `status`); the screen below it is shorter by its height. It is never `position: fixed` or `absolute`: it covers no composer, send button, rail button, header button, sheet or dialog. Collapsed it is one line (30 px on the desktop, 44 px on the phone, touch), the sentence cut with an ellipsis. (2) **Calm.** `build`, `test`, `drain`, `switch` and "Restarting Fyr · back in a few seconds" show as the line with a spinner; a press on it shows the whole sentence (and a failure's details), a second press makes it one line again. A success ("Fyr is updated · build <id>") shows for 5 s from when the page first sees it and then goes by itself, and only when it ended within the last 60 s (a page opened later shows nothing); once gone it is stored as dismissed (#85), so a reload does not show it again. A failure stays, in red, with **Details** and **×** (Dismiss), until it is dismissed; the dismissed result stays dismissed on this device (#85: its phase and build). **Restart now** (V0.83) is on the line while it applies. The fold of #81 is gone: the line is already small. (3) **Client-only.** An update that changes only the web client (V0.81) shows no status: the page's reload is its sign. The deployer marks its progress `client: true` (the phase `client` and its `done`), and the page shows neither. (4) **Not changed, named:** the desktop toast (bottom centre, 4 s or the undo window) and the phone toast (above the bottom bar or the composer at rest) still float over the content for their few seconds; the key hints bar (shown while the owner holds `?`) covers the bottom 28 px; menus, dialogs, sheets and the preview viewer are overlays the owner opens. The live view (V0.37) and the rail's load ring (V0.99) are in the layout already. Tests: `web/test/deploy.test.ts` (what shows when, the 5 s and 60 s), `TestDeployClientOnly` (the client mark), and the layout test: in each phase (`build`, `test`, `drain`, `switch`, `failed`, `done`) at 1280 × 800, 390 × 844 and 360 × 640, with a thread open and with the details opened, the status is not fixed or absolute, its box overlaps no visible button, field or composer outside it (cut by scrolling ancestors), and it is inside the screen below the top inset; `drain` has Restart now, `failed` has Details and Dismiss, Dismiss hides it, `done` goes by itself, `client` shows none; the press opens and closes the line on both fronts. |

| V0.120 | → V0.44, V0.50, V0.115, V0.117, decision 0015. **A provider's models are discovered; the owner turns each on or off, orders them and stars the default.** (1) **Catalog.** Each provider keeps what its endpoint or CLI reports (`catalog`, with when each model was first seen), when a check last succeeded (`checkedAt`) and why the last one failed (`checkError`). An API endpoint is asked at `GET <base URL>/v1/models` from the host with its key (`x-api-key` and `anthropic-version`, then `Authorization: Bearer` on 401 or 404; `has_more`/`last_id` paging up to 20 pages; 15 s and 2 MB a page; no redirect to another host; the key in no log or error). A Claude login uses `claudecli.Models` plus what its CLI reports: the installed claude (2.1.322) has no models command or flag, and its stream-json `initialize` answer lists `models[]` (`resolvedModel`, `displayName`, `supportedEffortLevels`); Fyr starts it with no prompt, no tools, no MCP servers, no settings files and no saved session, reads that answer and ends it. *Found live 16 Nov: that answer comes from a cache in the config folder's `.claude.json` (`additionalModelOptionsCache`) that the CLI fills in the background after it starts; with a cold cache it names only its aliases (default, opus, fable, sonnet, haiku). So while the answer names only aliases Fyr keeps the CLI running and asks again every second for up to 12 s, then starts it once more; if it still names only aliases, the catalog is Fyr's known list and `checkNote` says so in the Models foot ("…so this is Fyr's known list. Refresh in a minute."), never quietly. Every check is logged with the ids it reported (`providers: check <id>: N models reported (…)`). The Providers row says "3 of 14 models on" once checked.* Fyr checks when the owner opens the provider's page and the last check is over 10 minutes old, on Refresh (`provider.refreshModels`, owner only, with its receipt), once a day per provider, and at once after Add provider; a provider that needs a login or a key is not checked; one check per provider at a time; a failed check keeps the last list and shows its reason. (2) **On, order, default.** `models` is the list that is on, in the owner's order, so every picker, `Profile()`, `EffortFor` and `HaikuModel` read it unchanged; ★ is `defaultModel` and is always on (`provider.update` refuses a list without it: "Star another model first."). The stored models of V0.44 and V0.50 start as on; nothing moves; the first check marks nothing New, and an on model it does not list was typed by hand (Added by hand, not Gone). (3) **Marks.** New: first reported after the provider's first check, until the owner switches it or 14 days pass. Gone: on, and the last successful check did not list it; it stays and its threads keep running; off and gone leaves the list. Added by hand: an id the owner typed that no check lists; only these have ×, when off (`provider.update {forget}`). The Providers row says " · 4 new". (4) **Settings → Providers → a provider → Models** (desktop and phone): the header says when it was checked ("Checked 12 min ago", "Checking…", "Last checked yesterday 14:02", "Not checked: needs a login") with Refresh; search from nine models filters both parts by name and id ("13 of 342 match"); the On part with the reorder control of V0.119 (4), ★ and a switch (the default's is locked); the Off part by name with a switch, eight shown (three while searching) and "Show N more"; turning one on puts it last in On. A failed check's reason is the foot, in red. (5) **Other pickers** (New thread, Hand over, System agents, a project's default, the thread's model) keep their control and list only what is on, in order; a model in use that is off shows as "opus 5.4 (off)" there and nowhere else; a project whose default model is off says so on its row. (6) V0.117 holds: the default system agent of a Claude login is its Haiku even when Haiku is off.
