| V0.121 | → V0.23, V0.30, V0.58, V0.74, V0.114, decision 0016. **One project order, set by the owner, used in every list of projects.** *Amends V0.30 (the picker's order by last use).* The front keeps `projectOrder`, one list of project ids across all its hosts, in the database it keeps device data in, and serves it in the state and as the stream event `projectOrder`; `front.projectOrder {ids}` sets it: owner only (the browser's command path with X-Fyr), no host has the kind, so no agent tool or MCP call sends it; a repeated `commandId` answers its first reply; a front that cannot store it refuses with the reason. Listed projects come first in that order, then the rest by creation time, so a new project is last; an unknown id is ignored; with no order set, by name (`orderProjects`). The client's store keeps its projects in that order, so New thread, the project picker, the filter sheet, the filter chips (desktop and phone), the Changes board's chips, Settings → Projects and every other list of projects show it; search filters without reordering; a new thread with no filter and no remembered project starts on the first. Quiet projects stay hidden where V0.74 and V0.114 hide them and keep their place; Settings → Projects lists them. Settings → Projects reorders with the one reorder control: a grip on each row on the desktop (drag, or Alt+↑/↓), a Reorder mode with ↑ and ↓ of 44 px in place of the thread count on the phone (touch); every move saves at once and shows on this device first; while the search has text, reorder is off. Tests: `internal/front/order_test.go` (TestFrontProjectOrder, TestFrontProjectOrderReadOnly); `web/test/projects.test.ts` ("V0.121: listed projects first in the owner's order…", "V0.121: the store keeps its projects in the owner's order…", "V0.121: hiding quiet projects keeps the order of the rest", "V0.121: the Settings list keeps the given order…", "subtitles, and search that filters without reordering…", "SPEC 0.5 V0.30, V0.121: a new thread starts on…"), `web/test/new-thread.test.ts` ("#41, V0.121: the start project is…"); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.121 desktop: Alt+↑ on a project's grip sends front.projectOrder, the chips and New thread's list follow; V0.121 phone: the Reorder mode, ↓ swaps, the chips follow a set order). |
| V0.122 | → V0.44, V0.50, V0.77. **A token account's quota comes from its sessions, never from the usage endpoint; a rate-limit wait survives a restart.** *Amends V0.44 (2) and V0.50 (5).* (1) **A token account is never read.** The host never sends a token account's token to the usage endpoint. Its windows come only from the `rate_limit_event` of its own running sessions (V0.44), and its status carries `source: "session"`. The card shows the windows with their age ("From the last session, 12 min ago"); with none yet it says "usage shows after the next run" and the badge says "After a run". A token account with no token still says "No token yet; paste it in Settings → Providers" (checked locally, no request). (2) **A refused read is logged with its cause.** The log line names the status and the reply's `error.type` and `error.message`, cut to 200 characters, with the token and anything shaped like a key taken out. The owner's status keeps Fyr's own short sentence. (3) **The wait and the data survive a restart.** The host keeps each identity's last status, its next read time and its count of consecutive rate limits in `<data>/quota-state.json` (mode 0600, no secret in it), and loads it at start. A restart during a wait does not read early, and a restarted host continues the 5, 10, 20, 30 min steps where it was. (4) **The badge names its error.** "Rate limited" only for a rate limit; "No token", "Login expired" (a folder login refused or past its expiry) or "Can't read" for the rest. (Checked on this host: the Acme threads hit no inference limit. Every `rate_limit_event` on the account was `status: allowed`, `seven_day` at 67 to 78 %. The `rejected` in those events is `overageStatus` (extra usage is off for the org: `org_level_disabled`), not a limit.) |
| V0.123 | → 3.17, V0.51, V0.54, V0.14, decision 0025. **Multi-host, slice 1: a front finds the owner's other Fyr installs on the tailnet and follows each, as it follows a configured host. The fronts mesh, and the client keeps its one uplink.** *Amends decision 0021 (3) and (8).* (1) **Discovery.** A fyrd that runs a front (`both` or `front`) looks at start and then every 60 s. A candidate is a peer in tailscaled's status that is online, untagged and owned by this node's own Tailscale user, or an origin in config `peers` (flag `-peers a,b`). fyrd probes `GET <origin>/.well-known/fyr`, where a tailnet node's origin is `https://<MagicDNS name>:<port>` and the ports are `peerPorts`, default 7443. Each probe takes at most 3 s, and they run in parallel. A Fyr install answers `{fyr: 1, name, role, build, owner, host}` with `owner` equal to this fyrd's `ownerLogin`, and anything else is skipped. `host` is the URL where it serves the host protocol (its resolved `hostListen`), or empty. This install's own origin is never probed. A found install that stops answering stays listed as offline and is forgotten 30 days after its last answer. Every front answers `/.well-known/fyr` behind its guard (V0.14). (2) **Following.** A found install with a `host` URL joins the front's hosts while the front runs, as a remote host (no browser identity headers are passed on), and is followed and routed exactly as a configured one (V0.51, V0.54). A host already followed under the same name (configured, or the name it reports) or the same URL is not added again. A host part dials nothing. (3) **The client** is unchanged: one uplink to the front it opened. `hosts[]` in the state adds `found: true` for a host discovery added. Settings → Projects → Hosts writes such a row's status with "· found on the tailnet", the same on the desktop and the phone. (4) **Two installs on one machine** (development, tests): a `hostListen` on a loopback address serves the host protocol there behind the loopback guard, and config `name` overrides the hostname that the install reports. Not in this slice: keeping found hosts across a front restart, a relay through a third front, and removing the static `hosts` entries. |
| V0.124 | → V0.51, V0.54, V0.123, decision 0026. **Install keys, slice 1: every install has a key, and a host is shown by its id.** (decision 0026) Nothing is refused by key yet; trust by key comes with the roster (0026 slice 2). (1) **The key.** At its first start fyrd makes an Ed25519 key pair in `<data>/identity.key` (mode 0600, made once with O_EXCL) and logs its id. The id is the SHA-256 of the public key, the first 80 bits in base32 as four groups of four (`F7QK-2M9X-AB34-CD56`); the short form is the first group. A key file that is not a Fyr key stops fyrd with a message that names it; it is never replaced, because a new key is a new install. (2) **The id on the wire.** `/.well-known/fyr` adds `id`. A host's `GET /api/state` carries `hostId`, its own id; a front's merged state carries its own install's. (3) **Pins.** The front pins the first id each host reports, by host name, in its database (kv `front.hostIds`), and serves it in `hosts[].id`, also while the host is offline. When the host under that name reports another id, `hosts[]` adds `idWas` (the pinned one), the front logs it once and keeps following the host. `front.hostId {host}` (owner only: the browser's command path with X-Fyr; no host has the kind) pins the id the host reports now; a host that has reported none is refused with 409, an unknown host with 400; a repeated `commandId` answers its first reply. A host older than V0.124 reports no id, and its row shows none. (4) **The stream.** The front sends `hosts` (`{hosts: [...]}`, the same rows as the state) when a host goes online or offline and when a pin changes, so Settings follows without a reload. (5) *Amended by V0.125: Hosts is its own Settings page; the row opens the host's page, where Trust now is.* **Settings → Projects → Hosts**, the same row on the desktop and the phone: the status ends in "· id F7QK"; a changed id reads "online · Another install answers as <host>: id B7LA, it was AX2C. Trust it if you reinstalled or replaced this machine." in the error colour, with a **Trust B7LA** button at the end (44 px on the phone, for touch). Tests: `internal/identity/identity_test.go` (TestLoadMakesOnceAndKeeps, TestLoadRefusesABrokenKey, TestTwoInstallsDiffer); `internal/front/hostid_test.go` (TestFrontPinsHostIDs, TestFrontHostIDOfflineAndOld); `web/test/hosts.test.ts` ("V0.124: a host row shows its short install id…"); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.124 desktop and phone: the id, the changed line, Trust inside the screen, cleared after the press). |
| V0.125 | → V0.119, V0.124, decision 0026. **Settings → Hosts: a fifth Settings page lists the hosts; a host's page shows its full id, its address and route, and a changed id with Trust.** *Amends V0.124 (5) (the row's line changes; Trust moves to the host's page) and V0.119 (a fifth page).* Still slice 1 of 0026: nothing about trust changes. (1) **API.** `hosts[]` adds `url`: the URL the front dials for the host, as configured or as discovery found it, also while it is offline. The client keeps the state's `hostId`, so a front with no `hosts[]` still shows this install's id. (2) **The nav.** Providers, Automation, Projects, **Hosts**, This device (`?dialog=settings&page=hosts`, `&page=hosts/<name>`; phone `?settings=hosts/<name>`; the name URL-encoded). Its summary: "3 hosts", "3 hosts · 1 offline", "3 hosts · 1 needs you" (needs you wins), "This install only" with no or one host. The amber count shows beside any page that needs the owner (Providers as before, Hosts: the hosts with a changed id); the phone's More sheet adds the hosts to its count. Projects says "10 projects" and has no Hosts group. (3) **The Hosts page**, one shared row on both fronts, this install first: a button with a state mark (filled: online; a ring: offline; red: needs you, never colour alone), the name in mono (+ "This install"), and the line `<state> · <route> · id <short>`: "online since 08:12 · this machine · id F7QK", "offline since 19:40 · tailnet · id M3RS", "… · LAN · no id". A time on another day reads "Mon 06:00". The route comes from the URL: this install or a loopback address "this machine"; 100.64/10 or `.ts.net` "tailnet"; a private address, `.local`, `.lan`, `.home.arpa` or a bare name "LAN"; else "by address"; no `url` gives "tailnet" for a found host and no route otherwise. A changed id replaces the line ("Another install answers as lab: id B7LA, it was AX2C.") with the badge "Needs you"; that row alone is red, an offline one is not. No buttons; the foot: "Fyr finds your other installs on the tailnet by itself. A host it cannot see goes in fyrd's config (`hosts`)." (with one row it starts "This is your only Fyr install."). The phone's rows are 56 px (touch). (4) **A host's page** (`hosts/<name>`; an unknown name shows the list): the pill Online, Offline or Needs you (desktop: at the end of the title; phone: first on the page, as its header has only the title and Back). Identity: the full id with Copy (where the browser has a clipboard; "Copied" for 2 s) and "Fyr remembers this id…", or for this install "The id of the Fyr you are using…", or "None. This host runs a Fyr older than V0.124…"; for another host the row Trust: "Through your tailnet: Fyr follows it because it is a node of your Tailscale login, not because of its key." Connection: "Online since 08:31" / "Offline since 19:40" with the error ("Unreachable.") and "Its threads show as last read and cannot be changed.", or "Updating Fyr: running the tests"; Address (`url` · route); and how it got on the list ("Found on the tailnet … leaves this list 30 days after its last answer." / "Set in this front's config … until it is taken out of `hosts` there."). A changed id: a red note above ("**Another install answers as lab.** That is normal after you reinstalled or replaced lab. It is wrong if you did not."), Identity holds "It was" and "It is now" (both ids in full) and "Trust B7LA as lab" with **Trust B7LA** (`front.hostId`, as V0.124; 44 px on the phone); the foot: "Fyr still follows lab meanwhile: today it trusts a host through your tailnet login, not its key. If you do not know this install, take that machine off your tailnet." A refused Trust says "Not trusted: desk answered “…”. Nothing changed." on its row. Esc, the crumb and Back go to the list, focus on the row. (5) **This device** adds "How this browser gets in", from the page's host and the state's user: `.ts.net` "Through Tailscale Serve, as <user>. Any browser signed in to your tailnet with that login can open this Fyr."; localhost or a loopback address "On this machine itself (localhost). A browser on this machine is always allowed."; else "At <host>, as <user>. What stands in front of Fyr there decides who gets in." Nothing of the later slices is drawn: no Pair, Add by address, Found, Remove or Block, and no disabled buttons; the sentences above say how each thing is done today (`design/pairing/SPEC.md` part 3). Tests: `internal/front/hostid_test.go` (TestFrontHostURL); `web/test/hosts.test.ts` (V0.125: routeOf, the row's line, the rows, How this browser gets in); `web/test/settings-nav.test.ts` (five pages, Hosts' summary and badge, Projects without hosts, the More sheet's count, `hosts/<name>`); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.125 desktop and phone: the nav, the list, lab's page and Trust, a plain host's page and the way back, This device; phone screens "settings hosts" and "settings host changed" at every size). Screenshots: `design/pairing/impl/`. |
| V0.126 | → V0.13, V0.81, V0.111, V0.90, decision 0027. **Fyr updates itself only to CI-built, signed releases; a local build is a pressed developer switch.** *Amends V0.13 (2) and V0.111.* (#144, #157) (1) **Release.** A green push to main runs CI's `release` job: `fyr` and `fyrd` for linux and darwin (amd64, arm64), `manifest.json`, `SHA256SUMS` and its ed25519 signature, published as `build-<sha12>`; the tag `deployable` moves forward to it; the newest 30 build releases are kept. A push to main is never cancelled by the next (#146). (2) **Install.** `fyr.deploy` without `dir` installs a release (the given `commit`, else `deployable`): download into `builds/<sha12>` (reused when verified), verify the signature with the pinned key and every sum, then `fyrd selftest` on a database snapshot (`fyrd version` must be the build); no `make test`. Then the switch of V0.13 (3)–(5). Releases always restart. (3) **Trigger.** Every host reads `deployable` each minute; a move waits `updateQuietMinutes` (10) without another move, then installs once. Off when Auto-update is off. A move back is followed. A release that rolled back on this host is skipped until a newer one. (4) **Gate.** An ask without `dir` is approved by the Auto-update setting with the commit `deployable` names; an ask with `dir` always waits for the owner's press ("a local build of … needs your press"). (5) **Local build.** `fyr.deploy {dir, tests?}` builds the checkout, writes `build.log` (and `test.log`) into the build, runs the smoke test, or `make test` when `tests` is true; a failure names the failing test or step and the log on its first line and keeps the logs. Once it ran its probation, auto-update pauses (kv `update_paused`) until a release contains its commit or a release is installed by an ask. (6) **Health.** fyrd answers `GET /healthz` on its main listener (store read, client page, runner adopted); the ready file names the listener; the launcher rolls back a build that is not healthy within the ready timeout, or that crashes within 10 min of its switch; it writes `probation-ok` when the probation ended, and fyrd reports success then. (7) **Housekeeping.** After a good switch, builds beyond current, previous, next and the newest 3 are deleted, and `pre.db` stays only in current and previous; a build or download needs 2 GB (or 3× the database) free. (8) **Mac.** `scripts/install-macos.sh` downloads the `deployable` release; `scripts/update.sh` and its launchd job are gone. |
| V0.127 | → V0.115, V0.119, V0.120, V0.125, V0.126, decision 0016. **Every host's settings from any front, as if connected to it directly; slice 1: Providers, System agents and Automation, grouped by host.** *Amends V0.115 (2) (the Host picker goes: each host has its own System agents) and V0.119 (the per-host pages have one part per host).* (1) **Scope.** Per host: providers, logins and keys, the model catalog, System agents, Auto-deploy and the update record (V0.126), Auto-merge, projects and their defaults, quiet hours, project setup. Front-wide: push, presence, the project order (V0.121), the hosts and their ids (V0.124, V0.125), This device. (2) **Routing.** Every per-host read names its host with `?host=` and every per-host command with `args.host`; the front sends it to that host over its host client on the ordinary command path (`POST /api/command`, X-Fyr, the client's `commandId` unchanged, so a retry runs once), as the owner, as for any command (decision 0028 adds its checks there later). A project's commands keep going to the project's host by its id. A host name the front does not follow is refused with 404 ("this front follows no host lab"), for a command, a read, an upload and a preview: nothing goes to the first host instead. No name is the front's first host, as before. A key and a login stay on their host: a login (V0.53) runs there and the page follows it through the front. `/api/quota` stamps each quota with its `host` and keeps one per host, so the same provider id on two hosts has two quotas. (3) **Offline.** The front keeps the last answer of each settings read per host (`/api/providers`, `/api/providers/found`, `/api/systemAgents`, `/api/autoDeploy`, `/api/autoMerge`, `/api/updates`), also on disk with its cache (V0.54). While the host is offline, or when a read fails before the front has noticed, it serves that answer with `X-Fyr-Read-At: <when it was read>`; with nothing kept it answers 503 as before. Commands to an offline host are refused as before. (4) **The pages** (desktop and phone, the same components): with one host (or a front that lists none) Providers and Automation are as before. With several, each page has one part per host, this install first, then the front's order: a heading with the host's name (mono) and "This install", "Online since 08:31" or "Offline since 19:40"; under it the page's groups for that host (Providers, Found on this host, Add provider, System agents; Auto-update, Updates, Merge without asking). An offline host's part says "host-b is offline. These are its settings as Fyr last read them at 19:39; nothing here can be changed until it is back." and its controls are off; with nothing read it says "…has not read its settings yet. They show when it is back." and shows nothing else. A host's provider is `providers/<host>/<id>` (`?settings=providers/lab/claude` on the phone), titled with the provider and the lede "On lab."; Add provider on a host says its key stays there; Esc and Back go to the Providers page with focus on that row (`data-sub` "lab/claude"). The nav's lines count every host ("7 · 1 needs a login", "Deploys on 3 of 4 hosts · merges in 2 repos"); an offline host never read is left out; the phone's More sheet counts the same. A project's provider and model pickers list its host's providers. Not in this slice (slice 2): New project, a project's Change folder and New thread browse the folders of the chosen host (the folder picker reads the front's first host today). Tests: `internal/front/hostsettings_test.go` (TestFrontHostSettingsReachTheirHost, TestFrontRefusesAnUnknownHost, TestFrontServesLastKnownSettingsOfAnOfflineHost, TestFrontServesLastKnownSettingsWhenTheReadFails, TestFrontQuotaPerHost); `web/test/host-settings.test.ts`; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.127 desktop and phone: the four parts and their lines, the offline part read-only with its note, System agents per host, lab's Claude page with lab's quota and Esc back to its row, `host.systemAgents` and `host.autoDeploy` sent with `host: "lab"`, 44 px controls and nothing past the edge on the phone). Checked by hand with two fyrd on one machine (V0.123 (4)): from desk's front a provider, System agents, Auto-deploy and a project changed on lab and only there; lab stopped, its settings showed as last read. |
| V0.128 | → V0.37, V0.33. **A message typed on the View tab is an ordinary message, and the thread then shows Conversation.** *Supersedes V0.49 and V0.57.* The composer on the View tab sends exactly what the Conversation tab's composer sends: no surface mark, no prefix for the agent, and the view prompt says nothing about it. When the send succeeds the thread shows the Conversation tab, which opens at its end, where the new message is; a failed send stays on View with the text in the field. The View tab draws no chat: no bubble over the view, no reply, nothing to dismiss. Desktop and phone share the one rule (`sendFrom` in `web/src/thread.tsx`). A message stored with the old mark (`from: "view-typed"`) renders and reads as the owner's own message, without the View tag, on the fronts, in `thread_read` and in a handover; a stale client that still sends the mark sends an ordinary message. A press in the live view keeps its mark and its View tag (V0.37). The delivery state still follows the host's `outbox` (from V0.49). |
| V0.129 | → V0.12, V0.14, V0.51, V0.54, decision 0028 (slice 0). **Small closings: the MCP token in a file, `/mcp` refuses web pages, the both role's own host in-process, and a tagged node names no owner.** (decision 0028) Nothing a browser does changes. (1) **The token in a file.** A thread's MCP config (URL and bearer, V0.12) reaches the CLI as a file path in `--mcp-config`, never as JSON on the command line, where `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` shows it to every local user. The file is made with mode 0600 in `<data>/run` and removed when the start returns: the CLI reads it while it starts. fyrd removes any `mcp-*.json` left there when it starts. (2) **`/mcp` refuses a request that carries `Origin`** (403), before the token is looked at. An agent's MCP client sends none (checked against `claude` 2.1.322); a browser always does on a cross-origin POST. (3) **In-process link.** In the `both` role the front reaches its own host through an in-process listener at `http://localhost`; no loopback port is opened for it. The host's guard and the route ("this machine") are unchanged. (4) **A tagged node.** When tailscaled says this node has tags, fyrd takes no owner from it (before, it took the node's own name) and logs "this node is tagged and has no Tailscale user: set ownerLogin"; without `ownerLogin` it serves no browser over the tailnet. Discovery still knows its MagicDNS name. (5) **NixOS:** `services.fyr.ownerLogin` (string, default null) writes `ownerLogin` to the config, so a tagged host needs no ExecStart of its own. Tests: `internal/agent/mcpfile_test.go` (TestV129MCPTokenInAFileNotArgv: a 0600 file under the run folder holds the token, the argv does not, the file is gone after the start); `internal/mcp/mcp_test.go` (TestOriginRefused); `internal/front/pipe_test.go` (TestV129FrontFollowsAHostOverAPipe); `internal/tailnet/tailnet_test.go` (TestSelfOfATaggedNodeNamesNoOwner); `internal/cli/claudecli/claudecli_test.go` (TestArgs). |
| V0.130 | → V0.14, V0.129, decision 0028 (slice 1, first release: observe). **Every browser can hold a session; `fyr open` and the tailnet mint one; nothing is refused yet.** The next release (enforce) refuses what this one only logs. (1) **Owner socket.** A front (`both` or `front`) listens on `<data>/owner.sock` (mode 0600; the runner's short path when too long, V0.90) and accepts a connection only when the peer's user (SO_PEERCRED on Linux, LOCAL_PEERCRED on macOS) is fyrd's own or root; any other is closed and logged. `POST /session/once` answers `{url: "http://fyr.localhost:<port>/auth/once#<token>"}`, a one-time link valid 60 s. **`fyr open [-app]`** asks for it and opens it: with `-app` in a Chrome app window (`FYR_BROWSER` names another browser; macOS `open -na "Google Chrome" --args --app=…`), else with `xdg-open`/`open`. (2) **Sessions.** `GET /auth/once` is a page that posts the fragment's token to `POST /auth/once` (with `X-Fyr`) and goes to `/`; the front sets `fyr_session` (32 random bytes; HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, path /, Secure on HTTPS) for a new device `{id, name, how: local\|tailnet, created, lastSeen}`, named from the User-Agent ("Chrome on Linux · on desk"). A used, unknown or older-than-60-s link gets 410 with "that link no longer works: run fyr open again". A browser that already holds a valid session keeps it and gets no second device. The front keeps only each session's SHA-256 (kv `auth.devices`); a session unseen for 90 days ends. (3) **Serve's socket.** A front also listens on `<data>/serve.sock` (0600; tailscaled runs as root) and serves the browser API there. Only a request that came through it counts as come through Serve: with `Tailscale-User-Login` equal to the owner and no session, it mints a `tailnet` device (Secure cookie). The same header over TCP mints nothing. NixOS: `fyr-tailnet.service` runs `tailscale serve --https=PORT unix:<home>/.local/share/fyr/serve.sock`. (4) **The guard** takes `fyr.localhost` (any `*.localhost`) as a loopback name. (5) **Observe.** A request to `/api/` without a session is still served as before (V0.14) and logged, at most once per way in (loopback, a tailnet header over TCP, Serve's socket) every 10 minutes: "auth (observe): … has no session; the next release refuses it". `/healthz`, `/.well-known/fyr`, `/mcp` and the client's files are not logged. Tests: `internal/auth/auth_test.go` (TestOnceWorksOnceWithinAMinute, TestRedeemKeepsAValidSession, TestLookupStoresOnlyTheHashAndExpires, TestSessionsSurviveARestart, TestPeerAllowed, TestOwnerSocketTakesThisUser); `internal/front/auth_test.go` (TestV130OnceLinkSetsTheSession, TestV130ServeSocketMintsATailnetSession, TestV130HeaderOverTCPMintsNothing, TestV130FyrLocalhostPassesTheGuard); `cmd/fyr/open_test.go` (TestOnceURLAsksTheOwnerSocket, TestBrowserCommand). |
| V0.131 | → V0.13, V0.90, #148, #167. **Only the live install's own fyrd uses its runner and ready file; an agent's fyrd or test can no longer end other threads' processes.** (16–17 Nov: 11 runs ended with "lost the link to the runner: EOF". An agent's scratch `fyrd serve -data …` inherited `FYR_RUNNER`, adopted the live runner's processes, found none of their threads in its own database and killed every one.) (1) **fyrd takes the launcher's variables** (`FYR_RUNNER`, `FYR_LAUNCHER`, `FYR_READY_FILE`, `FYR_LAUNCHER_PID`) once at start and removes them from its environment, so no agent, test or tool it starts inherits them; the agent spawn paths (runner and child, `claude -p`, the model list, login) also leave them out. It keeps each only when it names its own data folder (`runner.SocketPath(data)`, `<data>/ready`) and its parent is the launcher: the pid in `FYR_LAUNCHER_PID`, or with a launcher from before V0.131 the process that listens on the runner socket (SO_PEERCRED, LOCAL_PEERPID on macOS). Otherwise it logs "launcher: not this install's, left alone", runs its agents as its own children, writes no ready file and cannot switch builds. `fyrd -runner SOCK` uses a runner without the checks (told explicitly). (2) **The runner serves only the launcher's fyrd.** The launcher passes `FYR_LAUNCHER_PID` and allows a connection only from the pid of the fyrd it started; any other is refused with an error frame and logged ("runner: refused pid N"). (3) **The launcher trusts only its fyrd's ready file**: the pid in it must be its child's, and the /healthz address is read only from that line; another pid is ignored and logged. (4) **Adopt ends nothing of another install**: when none of the runner's processes belongs to a thread in this database, fyrd leaves them all alone (the runner's V0.90 (2) rule ends what nobody collects). (5) `make` unexports the variables. A pending question is not more exposed than a running turn: idle stop skips a session with open asks, and V0.90 (2) counts only while no fyrd is linked. Tests: `internal/deploy/isolation_test.go` (TestStrayFyrdLeavesTheLiveRunnerAlone: a launcher, its runner and a live fyrd holding an agent process; a child with the live fyrd's whole environment runs a fyrd of its own; the live process, its link and the ready file stay. TestLauncherIgnoresAnotherProcesssReadyFile); `internal/runner/isolation_test.go` (TestQuestionPendingAcrossStrayAndUpdate, TestAgentProcessGetsNoLauncherVars, TestPeerPidIsTheListener); `internal/launchenv/launchenv_test.go`; `internal/engine/engine_test.go` (TestAdoptLeavesAnotherInstallsProcesses). |
| V0.132 | → V0.42, V0.54, V0.55, V0.121, V0.125, V0.127, decision 0016, decision 0026. **Multi-host, slice 2: New project, a project's Change folder and New thread work on the chosen host and browse its folders; the owner orders the hosts, and the first is the default everywhere.** *Amends V0.54 ("the first host (the front's default)": now the first in the owner's order), V0.125 (3) and V0.127 (4) ("this install first": now the owner's order).* (1) **Folders of the chosen host.** The folder picker's reads and New folder name their host (`/api/fs/list|recent|repos?host=`, `POST /api/fs/mkdir?host=`); the front sends them there (V0.127 (2): a host it does not follow is refused). The picker's title says the host ("Project folder on lab") with several hosts. An offline host's picker shows "host-b is offline since 19:40. Its folders cannot be read and nothing can be made on it until it is back; pick another host or wait." and no other host's folders. (2) **New project** (Settings → Projects, and the New project step of New thread): with several hosts a Host field comes first, in the owner's order ("desk (this install)", "host-b (offline)"), the first host picked; its folder is that host's, and picking another host drops a folder picked on the first; `project.create` carries `host`. A project made from threads (the project picker, V0.30) lives on their host and says "On lab."; nothing is made on an offline host (the note above, on the form, and no command is sent). (3) **New thread:** the host follows the project (the Host field is locked and says "Follows Lab notes."); with No project, or a project made in the form, the owner picks it. A new host drops a picked folder and moves the provider and model to ones that host has (its `hosts[].profiles`). Change opens that host's folders; `thread.create` carries `host`. (4) **Change folder** of a project browses the project's host. (5) **The host order** is the owner's, not a front's: Settings → Hosts reorders with the one reorder control (V0.119 (4): a grip and Alt+↑/↓ on the desktop, a Reorder mode with ↑ and ↓ on the phone); each move sends `front.hostOrder {names}`. The front stamps it with the time and writes it to every host it follows with `host.hostOrder {names, at}` (owner only, with a receipt, kv `hostOrder`; an older `at` changes nothing), keeps a copy, and every host sends a newer order on its stream (`hostOrder`), so every front that follows the host shows it at once; a host that was offline gets the newest when a front sees it again, and a front started later reads it from its hosts' state. *Why on the hosts:* every front follows the owner's hosts, so the order reaches the phone's front on host-b and the laptop's alike, today; the signed roster of decision 0026 slice 2 will carry it when it exists. The front sends `hosts[]` (state and the `hosts` event) in that order, and the first is the default host: a command, read or upload that names none (`byName("")`), New project, New thread with no project, the folder picker; the Hosts page, the Host fields and the per-host groups of V0.127 list hosts in it. Hosts the order does not name follow, in the config's order. The project order (V0.121) stays per front for now. Tests: `internal/front/hostfolders_test.go` (TestFrontCreatesOnTheChosenHost: from host a's front, b's folders, New folder, a project and two threads on b; TestFrontFoldersOfAnOfflineHost), `internal/front/hostorder_test.go` (TestHostOrderIsTheOwnersAcrossFronts: two fronts, the order set on one, the other shows b first and sends a command with no host to b; TestHostOrderReachesAHostThatWasOffline), `internal/engine/hostorder_test.go` (TestHostOrderNewestWins); `web/test/host-folders.test.ts`; `web/test/hosts.test.ts`, `web/test/host-settings.test.ts` (the order); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.132 desktop and phone: New project on lab with lab's folders and `project.create` with host lab, the offline host's note and no command, New thread locked to its project and then on lab with lab's picker and `thread.create` with host lab, the Host field 44 px on the phone, reorder with Alt+↑ and with the Reorder mode sending `front.hostOrder`, and lab first as the default host and first group). |
| V0.133 | → V0.7, V0.37, V0.90, V0.3, V0.16. **Fyr never closes an agent's input while the CLI has background work, and a tool call the CLI refuses without asking Fyr is shown as that, not as the owner's stop.** *Amended in #180: a refresh that waited for background work closes the input only after `RefreshGrace` (2 s) with no turn started, since the CLI starts its own turn right after its last task ends; a turn in the grace ends with the refresh.* *Amends V0.7 (the idle stop) and V0.37 (the view refresh).* **Cause, reproduced with claude 2.1.322 in stream-json mode:** Fyr closed the CLI's stdin (the idle stop after 10 minutes, or the end of a process after a view or effort change) while a background Bash, Monitor or background agent still ran. The CLI lives on for that work and starts a turn by itself when it ends; every control round trip of that turn (a hook callback, can_use_tool) fails on the closed stream ("AbortError: Stream closed"), and the CLI cancels the tool call with "The user doesn't want to take this action right now. STOP what you are doing and wait for the user to tell you how to proceed." (`tool_result_meta` `non_execution_kind: cancelled`, or no meta in a subagent). The agent then told the owner it stopped "as you asked". With stdin open the same turn asks the gate hook and runs. Of 30 such refusals in 13 thread logs (16–17 Nov), 16 are this case (`cancelled`, in a turn the CLI started after Fyr closed the input). The other 14 are tool calls of subagents, mostly nested or in the background, refused within about 80 ms with no meta and no gate hook while the input was open; that cause is not reproduced yet, and only (2) and (3) cover it. (1) **Background work keeps the input open.** The agent manager counts the tasks of the CLI's last `background_tasks_changed` (an adopted process starts from the thread's running tasks). The idle stop (V0.7) and the end after a run for a new view or effort (V0.37, V0.50) wait while that count is above 0; when it drops to 0 between turns, the waiting end goes ahead, or the idle stop is armed again. An explicit stop (Stop, a handover's end) still closes at once. (2) **A refusal is recorded.** A `tool_result` with `is_error` whose text starts with the CLI's reject text (Fyr's own denials say something else) adds a `tool.refused` event `{tool, toolUseId, why, subagent?}`; *why* says Fyr had closed the input when it had, else that the CLI refused without asking Fyr, and ends "not the owner". fyrd logs it. The conversation shows it as one failed line, "Bash was refused without asking you", with *why* as its text, on desktop and phone (the one conversation component). (3) **The prompt** (V0.12) says that this text in a turn no message started is not the user: the agent says which call was blocked and waits, and never tells the user they asked it to stop. Not covered: a CronCreate or ScheduleWakeup prompt that fires after the input closed is not a background task the CLI reports. Tests: `internal/engine/closedinput_test.go` (TestIdleStopWaitsForBackgroundWork: idle stop 100 ms, a background task of 700 ms, the turn after it reads its output and the process stops afterwards; TestViewRefreshWaitsForBackgroundWork; TestCLIRefusalIsRecorded); the fake CLI's `bgwait N` and `refused` behave as claude 2.1.322 does on a closed stdin; `internal/agent/prompts_v133_test.go`; `web/test/conversation.test.ts` (the refusal line). |
| V0.134 | → V0.23, V0.100, V0.114, V0.121, V0.132. **The thread list filters by host as well as by project, and shows a filter value only while it has threads in view; a selected value stays until the owner clears it.** *Amends V0.23 (the project filter: now one of two filters), V0.100 (2) (the chip row shows every project with threads: now only active ones, after the host chips) and V0.114 (2) (a quiet project's chip: not shown while locked).* The list's filters today are these two; the phone menu's "Filter by project…" sheet is another way to the project filter and keeps listing every project with threads. (1) **Host filter.** One chip per host with the host's dot from Settings → Hosts (filled online, a ring offline, signal when its id changed or the front no longer lists it) and its name in mono, in the owner's host order (V0.132), before the project chips with a thin line between the groups. Picking a host shows only its threads (a thread that names no host is on the front's own host). It combines with the project filter: a thread shows when it passes both. A click on the selected chip clears that filter; the × clears both. Remembered per device (`fyr.hostFilter`, beside `fyr.projectFilter`). (2) **Only active values.** A value is active when at least one thread in view matches it: a pinned thread, or a thread in a section that is not folded (Snoozed and Settled are folded by default; any section the owner folded counts as folded; the threads past "Show all" are in view). Each group counts the threads the other group lets through, so with a host picked the project chips are that host's projects. A group shows only with two or more active values: one host, no host chips; one project in view, no project chips. A chip's tooltip says how many of its threads are in view. (3) **A selected value stays.** A selected chip stays, selected and in its place in the order, when its last thread leaves (settled, archived, moved) or is folded away, and its group stays with it even with one value; a host the front no longer lists keeps its chip by name, after the listed ones. The owner clears it by hand. While a filter is on and none of its threads is in view, the list shows above its sections "No threads in view for <host> · <project>.", "N more in folded sections." when folded sections hold some, and a "Clear filter" button that clears both filters (44 px high on the phone). The section headers keep their counts. A project that no longer exists filters nothing, as before. (4) **Quiet projects (V0.114).** A quiet project's chip is not shown and its threads count for no chip while it is locked; during the unlock its chip shows (its threads count for the project chips, and for the host chips only when it is picked, as the inbox shows them only then). A quiet project that was selected keeps its chip and its gate (V0.114 (2)) in place of the empty state. An offline host's threads still count. (5) **Desktop and phone** render the one row and the one empty state (`FilterRow`, `FilterEmpty` in `web/src/inbox.tsx`); the rules are pure in `web/src/filters.ts`. The phone sets 32 px chips (touch). Tests: `web/test/filters.test.ts`; the layout test's V0.134 sections, at 1280 and 390 wide. |
| V0.135 | → V0.91, V0.100, V0.104, V0.116, V0.22, #56. **The title gets the room in an inbox row: two lines before it is cut, the project as its icon, and a quiet ↳ for a started thread.** *Amends V0.91 (1) and (2) and V0.100 (1): the project tag (icon and name) is no longer first on line one, and "from <thread>" is no longer in the row.* On both fronts (`web/src/row-ui.tsx`), in every section: (1) **Title.** The project's icon, then the title, which wraps to a second line and is cut with an ellipsis only at the end of that line. Its box is always two lines high, a short title too, so a row keeps its height while its title streams in or changes. The title is in the ink colour, not muted. The pin mark (V0.104) follows the icon. The age stays at the end of line one; the state mark stays at the left. (2) **Project.** The icon alone, with the name as its tooltip and accessible name. The name is shown, muted, at the start of the second line only when the project has no image icon (V0.116), since a monogram or glyph may not say which project it is, and never while a project filter is on (then every row is that project). (3) **Started by.** A thread another thread started shows a single "↳" on the second line, in the line's muted UI font (not monospace, not link blue); its tooltip and accessible name are "from <parent title>", and on the desktop a click opens the parent. Many rows share one parent (a coordinator's threads), so the row never repeats the parent's title; the open thread's header still says "from <parent title>" in full (V0.22). (4) **Second line.** In the other sections it is in the UI font, not monospace. Everything else on it and the chips are as in V0.91. The phone keeps its larger sizes (15 px title, 16 px icon) for touch. *Tests:* `web/test/rows.test.ts` (when the name shows), `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.135 desktop" and "V0.135 phone": at 1280 px and at 390 px every row's title box is at least 60 % of the row, exactly two lines high and clamped at two; the icon is at the title's start; no project name on line one; no row says "from "; the ↳ mark is neither monospace nor link blue. |
| V0.136 | → V0.43, V0.97, V0.102, V0.112, V0.101, 5.10. **Fyr asks the owner to merge only when the merge can run now: an agent's merge ask waits for green CI on its head.** *Amends V0.43 (2), V0.97 (1), V0.101 (1), V0.102 (2) and V0.112 (3), (4).* (#846, V0.112) (1) **One gate.** Every merge ask (`change_request {ask: merge}`, `effect_request pr.merge`) goes through `mergeGate`, which reads only the PR snapshot the poller keeps (no new GitHub reads): **close** when the PR is not open, its head is not the ask's head, it is a draft, it conflicts, a review asks for changes, CI failed ("CI failed on 7333251: test, lint", at most 5 names), it is behind its base, or GitHub's branch rules block it (see (7)); **wait** while its checks are pending (running, or none reported yet on a repo with CI, V0.112) or GitHub's merge state is UNKNOWN; **open** otherwise. A merge state Fyr never read (empty) opens; the runner reads GitHub again before it merges (V0.112). A repo with no CI does not wait. (2) **At the ask.** Open: the ask is a Decision as before. Wait: the ask is recorded in the thread's `heldAsks`, not in `decisions`: no card, no Needs you, no attention change and so no push, and the event `decision.held`. The tool answers "Recorded. Fyr shows it to the owner when CI is green on 7333251, and tells you in a [Fyr] message if CI fails or GitHub cannot merge it." Close: the request is refused with the reason ("Fyr does not ask the owner to merge: CI failed on 7333251: test. Fix it, push, and ask again"). An `effect_request pr.merge` on a PR no change of the thread tracks links it first (as `pr_link`), so the poller reads it. (3) **After every reduce** (the poller's read included), Fyr runs the gate on each held ask and each open merge ask: a held ask opens on green (`decision.opened`, created now, so a snoozed thread wakes) and auto-merges where the repo's setting is on (V0.102); an open ask goes back to held when CI runs again on its head; a held or open ask closes on red with `decision.closed {gate: true, reason}`, its answer "Not asked: <reason>", and the agent hears "Fyr did not ask the owner to merge <PR>: <reason>. Fix it, push, and ask again; Fyr shows the new ask when CI is green on its head." A held ask on an ended or done change, or in an archived thread, closes the same way (0024 §5). (4) **A push closes the ask.** *Amends V0.102 (2)*, where a push left the ask open and the owner's press got "The head moved": the ask closes with "the head moved to b2c3d4e", because CI on the old head can no longer turn green. (5) **The card.** A change with a held ask carries `need {cause: waiting, muted: true, text, head}`: "Waiting for CI: 3 of 5 checks done on 7333251. Fyr asks you to merge when it is green." ("no check has reported yet" when GitHub lists none), with no beam and no button, and it is not in the board's Needs you column. The poller's PR snapshot carries `checksDone`, `checksTotal` and `failedChecks` from the rollup it already reads. While the change's checks are pending, its built-in Merge is not offered; the PR tab keeps Merge… disabled with V0.112's reason. V0.112's `need.wait` and the disabled yes on an ask go: an open merge ask is green by construction. Desktop and phone share the card (`NeedLine`). (7) **Blocked by the branch rules.** When GitHub reads the asked head as BLOCKED with its checks green, the ask closes (or is refused at the ask) and the agent hears the rule and its next step, as for a CI failure. The poller names the rule: for such a PR only, it reads the base's active rules once per repo and base, kept 30 minutes (`GET repos/{repo}/rules/branches/{base}`; when no ruleset applies, `GET repos/{repo}/branches/{base}` for classic protection and its required checks); the PR snapshot carries `rules`, `missingChecks` (required checks that did not report on the head) and `protected`. The first that applies: a required review (`reviewDecision` REVIEW_REQUIRED, or a `pull_request` rule while not approved) → "request a review … (gh pr edit --add-reviewer), or ask the owner to approve it"; a required check that did not report → "make the required checks run on this head"; `required_signatures` → "sign the commits … and push"; `required_linear_history` → "rebase on <base> … and push"; `required_deployments` → "deploy to the required environment, or ask the owner"; `merge_queue` → "ask the owner to add the PR to the merge queue on GitHub"; any other rule, classic protection, or nothing read → "ask the owner to check the rules, or to merge it on GitHub with admin rights". Every close carries its next step: the agent hears "Fyr did not ask the owner to merge <PR>: <reason>. Next: <step>; then ask again, and Fyr shows the ask when the PR can merge." (8) **A refused merge.** When a merge Fyr runs fails (the owner's yes or press, the PR tab, auto-merge), refused by GitHub ("Base branch policy prohibits the merge", a required status check) or by Fyr's last read (V0.97, V0.112), the PR's change gets the condition `mergeable` failed with "Merge refused: <the first line of the error>", so the card shows it and Stopped on a failure (V0.101) applies, and the agent hears once "The merge of <PR> failed: <error>. Next: <step>." (the step from the error's words and the rules Fyr read). A push to the head or the merge clears the condition. (9) **Merge as admin is left out.** It bypasses the owner's own branch rules, and Fyr would need to read whether this login may bypass them; the agent's message names the owner's way instead: merge it on GitHub with admin rights. (6) **The agent.** The `change_request` and `effect_request` descriptions and the system prompt say: ask "merge" right after you push; Fyr shows it to the owner when CI is green on that head and tells you if CI fails. Tests: `TestMergeAskOnPendingHeadIsHeld`, `TestHeldMergeAskOpensOnGreen`, `TestHeldMergeAskClosesOnRed`, `TestMergeAskOnRedOrGreenHead`, `TestBlockedMergeAskTellsTheAgentTheRule`, `TestRefusedMergeTellsTheAgent`, `TestFetchCountsChecks`, `TestFetchNamesBlockingRules`, `TestEffectMergeLoop`, `TestMergeBoundToHead`, `test/merge-wait.test.ts`. |
| V0.137 | → V0.86, V0.99, V0.119 (4), V0.121, V0.134, V0.135. **The threads that wait for a slot have their own section, numbered by their place in the queue, and the owner reorders the queue.** *Amends V0.86 (1) (first come, first served: now the owner's order, once set) and (6) (the queue shows in the list too), and V0.99 (the rail adds the section's count).* (1) **Section.** "Waiting for a slot" sits right after Working, on the desktop and the phone (one component, `web/src/slot-queue-ui.tsx`), and shows only while a host's queue is not empty. It lists every queued thread the list shows, in the queue's order; a queued thread leaves its own section for it, except one in Needs you, which stays there (the owner acts on it first) and keeps its number in the queue. A pinned queued thread is in this section, not in the pinned block, so the queue reads in its real order. The section has no collapse and no g key (g1 to g5 stay Needs you to Settled). The rail (desktop) and the strip (phone) show its item with the count only while threads wait; the strip stays one line with six items. The title's count is Needs you's, so it does not change. (2) **Rows.** Each thread's row is its section's ordinary row (V0.135) with its place, "#2", where the state icon stands; the second line keeps "waiting for a test slot (1 ahead)", which names the kind. The self-deploy's or the updater's priority wait is a row of its own on top, "#1 Fyr's own update · test", marked "first", and cannot be moved. Places count every waiter of the host (the priority waits and the threads a filter or search hides), so a hidden thread leaves a gap in the numbers. (3) **One order per host, not per kind.** Every kind takes from the host's one pool (V0.86 (1)), so the order is one list per host. With more than one host with a queue, the section has one part per host under its name (mono), in the front's host order. (4) **Reorder.** The one reorder control of V0.119 (4): on the desktop the place "#2" is the grip (drag, or Alt+↑/↓ on it; a press on it does not open the thread); on the phone a Reorder button on the section's header turns on a mode with ↑ and ↓ of 44 px beside each row (touch: no drag handle fits beside a swipe row and there is no hover). Every move shows on this device at once and sends `host.slotOrder {host, owners}`: the host's queued threads in the new order, a thread the list hides kept in its place. It is owner only (`From: ownerOnly`, with a receipt: the host's pool as it is now, which the page shows), no agent tool sends it, and the front routes it by `args.host` (V0.127 (2)). The host's pool (`slots.Pool.Reorder`) puts the listed owners that still wait first in the given order and the other waiters after them in their own order; priority waits stay ahead of all; an owner that no longer waits is ignored, so a page that read the queue a moment ago drops nobody; the order is stored with the pool (V0.86 (3)). The next free slot goes to the first in that order. An agent's "N ahead" answers the new place; a thread queued later joins at the end. A host without slots refuses it (409). (5) **Live.** The page reads the queue from `GET /api/load` (V0.80's 5 s poll, and at once after a reorder): a thread that gets its slot, releases its place or whose thread goes leaves the section, and the others renumber. Tests: `internal/slots/slots_test.go` (TestReorder), `internal/engine/slots_test.go` (TestV0137SlotOrderDecidesTheNextGrant: the reorder's receipt, an agent refused, the next slot to the thread moved first and only it told); `web/test/slot-queue.test.ts`; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.137: the section after Working, the places and the rail count, Alt+↑ sends `host.slotOrder` and the rows renumber, a granted and a released thread leave, no section without a queue, one part per host; phone: the strip in one line, the Reorder mode's 44 px ↑ and ↓, ↓ sends the order). |
| V0.138 | → V0.134, V0.109, V0.114. **A thread that opens clears the filters it fails, so its row is in the list and marked.** *Amends V0.134 (3) ("the owner clears it by hand": also this) and V0.109 ("a row that search or the filter hides stays hidden": the filters no longer hide it; search still does).* (1) When a thread opens, on the desktop or the phone, by any path (a row, a link such as "From thread" or ↳, a [Fyr] notice, `?open=`, back and forward, a board card, Needs you, a push tap, a new thread on another host or project), and the thread list's filters hide it, Fyr clears the filters it fails and keeps the others: a thread on lab under the host filter desk and the project filter acme/web, in acme/web, clears the host filter only. Its row then shows and is marked (V0.109; a closed section opens as before). A project filter whose project no longer exists filters nothing (V0.134), so it is not cleared. The cleared filter is cleared on this device as if the owner had cleared it (`fyr.hostFilter`, `fyr.projectFilter`). (2) **Once per opening.** It happens when a thread opens, not while it stays open: a filter the owner picks while a thread is open stays, and hides the open thread's row if it fails it, until another thread opens. (3) **Quiet projects (V0.114).** It happens only once the thread is admitted: a link to a quiet thread shows the gate and clears nothing; after the 30 s hold the thread opens and its failed filters clear. (5) Tests: `web/test/filters.test.ts` (clearFor: none, host only, project only, both, a removed project, a thread without a host, No project); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.138 desktop" and "V0.138 phone" (desk and acme/web, a link to host-b's t_lasso: the host filter cleared, the project filter kept, the row marked on the desktop and in the list after Back on the phone; a host picked while it is open stays; both filters failing clear both; a link to a quiet thread keeps the filter). |

A project does not move to another host."), a thread's header a host chip (the desktop's now as the phone's); the default host is the first in the owner's order (V0.132), never the host the browser is connected to; an offline host says so ("host-b is offline since 19:40. This project shows as last read and cannot be changed until it is back.") and its controls are off. (1) **New thread**: Host, Project, Folder, Provider. It starts on the project filter's project's host, else the first host in the owner's order; a remembered project on another host does not move it.
