| V0.141 | → V0.99, V0.118, V0.40. *Amended by V0.167: each host is two labelled meters ("ld", "job"), the same on both fronts; the rail's middle has no scrollbar and the rail is compact under 940 px.* **The desktop rail fits the window at any height; only its middle gives way.** *Amended by V0.152: the rail's middle is one card per host (the load as a share of the cores, a short host name, a slot meter with a word only when it matters) and a quota grid with provider initials.* *Amends V0.99 (where the rings sit) and V0.118 (1) (the shell's height).* *Cause:* the rail's fixed items alone (brand, five sections, List, bell, Changes, Settings, the host line) are about 730 px; the rail is a flex column whose items never shrink and which does not clip, so it overflowed its grid cell and the document scrolled. (1) **The shell** (`.shell`, V0.118) is the window's height (100dvh) and clips; the app's grid row is `minmax(0, 1fr)`; the document never scrolls, and the list, the thread and the rail each scroll on their own. (2) **The rail's middle** (`.rmid`: the load rings with their slot pips per host, then the quota lines) is its own scroll area between List and the bell. The sections and List above it and the bell, Changes, Settings and the host line below it keep their size and stay in view. *Why a scroll area and not a combined ring or one folded quota line:* nothing moves behind hover (hover cannot be reached from the keyboard or seen at a glance), it holds any number of hosts and providers, and at a normal height everything shows as before. (3) **Compact.** In the middle the rings sit side by side and the quota lines two to a row, where they fit (V0.99's ring, pips and tooltip are unchanged; V0.140's host name under each ring is cut to the ring's column, 36 px, with an ellipsis; more than two hosts, or pips wider than half the rail, wrap to the next row). A rail under 860 px tall drops the key hints under the sections and List (`g1`…`g5`, `\`; the tooltips still name them), tightens their padding, and puts a section's icon and count on one row with the name under them. So two hosts and four providers fit whole at 900 px and at 700 px, also with V0.137's sixth section (Slots, while threads wait), the bell and the install button. If even the fixed items do not fit (a window under about 620 px), the rail scrolls as a whole, without a visible bar. (4) **The phone** has no rail and does not use `.shell`; it does not change. Tests: `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.141, with `?mock=rail&hosts=2` (the mock adds lab's load and lab's Z.ai quota: two rings, four quota lines) at 1280 × 700 and 1280 × 900: the rail's first item (the brand) and last (the host line), every section, the bell, Changes and Settings are inside the window; the document does not scroll; the rail is the window's height and does not scroll as a whole; the two rings sit side by side; at 900 the middle shows everything and the key hints show; at 700 and 900 with the mock's Slots section; at 700 the hints are gone and the middle has room; scrolling the middle leaves the document where it was. V0.80's check of the load line now leaves out the middle that holds it. |
| V0.142 | → V0.80 (5)–(6), V0.86, V0.99 (1), decision 0029 (slice S1, #185). **Each host measures how slow a new job would run and what each heavy job costs, and shows it; admission does not change yet.** *Amends V0.80 (6) and V0.99 (1): the ring's colour.* (1) **Capacity.** A host reads what its agents may use: cores = the smallest of `agents/cpu.max`, the cpuset and the CPU count; memory = `agents/memory.high` (else `memory.max`), else 80 % of the RAM. (2) **Provider.** `cgroups` with V0.80's tree, `proc` on Linux without it, none without `/proc` (macOS for now): then every field below is absent and V0.86's fixed count and V0.99's ring hold as before. (3) **Slowdown.** fyrd runs `fyrd loadprobe` as a child in `agents/probe/` (with the tree; else in fyrd itself): every 5 s it times a ~5 ms CPU burst (wall ÷ the thread's CPU time, `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`). The slowdown s is the median of the last three (absent when the newest is older than 20 s), raised to what the host's own fit says the CPU pressure (`agents/cpu.pressure`, else `/proc/pressure/cpu`) means: per 10 % band of pressure the median of its last 60 probes, used from 10 probes. (4) **Memory.** Headroom is the share of the memory capacity left: MemAvailable over it, and with the tree also what `agents/` does not use, counted by its working set: `memory.current` less `inactive_file` from `memory.stat` (page cache the kernel drops first; *hotfix 17 Nov*: the raw `memory.current` fills to `memory.high` with cache and read 0 % free); PSI memory full avg10 gives the stall level: ok, elevated from 5 %, critical from 10 %. (5) **Job records.** A heavy command is recognised by pattern (the host setting `heavyCommands` `[{name, pattern}]`, `$1` the first group; the defaults cover make test/check/e2e/lint, `go test./...`, playwright, yarn and npm test/e2e/build, nix build, cargo, pytest; a quoted message or title (`-m`, `--message`, `--title`, `--body`) is not a run, a quoted script (`sh -c '…'`) is). The gate hook passes every Bash command (a subagent's too) to the host. Every 5 s the host follows the pool: at a grant it takes the thread cgroup's usage_usec, cpu.pressure total, memory.current and a `memory.peak` fd it resets; while held it reads them again; when the hold is gone it keeps a record: class (project + the newest heavy command seen in the hold, else the kind), wall time to the last reading while held, core-seconds less the thread's own baseline (its cores just before the grant, at most 0.25), mean cores, demand (cores ÷ the share of time the cgroup was not stalled, at most 4×), memory peak above the thread's memory at the grant, and the mean s. The last 200 records are stored (KV `slot-jobs`). (6) **API.** `GET /api/load` adds per host `provider`, `capacity {cpu, memory}`, `slowdown`, `headroom`, `memoryPressure`, `memoryStall`, `agentsCpuPressure` and `jobs` (per class, newest first: runs, the newest run's cores, demand and peak, the median wall time of the last ten); per thread `cpuPressure`, `peak` and `job` (the class its slot runs now). (7) **Shown.** The ring's colour follows s and the stall level where the host has a provider: warm from 1.5× or elevated, hot from 3× or critical (the figure stays the 1-minute load). The tooltip adds the slowdown, the agents' CPU pressure, the memory free of the capacity with the stall level, and the provider. A slot holder's row (desktop and phone, one component) says the job and its memory ("make test · 3.2 GB") in place of "test slot" once the gate hook saw its command, first on its second line (after the project), since that line is cut at its end; a holder without a matched command and a waiting thread keep V0.86's text at the end. Settings → Automation (desktop and phone) lists each host's job classes under Heavy jobs ("14 runs · 2.3 cores, would use 3.4 · peak 2.1 GB · 1 min 35 s"). (8) **Not yet:** admission by these numbers (S2, #186); per-job attribution without cgroups (the process tree) is left to a later slice; macOS. *Amended by V0.164: Settings → Automation → Heavy jobs also lists Undeclared now, Suggested heavy jobs, Heavy jobs you added and When a thread counts as heavy.* |
| V0.143 | → V0.7, V0.33, V0.90, V0.133, V0.21, V0.12. **Fyr notices an agent process that takes nothing it writes, says so with the reason, and restarts it on the same session; the runner reports a closed input, and agents keep no background Bash for hours.** **Cause, from its log and reproduced with claude 2.1.322:** the turn ended at 07:59:30 with background tasks running; the fyrd then installed (before V0.133) closed the CLI's stdin at its 10-minute idle stop. The CLI lived on for the tasks: its turn at 08:10 on a Monitor event had its Bash refused (V0.133's case), and it read nothing more. The owner's "Keep going" (08:19), "status?" (09:09), seven Stops (09:45) and the coordinator's message (09:46) went to the runner, which wrote them into the closed pipe and dropped the error; the protocol log shows them as `>>` because fyrd writes it on its side. The fyrds that adopted the process at 08:26 and 09:46 could not know the input was closed, so the thread said Starting. At 09:50:33 the CLI itself ended the last task, a Cloud SQL proxy started with Bash `run_in_background` and `timeout` 7200000 (the 2 h maximum; the script's own 4 h never counted); it then ran one turn of its own and exited on the closed stdin. Nobody killed it; the unread messages went to lost. With stdin open the same CLI reads a message (command_lifecycle queued and started within 0.1 s) and answers an interrupt at once while a background Bash runs: background work does not block the input; only the closed input did. (1) **The runner says when an input is closed.** Its process info has `inputClosed` (a close from fyrd or the runner's own end of an abandoned process); a write after it is logged as lost. A fyrd that adopts such a process takes it as closed. (2) **The watchdog.** Each message Fyr writes waits for the CLI's command_lifecycle report (queued, started or cancelled) for `AckTimeout`, 60 s (only once the process has reported lifecycle at all); a Stop waits for its control response for `ControlTimeout`, 20 s. A miss makes the process unresponsive. A message for a process whose input is closed is not written: it waits in the outbox, and the process is unresponsive at once when it still has background work (without, it ends in a moment). Unresponsive is a thread fact `unresponsive {since, at, reason}`: since is the process's last output, the reason says what it did not take and names the running background tasks. It puts the thread in Needs you (blocked, "Agent not responding since 08:58: …"), ahead of Working, with an `agent.unresponsive` line in the conversation. Output from the process clears it, unless the input is closed or a message still waits past its ack time. (3) **Recovery.** The owner's `thread.restart` ("Restart agent", available only while unresponsive), or Fyr by itself after `RestartAfter`, 10 minutes unresponsive: Fyr ends the process with its process group, so its background tasks end too (they are the CLI's children; a task that must outlive a restart runs detached, (5)), records `agent.restarted {why, tasks}` (the run ends without a failure, the tasks show stopped), starts the same session with --resume and writes the unread messages again from the outbox (which now keeps each message's text), the first with a [Fyr] note that says why and which tasks ended. They are not shown twice: only their delivery state moves. A process whose input was closed that ends by itself with messages unread does the same without a kill: the messages go to the next process instead of lost. Images and attachments of a message are not delivered again; its text is. (4) **The status line** above the composer, one component on desktop and phone (`web/src/agent-status.tsx`): while unresponsive it says "Not responding since 08:58 · <reason>" with the blocked icon instead of the spinner and "Starting", and a Restart agent button (44 px tall on the phone, touch). (5) **Prevention.** The prompt (V0.12) says to keep no background Bash for hours and to run a process that must stay up detached with a bounded lifetime and a pid file (`setsid nohup timeout 4h <cmd> > <log> 2>&1 & echo $! > <pidfile>`), watched with Monitor. The gate hook adds the same note as context (not a denial) to a Bash with `run_in_background` and a timeout of an hour or more. Tests: `internal/engine/unresponsive_test.go` (TestUnresponsiveAgentIsNamedAndRestarted, TestUnresponsiveAgentRestartsItself, TestUnansweredStopMarksUnresponsive, TestAdoptedClosedInputIsNotWrittenTo); the fake CLI's `deaf N` reads nothing after its first turn and keeps a background task N ms, as that CLI did; `internal/runner` TestListSaysInputClosed; `internal/core/unresponsive_test.go`; `internal/agent/prompts_v143_test.go`; `web/test/tasks.test.ts`, `web/test/conversation.test.ts`; the layout test V0.143 on desktop and phone. |
| V0.144 | → V0.86 (1), (3), (5), V0.142 (5), decision 0029 section 5, #174. **A heavy command runs only while its thread holds a slot; the agent is told how, and reminded to give an idle slot back.** *Amends V0.86 (5) and V0.142 (5)'s matching.*  (1) **Matching.** A heavy command counts only where the shell runs it: the command line is split at `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `$(`, a backtick and newlines, heredoc bodies are dropped, and each part loses its wrappers (an assignment, `timeout`, `time`, `nice`, `env`, `exec`, `command`, `npx`, `nix develop … -c`, `sh -c`, a quote); a pattern of `heavyCommands` must match at the start of a part. So `grep "make test"`, a sed script, a heredoc and a commit message are not runs. The defaults are make test…/check/e2e…, `go test./...` (any `./…/...`), playwright test, yarn test…/screenshot:diff/e2e…, `npm run test:…`/`e2e…`, nix build, cargo test, pytest, and (*amended 17 Nov*: the two jobs behind that afternoon's thrash matched none) `node …e2e….mjs` (a harness over many headless Chrome tabs, kind e2e), `yarn pre-push…` (kind test) and anything that starts headless Chrome or Chromium (kind e2e); `npm test` (3 s at the median) and `npm run build` (9 s) are not heavy by default. (2) **Gate.** At the gate hook (PreToolUse on Bash, a subagent's too), a heavy command of a thread that holds no slot is refused (`permissionDecision: deny`) with the reason: without a place in the queue "Heavy job (make test) without a slot: call mcp__fyr__slot_acquire {\"kind\":\"test\"} first, run it once you hold the slot, then call mcp__fyr__slot_release. …"; queued "Heavy job (…): you are queued for a test slot (N ahead) and hold none yet. Do not run it now; …". The kind follows the class: e2e for e2e, playwright, screenshot and layout runs, build for builds, else test. A thread's slot covers its subagents. Slots off (`slots: 0`) or the host setting `heavyGate: "off"` refuses nothing. (3) **Idle reminder.** A held slot of a running thread whose job tracker (V0.142) saw no heavy command and no reading above the thread's baseline + 0.25 cores for 5 min gets one notice per hold, at the agent's next tool call (no wake): "You hold a test slot but no heavy job ran in it for 5 min. If the job ended, call mcp__fyr__slot_release so another thread can run." The slot is not taken. (4) **Prompt.** Fyr's agent prompt names the heavy commands, the kind to ask for, that a subagent's or background run counts and the slot covers subagents, what to do while queued, to release as soon as the job ends, that Fyr refuses a heavy command without a slot, and what needs none (a single test file, `go test./pkg -run X`, a typecheck, a lint). |
| V0.145 | → V0.109, V0.138, V0.32. **On the desktop, Back and Forward move between the threads the owner opened.** *Amends V0.109 (2): each path there that opens a thread also adds a history entry.* (1) **One entry per opening.** A thread the owner opens on the desktop (a row click, a link such as "From thread", a notification, j/k and g1–g5 when they open the row, settle or archive opening the next row, a new thread) is one browser history entry, with the URL `?open=<id>` (other parameters stay; `dialog` goes). The same thread again adds none. Fyr's own openings add none: the first thread on load replaces the page's entry (keeping a deep link's `tab`), and the stream, the view refreshing and filters clearing open nothing. (2) **Tab.** A tab picked in the open thread (View, Conversation, Changes, PR) is written into its entry, so Back or Forward to it shows that tab. (3) **Back and Forward.** The mouse's side buttons (3 and 4), Alt+Left and Alt+Right and the browser's arrows are the browser's own and fire popstate, in a tab and in the installed window alike (Chrome handles the side buttons in the page, so no handler of Fyr's is needed; measured in headless Chrome with real button events). The entry's thread opens as by any path: the marked row follows (V0.109) and the filters it fails clear (V0.138). (4) **Gone.** A thread archived or deleted since is skipped in the direction of travel; below Fyr's first entry it is not skipped (that would leave Fyr) and the thread on screen stays. (5) **Not entries.** Settings, the board of changes and the other desktop dialogs add no entries (they are dialogs over the thread, as before); a preview viewer keeps its own entry (Back closes it). (6) **Phone.** The phone keeps its own history of screens and sheets (V0.32, nav.ts), unchanged: its back gesture works as before. A desktop entry is also a phone screen, so a window that narrows to a phone reads it. Tests: `web/test/desk-history.test.ts` (push, none, replace, the tab, the URL, a pop, a gone thread, the first entry's tab); layout e2e "V0.145 desktop" (A, B on another tab, C from the list: three entries and none while waiting; the mouse's Back button and history.back() give B then A; history.forward() gives B on its tab; the mouse's Forward button gives C). |
| V0.146 | → V0.86 (1), (2), (6), V0.99 (2), V0.137, V0.142, V0.144, decision 0029 (slice S2, #186). **A queued job starts when the slot budget, the slowdown and memory allow it, not when a count is free; one job always runs.** *Amended by V0.152: the rail's middle is one card per host (the load as a share of the cores, a short host name, a slot meter with a word only when it matters) and a quota grid with provider initials.* *Amends V0.86 (1) (the admission) and (6), V0.99 (2) (the pips).* (1) **Budget.** A host's pool has a budget of N cost units (`slots`, V0.86). A held slot costs 1.0, or 0.1 while it is idle: its job tracker (V0.142, V0.144) saw no heavy command and no reading above the thread's baseline + 0.25 cores for 3 min; the self-deploy's slot always costs 1.0. A new job costs 1.0 until S3 learns costs. (2) **Admission.** The queue keeps its order (V0.86, V0.137, the self-deploy first); only when its head may start changes. In order: without a provider (V0.142 (2); macOS for now) the fixed count of V0.86 holds; memory headroom under `slotMemoryReserve` (a share of the agents' memory, default 0.10) or a critical memory stall level holds every job (*hotfix 17 Nov*: not elevated, which reclaiming page cache at `memory.high` alone reaches; elevated still colours the ring); with no slot held the head starts (one job always runs); the held costs plus the head's must fit the budget; a head costing more than 0.25 waits while the slowdown is at or above `slotSlowdownMax` (default 1.5). Admission is asked at `slot_acquire`, at every release and at the engine's 10 s tick (the conditions change without a release). The self-deploy's priority wait goes through the same rules. (3) **Why.** The pool keeps why its head waits ("2.0 of 2.0 in use", "CPU: new jobs run 2.1× slower", "memory: 6 % free", "memory stalls (elevated)", "all 2 slots in use"); `GET /api/load` adds `slots.used` (the held costs summed), `slots.why` and each holder's `cost`. A queued `slot_acquire` at the head says it ("Queued for a test slot: 0 ahead. Waiting: CPU: new jobs run 2.1× slower. …"); the first waiting thread's row says it (desktop and phone, one component): "waiting for a test slot (0 ahead): CPU: new jobs run 2.1× slower". Since a row's line is cut at its end, V0.137's section Waiting for a slot also says it under each host's part, on both fronts: "The next waits: CPU: new jobs run 2.1× slower". (4) **Memory guard.** When the headroom falls under the reserve or the stall level is critical, the holder whose memory grew most since its grant gets one notice per episode at its next tool call: "Memory is short on this host (memory: 6 % free); your job grew 4.1 GB since its slot was granted, the most of the running jobs. New heavy jobs wait until memory recovers; nothing is stopped. …". Nothing is stopped or frozen (S5). (5) **Rail.** A host that admits by a budget shows a bar under its ring in place of the pips: the budget in use over N (full past it), "1.1/2" under it, the hot colour and "+n" while threads wait; the tooltip says "Slots: 1.1 of 2 in use (test 1.0, e2e 0.1 idle), 2 waiting; the next waits: …". A host without a provider keeps V0.99's pips. (6) **Not yet:** learned costs and backfill (S3, #187); predicted memory peaks in the headroom rule; the adaptive budget (S4). (7) **Test fix** (`TestLauncherRollsBackUnhealthy`): the health probe's report keeps the last /healthz answer over a later connection error (a build that answered 503 and then died reports the 503), and the test's ready deadline is 8 s, not 3 s. (8) **Matching fix** (V0.144 (1)): a quoted message or title ends at its line, so `--body "$(cat <<'EOF'` keeps its heredoc and the heavy commands named in the body are not runs (the gate refused a `gh pr create` whose body named `make test-go`). |
| V0.147 | → V0.80, V0.86, V0.90, V0.142, V0.144, V0.146, V0.39, V0.143. **fyrd's engine never waits on the job tracker in a cycle, a stall is found, logged and shown, a read never blocks on the slots, and a stuck engine restarts itself.** **Cause, from the SIGQUIT dump in daemon.log (1,333 goroutines, blocked 61 min):** a lock-order inversion. The engine's 10 s tick (`sweepSlots`, #174) held `e.mu` and asked the job tracker when a held slot was last active (`Jobs.Active`, which takes `Jobs.mu`). The sampler's 5 s tick (`Jobs.tick`, V0.142) held `Jobs.mu` and, for a hold that had just ended, asked the engine for the thread's project (`Jobs.end` → `Engine.ThreadProject`, which takes `e.mu`). After that, every `Engine.Apply` (agents' turns, hooks, the PR poller) and every `GET /api/load` (`Sampler.Get` → `Jobs.Classes`) waited forever. (1) **The fix.** The tracker calls nothing out of itself and does no I/O under its lock. `tick` finds the new holds under the lock, reads the cgroup files and opens `memory.peak` without it, applies the readings under it, and makes the ended holds' records after it (`finish`: the peak, the project, then the KV write). `sweepSlots` asks the tracker for the holders' activity before it takes `e.mu`, and reads the holders from its pool snapshot, not from `Pool.Holding`, under `e.mu`. The rule, written at `Engine.mu`: under it the engine calls no other package's locking method and no callback field. (2) **Reads from a snapshot.** `GET /api/load` takes no lock that another path holds across I/O or a call out. The pool's part is `Pool.View`: a fresh copy when the pool's lock is free, else the last copy. The tracker's part (classes, live jobs) is a view it publishes after each change. The machine's part is the last read while a read runs. `Sampler.Signals`, which admission (V0.146) asks under the pool's lock, reads the last published read (taken every 5 s by the tick) and does no I/O. (3) **Watched locks.** The locks of the engine, the job tracker, the sampler and the pool are `lockwatch.Mutex`: a mutex that records its holder (goroutine, function, file:line) and since when. (4) **Stall watchdog.** Every second fyrd looks at the watched locks and at every running request. A lock held, or a request running, past **10 s** (`lockwatch.StallAfter`) is a stall: fyrd writes the held locks and running requests (the longest first) and every goroutine's stack to `<data>/stalls/<time>.txt` (the newest 20 are kept), logs a line in daemon.log naming the holder and the file, and tells the owner. Once per holder. (5) **503.** Each request except the stream, `/mcp`, attachments, previews and update files has a deadline: **10 s** for GET and HEAD, **60 s** for other methods (a command may wait for its effect). Past it the client gets 503 with "Fyr did not answer in time: it is busy or stuck; the stall is logged". The handler stays watched until it returns, so its stall is dumped. (6) **Exit.** The engine's lock held past **2 min** (`lockwatch.ExitAfter`) writes one more dump and ends fyrd with code 70. The launcher restarts it (within a new build's watch window it rolls back, #148). The runner keeps the agent processes (V0.90), so no turn is lost. (7) **SIGQUIT** writes the same dump to `<data>/stalls`, also prints it to daemon.log as before, and exits 2. (8) **The owner sees it.** A stall is a host fact for 24 h: `stall {at, what, file, exiting}` in the state and the stream frame `stall`, kept under its own lock so it goes out while `e.mu` is stuck. A front lists each host's (`stalls`, with `host`). After a restart the newest dump of the last 24 h is shown, so an exit is told too. On both fronts a red line takes the update banner's place and style (one component, `web/src/stall-ui.tsx`; 44 px targets on the phone): "Fyr stalled at 12:03: a step took over 10 s", or "Fyr was stuck and restarted itself at 12:05; no turn was lost". A host's name is added when there are several hosts. Details shows the holder and the dump's path; × dismisses it on that device. (9) **Runner.** A CLI that reads nothing fills its stdin pipe. The runner wrote fyrd's lines on the link's only reader, so a full pipe stopped it from reading that link's `close`, `kill` and `ack` frames: V0.143's restart of an agent that takes nothing could hang in `Kill`. The writes now wait in a per-process queue with one writer goroutine. A failed write drops the rest of the queue and logs once. (10) **Audit** of the other locks for the same patterns. Clean: the agent manager (pure map work; buffered `select/default` sends), `claude.Client` (`amu`, `pmu`), the front (`Front.mu` publishes without blocking; `runMu` is always taken before `mu`; HTTP outside the lock), `stream.Hub` (drops a slow subscriber), the PR poller (no lock; non-blocking `Kick`; `Details.mu` released before the fetch), the quota service and the provider registry (never nested), the deployer (`deployMu` is taken before `Deployer.mu`; callbacks outside the lock), and every `e.mu` site (callbacks, `Hub.Send` and `notify` after the unlock). Views live under `e.mu`; there is no separate view store. Known, bounded, left as is: SQLite writes under `e.mu`, the pool's lock and the quota lock (one connection, never taken in the reverse order); `Engine.press` held across awaited effects (each has its own timeout, and (9) removes the hang under them). (11) **Test fix** (`TestContinueAfterForcedRestart`, a data race also on main): the test put its fake agents in place after the settle and the archive, whose effects read them on their own goroutines; they are now in place first, and the waits are bounded (5 s for the send, 100 ms for a second); `-race -count=50` passes (it failed 2 of 50 before). `TestReportPending` (deploy) read its fake host's reports without the lock after a fixed 50 ms sleep; it now reads them under the lock, waiting up to 5 s; `-race -count=50` passes. Tests: `internal/engine/lockorder_test.go` (TestV147LoadSlotsAndTicksDoNotDeadlock: engine, pool, tracker and sampler wired as fyrd wires them, with ticks, load reads, thread reads, acquire and release running at once for 1.5 s, which fails on a hang with every stack; it hung before the fix with the dump's two stacks. TestV147StallIsToldWhileTheEngineIsStuck); `internal/cgroup/lockorder_test.go` (a `Project` that asks the tracker back; `/api/load` and `Signals` while the pool's and the tracker's locks are held); `internal/lockwatch` (the holder named; one dump per stall; the exit); `internal/server/deadline_test.go` (503 at the deadline, watched until it returns, no deadline on the stream); `internal/runner` TestV147KillReachesAProcessThatReadsNothing (800 KB to `sleep`, then a kill; it hung before); `web/test/stall.test.ts`. |
| V0.148 | → V0.146, V0.142 (5), V0.144, decision 0029 (slice S3, #187). **Each kind of heavy job gets a learned cost, and a cheap or short job may start ahead of a head that does not fit, without delaying it.** *Amends V0.146 (1) (costs) and (2) (backfill), V0.142 (7) (Settings).* (1) **Learning.** Each finished record (V0.142 (5)) teaches its class (project + heavy command): demand in cores as an exponential average (α 0.3, half when the run was starved: demand over 3× its cores), the memory peak as a slowly decaying maximum (max of the new peak and 0.9 × the old), and the last ten wall times. The table holds raw cores, bytes and seconds per host (KV `slot-costs`) and survives a restart. (2) **Cost.** A class's cost on a host is the larger of demand ÷ (the agents' cores ÷ N) and peak ÷ (the agents' memory ÷ N), bounded 0.1–2.0; under three runs never below the prior 1.0; computed at admission from the host's capacity (V0.142 (1)), so it holds on any host. A class not learned costs 1.0. A held slot costs the larger of its class's cost and what it uses now (its cores over the last reading and its memory growth since the grant, by the same formula); an idle hold 0.1 (V0.146 (1)); the self-deploy 1.0. (3) **The job's class before it runs.** `slot_acquire` takes an optional `command` ("the command you will run"); the gate's refused heavy command (V0.144 (2)) names it too; a named class counts for 30 min while the thread waits; else the class is the slot's kind. The prompt asks for `{kind, command}`. (4) **Backfill (EASY).** When the head does not fit, a later job in the queue starts if it fits now by every rule of V0.146 (2) and either costs at most 0.25 or its class's p90 wall time ends by the earliest expected end of the running jobs (each holder's grant + its class's p90; a holder without an estimate allows no such promise). A head is passed at most 3 times, and never once it has waited 10 min. Backfill runs at `slot_acquire` (the job may start at once), at a release and at the tick; the queue's order does not change. (5) **Heavier than predicted.** When a running job's cost now is more than 0.25 above its prediction, its thread gets one notice per hold at its next tool call: "Your job (fyr: make test) uses 1.6 of the slot budget; Fyr expected 1.0. Other heavy jobs wait until it ends; nothing is stopped. Fyr learns the new cost from this run." Its higher cost holds new admissions by itself. (6) **Settings.** Automation → Heavy jobs (desktop and phone) adds each learned class's cost ("cost 1.7 · learned from 14 runs", or "cost 1.0 · 2 of 3 runs to learn") and a **Reset** button, the owner command `host.jobReset {class}` (owner only, with a receipt): the class is forgotten and costs 1.0 until learned again. (7) **Test fix** (`TestQuestionPendingAcrossStrayAndUpdate`, internal/runner): the runner's Abandon in the test is 2 s, not 50 ms (the update's Detach→Attach gap outlasted 50 ms at load 50, and the janitor ended the process), and the linked phase waits Abandon + 300 ms; the test server's log closes at cleanup (a process exiting after the test logged through the finished test). |
| V0.149 | → V0.60, V0.68, V0.139, decision 0018. **A command the agent wants the owner to run is a Run card: the full command, the host, why, and Run, Run as root, Copy and Not now.** *Amends V0.60 (1), (3), (5) (a run request is a thread action) and V0.68 (1) (the card is the confirmation).* (1) **The tool.** `run_request {command, why, root?, cwd?, timeoutSec?, id?}` declares a *run request*: a thread action (`on: thread`, `run: script`) with `request: true`, the shell text `command` (at most 8 000 characters), `why` (one line, at most 200) and `root`. Fyr runs it as `bash -euo pipefail -c <command>` in `cwd` (default the thread's folder), on the thread's host (V0.139), with the time limit of V0.60 (2) (default 600 s). Several commands are one request, one script. `root: true` runs the whole script as root through `pkexec`; a command with `sudo` in it is refused with that advice ("Drop sudo and pass root: true"). The id defaults to `run-<n>`; the same id replaces the request. The digest covers `command`, `why`, `root` and the folder. (2) **The card**, the same component on the desktop and the phone, in the conversation tab under the newest message, newest last: the label "Run on <host>" (the thread's host, V0.139; "Run" when the client does not know it) and `why`; the full command in monospace, wrapped at any character, never scrolled sideways; a Nix store path shortened in the middle (`/nix/store/prc4…-name`), the full text on a press (a tap on the phone, a click or Enter on the desktop); one muted line with the folder (V0.68 (3)) and "as root" for a root request. (3) **Buttons.** **Run** (a request without `root`), or **Run as root** (with it); **Copy** (the full command; a root request's as `sudo <command>`, or `sudo bash -euo pipefail -c '<script>'` for several lines); **Not now**. Off (dimmed, with the reason) while it runs. The card is the confirmation: a press runs what it shows, with no second dialog, and the press is the command `action.run` with the digest (V0.60 (3)) and its receipt. While it runs: **Stop** (`action.stop {on, action}`), and the output streams into the card (the newest 4 KB, at most once a second). After it: the exit code and the output, folded after 12 lines; Run again, and Not now becomes **Close**. (4) **Root.** Run as root needs a polkit authentication agent of the owner's session on the thread's host: the host reads it from its processes (a process of the owner's uid whose name has `polkit` and `agent` and is not `polkit-agent-helper`, or one of `lxpolkit`, `hyprpolkitagent`) and `pkexec` on its PATH, at most every 30 s, and the thread says `rootPrompt` (why not) only when it cannot. Then Run as root is off, and the card says it plainly: "No password prompt can show on <host>: <why>. Copy the command and run it in a terminal there." When it can, the card says "The password prompt shows on <host>'s screen." On the phone that line is the same; the prompt is answered at the machine. Fyr never stores or asks for a password. (5) **Needs you.** A thread with a request that never ran, and no run in progress of the agent, is in Needs you (state blocked) with the reason "Run: <why>"; the row shows it. (6) **Results.** Every press and result is an `action` event (the conversation line of V0.60 (4)) carrying the command and whether it ran as root, so the thread's log records both. The agent's `[Fyr]` notice of a request's result names the exit code and adds the output's last 1 500 characters in a code block. Stop ends the run as failed with "stopped by the owner". Not now removes the request and tells the agent "The owner chose Not now for <why>; do not ask again unless they ask"; Close on a run request removes it quietly. (7) **The prompt rule.** Fyr's prompt: never ask the owner to type or paste a command in prose; call `run_request`, one script for several commands with `set -e`, `root: true` instead of `sudo`. The PreToolUse gate (V0.12) denies a Bash call that runs `sudo` (it waits for a password nobody can type here) and names `run_request` with `root: true`; `sudo -n`, which never asks, passes. (8) **Prose commands.** An agent message with a fenced block whose lines start with `! ` (Claude Code's convention), or a shell block (`sh`, `bash`, `shell`, `console`, `zsh`) after a line that asks the owner to run it ("run this", "please run", "can you run", "you need to run"), still renders the block as a Run card with Copy only (no Run: the agent did not declare it), and the agent gets one `[Fyr]` notice at its next step: "You asked the owner to run a command in your message. Call mcp__fyr__run_request …". Tests: `internal/core/runrequest_test.go` (the request, Needs you, Stop, Not now, the agent's notices, prose commands, the sudo gate); `internal/engine/runrequest_test.go` (the tool, the streamed output, Stop, pkexec's argv, the polkit agent from the processes); `internal/agent/prompts_v149_test.go`; `web/test/runcard.test.ts`; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` (V0.149 desktop and phone: the card wraps, no sideways scroll). |
| V0.150 | → V0.142 (2)–(4), (8), V0.146 (2), decision 0029 section 0 (c). **A Mac measures its load too, so its slots admit by the budget, the slowdown and memory like a Linux host.** *Amends V0.142 (2) and (8) and V0.146 (2): macOS is no longer a host without a provider.* (1) **Provider.** Without `/proc` on macOS the provider is `sysctl` (x/sys/unix, no cgo). Another OS without `/proc` still has none and keeps V0.86's fixed count. (2) **Capacity.** Cores = the CPU count; memory = 80 % of `hw.memsize`. (3) **Memory.** Headroom is the available memory over the capacity: the kernel's own available share (`kern.memorystatus_level`, the figure `memory_pressure` prints) of `hw.memsize`. macOS keeps few pages free and compresses instead, so the free pages alone would hold every job while the kernel sees no pressure; without that sysctl the free, speculative and purgeable pages and the file cache are used. The stall level is the kernel's memory pressure level (`kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level`): 1 ok, 2 elevated, 4 critical. (4) **Slowdown.** The probe of V0.142 (3) runs in fyrd itself (no tree), with the thread's CPU clock `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`, which macOS has. (5) **Load.** The 1, 5 and 15 minute load comes from `vm.loadavg`, so V0.99's ring figure is the Mac's (it read 0 before). (6) **Not yet:** per-thread CPU and memory and job records on a Mac (the process tree, a later slice); the rows keep "no per-thread figures". Tests: `internal/cgroup/host_darwin_test.go` (run on macOS: the provider, the capacity, the headroom, the stall level, the load and a burst; the `vm.loadavg` layout and the pressure levels). |
| V0.151 | → V0.146 (4), V0.148, V0.80 (2), decision 0029 (slice S5, #189). **Under the memory guard Fyr pauses the newest heavy job (freezes it, never kills it) until memory recovers.** *Amends V0.146 (4) (the guard only told).* (1) **The job's own cgroup.** Every 5 s the host puts each slot holder's heavy job in `agents/<thread>/job/`: the topmost processes of the thread's cgroup whose command line holds the text of the thread's newest heavy command (V0.144 (1); from the gate or `slot_acquire`'s `command`), and every process descended from them; the processes they start later are born there. A thread's cgroup enables no controllers for its children, so the job's cgroup sits beside the agent CLI. The command is never rewritten: a rewrite at the gate would need an "allow" decision, which would skip the CLI's own permission check (auto mode) of the whole command. When the slot goes back, the job's cgroup is thawed and removed once empty; a thread's Release removes it first. Without cgroups nothing is moved and nothing is frozen (the guard only tells, as V0.146 (4)). (2) **Freeze.** While the memory headroom is under the reserve or the stall level is critical, the newest slotted job (by its grant) is frozen (`cgroup.freeze` 1): never the oldest running job, never the self-deploy, one at a time, each hold at most once. Its thread gets, at its next tool call: "Fyr paused your heavy job: memory is short on this host (memory: 6 % free). Its processes are frozen, not killed, and resume when memory has recovered for 30 s, within 10 min at most. A timeout inside the job may fire after it resumes; check its result." (3) **Thaw.** The frozen job thaws when the headroom is at least the reserve + 5 % and the stall level is neither elevated nor critical for 30 s, after 10 min at most, or when its slot went back; its thread is told "Your heavy job runs again: Fyr thawed it." (4) **Shown.** `GET /api/load` adds per thread `paused`; the row of a paused holder (desktop and phone, one component) starts its line with "yarn build · paused: memory" (or "test slot · paused: memory"). (5) **Not in this slice:** job records from the job's own cgroup (they stay the thread's, V0.142 (5)); a freeze on hosts without cgroups. *Amended by V0.164: undeclared work is paused first (section 7 of that row); a job that asked is frozen only when no undeclared work with memory over is left.* |
| V0.152 | → V0.99, V0.140, V0.141, V0.146 (5), V0.44. *Amended by V0.166: the phone shows the load too, a strip in the inbox header and a sheet.* *Amended by V0.167: each host is two labelled meters ("ld", "job"), the same on both fronts; the rail's middle has no scrollbar and the rail is compact under 940 px.* **The rail's middle reads at a glance: one card per host, the load as a share of the cores, a slot meter that says what it means, and a quota grid with provider initials.** *Amends V0.99 (1) (the figure in the ring) and (2) (the pips' "+n"), V0.140 (1) (the name under the ring), V0.141 (3) (the middle's layout) and V0.146 (5) (the bar's "1.1/2" and "+n").* What was wrong: both hosts read "home-…"; "2.0/2+1" was cramped, cryptic and red with no reason given; the rings showed "19" (the load average on 32 cores) beside "0.2" (on 8); the slot pips did not read as their host's; the quota dots had no provider. (1) **One card per host**, centred, two to a row where they fit: the ring, the host's short name, the slot meter. (2) **The figure** is the 1-minute load as a share of the cores, on the arc's scale ("59%", "130%" past the cores, the "%" set smaller), so hosts of any size read alike; the ring's colour rule is V0.99's and decision 0029's, unchanged. The tooltip keeps the three load averages and the cores; the screen reader's label is "desk: load 10.4 on 8 cores (130% of the cores), …". A host that reports no cores shows the load as before. (3) **The short name** (with several hosts) drops the parts all names share at the start or the end ("home-laptop", "home-server" → "laptop", "server"; "a.lan", "b.lan" → "a", "b"); when that leaves a name empty or two alike, each keeps its full name. It is cut to the card's 34 px with an ellipsis, and the full name shows on hover. (4) **The slot meter** stays under its host's ring: V0.146's thin bar for a host that admits by a budget, V0.99's pips otherwise. A word shows under the bar only when it matters: "full" when the budget is used and nobody waits, "1 waits" ("2 wait") while jobs are held back, in the hot colour then and only then; the "1.1/2" figure and the "+n" go. The meter has its own tooltip: "Slots for heavy jobs (tests, builds, e2e runs): 2.0 of 2 in use (test 1.0, e2e 1.0), 1 waiting; the next waits: …". (5) **Quotas** are one 2-column grid under a short line, each a provider's initial in its colour (on a 20 % wash of it) and its value, right-aligned with tabular figures; red only from 80 % (V0.44's rule). Initials tell providers apart: the name's first letter; where two different names share it, the first letter of a later word no other name starts with ("Claude", "Claude (work account)" → C, W), else the shortest start that differs ("Codex", "Claude" → Co, Cl); one provider on two hosts keeps one initial, and with several hosts the tooltip says whose ("Claude on lab 5 h 12 % · …"). (6) **Height**: the middle stays as tall as before (96 px for two hosts and four providers in the mock), so V0.141's fit holds. The phone shows no load and lists quotas by name (Settings), so it does not change. Tests: `web/test/load.test.ts` (the share figure, short host names), `web/test/admit.test.ts` (the meter's word and tooltip), `web/test/quota.test.ts` (initials), `web/test/host-display.test.ts` (the label); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.152 with `?mock=rail&hosts=2` at 1280 × 1000 (light and dark) and 1280 × 700: the figures are shares, the cards name desk and lab with the full name on hover, each meter has its tooltip, "1 waits" is hot and only on the host where a job waits, the quotas are a 2 × 2 grid with C, W, C, Z, red exactly from 80 %, tooltips name provider and host, the middle is centred and no wider than the rail, and at 1000 px it does not scroll; V0.80's checks read "130%" and "1 waits". |
| V0.153 | → V0.143, V0.146, V0.148, V0.144, V0.80, decision 0029. **On a thrashing host Fyr waits for the machine: it kills no agent CLI for being slow, retries a start with backoff, holds the automatic restart, and the thread says "Waiting for the machine (memory pressure)" instead of "Could not start the agent".** (coordinator, 17 Nov, live evidence 14:04–14:11: load 46–59 on 4 cores, memory PSI full avg60 22 %, 14 GB swap, agents/ at memory.max 82k times with no oom_kill; three runs died "signal: killed", two restarts failed "initialize: context deadline exceeded (signal: killed)".) **Who killed what, from the journal and the runner's log:** the three running agents (t_b27e705cd7b59bee 14:04:23, t_46e354958b1a26c9 14:05:48 and 14:11:08) were killed by systemd-oomd, which the host's `agent-budget.nix` sets on fyr.service (`ManagedOOMMemoryPressure=kill`, limit 50 % for 20 s); it kills a whole agents/<thread> cgroup with SIGKILL, so the kernel counts no oom_kill. It took the cgroups with the most reclaim, which were the memory users: t_b27e705cd7b59bee ran the layout e2e harness (`node test/_only.e2e.mjs`, a filtered copy of the layout test) and t_46e354958b1a26c9 acme's `yarn pre-push:check`, both without a slot. The two restarts of t_b27e705cd7b59bee (started 14:04:51 and 14:07:30, killed 14:05:51 and 14:08:30) were killed by Fyr itself: the 60 s initialize deadline in agent.start, which kills the CLI that did not answer. The V0.143 watchdog did not act (it logs every mark and restart; there is none). (1) **The signal.** `slots.Thrashing(signals, policy)`: memory stall at the critical level (PSI full avg10 ≥ 10 %), or the probe's slowdown at 2 × SlowdownMax or more (3× by default); it names memory pressure or CPU pressure. fyrd gives it to the agent manager from the sampler that admission reads. (2) **Start.** The initialize deadline (`InitTimeout`, 60 s) holds on a calm host as before. Past it under pressure Fyr waits on, up to `PressureMaxWait` (15 min) more, and the thread has `machineWait {since, why}`. A start that still fails under pressure is tried again with backoff (InitTimeout/2, doubling, at most 2 min) within PressureMaxWait, then fails as before. (3) **The watchdog (V0.143)** under pressure: a message not acknowledged within AckTimeout is looked at again after AckTimeout, up to PressureMaxWait, before the process counts as unresponsive; a Stop not answered marks nothing; the automatic restart waits while the host thrashes and comes when it is calm. The owner's Restart stays. (4) **The thread.** machineWait puts an idle thread in Working as waiting, reason "Waiting for the machine (memory pressure)", and the status line above the composer (desktop and phone, one component) says "Waiting for the machine (memory pressure) · since 14:05". It ends with the start, the end of the wait, or the process. Not done here: the owner's message shows in the conversation only once the start succeeds (as before); the oomd limit is the host's setting (50 % for 20 s on fyr.service, while the agents slice has 80 %), and Fyr does not change it. **Admission (decision 0029):** V0.148's rules would have refused a new slot at those numbers (a critical stall and memory headroom under 10 % each hold every job), but neither heavy job asked: V0.144's heavy-command patterns match neither `node test/*.e2e.mjs` nor `yarn pre-push:check`, so both ran without a slot. That is the slots thread's to close. Tests: `internal/engine/pressure_test.go` (TestSlowStartWaitsUnderPressure, TestSlowStartFailsOnCalmHost, TestFailedStartRetriesUnderPressure, TestNoAutoRestartUnderPressure; the fake answers initialize after the ms in FYR_FAKE_INIT_DELAY_FILE); `internal/slots/thrash_test.go`; `web/test/tasks.test.ts`. |
| V0.154 | → V0.146 (1), (5), V0.148, V0.151, decision 0029 (slice S4, #188). **The slot budget adapts slowly to the host: it grows while jobs wait on a calm host and shrinks when new jobs run slow or memory is short.** *Amends V0.146 (1) (the budget was N).* (decision 0029 section 3: "AIMD-style adapting of the budget from PSI".) (1) **Step.** At most every 30 s (the engine's tick): with memory short (headroom under the reserve, or a critical stall) the budget becomes 0.75 × itself; after a slowdown above 3× held for 60 s, the same; while a job waits, the slowdown is under 1.2, memory is neither short nor elevated, and nothing was admitted for 60 s, it grows by 0.25. Bounds 1.0–2 × N; a host without a provider keeps N. It starts at N after a restart. *Elevated* alone does not cut: reclaiming page cache at `memory.high` reaches it with plenty free (the V0.146 hotfix). (2) **Admission** uses the budget in place of N (V0.146 (2), V0.148 (2)); a change admits what now fits at once. (3) **Shown.** `GET /api/load` adds `slots.budget` (absent while it is N) and `slots.budgetWhy` ("the host is calm and jobs wait", "new jobs run 3.5× slower", "memory is short"). The rail's bar reads "2.1/2.5"; its tooltip adds "Budget 2.5 (N 2): …"; a queued thread's reason names the budget ("2.5 of 2.5 in use"). |
| V0.155 | → V0.14, V0.51, V0.123, V0.124, V0.129, V0.130, V0.131, decision 0030 (slice 0, no behaviour change). **The authentication chain has a test harness, and today's ways in are pinned by it.** (1) **Harness** (`internal/authtest`). | V0.156 | → V0.14, V0.47, V0.51, V0.129, V0.130, V0.155, decision 0030 (slice 1). **Every hop names who acts: the principal travels as `Fyr-Actor`, and a host guards nothing by the browser's headers behind a proxy.** *Amends V0.14 and V0.51 (the host's guard on what a front forwards) and V0.130 (3) (the host's Serve mark and the Host rewrite for the hop).* (1) **Principal** (`internal/authn`): `Principal{Kind, ID, How, Front}`, kinds `device`, `local`, `install` (and `agent`, which never crosses a hop); hows `local`, `tailnet`, `code`, `link`, `legacy`. The wire form is `Fyr-Actor: v=1; kind=…; id=…; how=…[; front=…]`, parsed strictly (a repeated or unknown field, an unknown kind or how, an id over 64 plain characters, is malformed). (2) **The front** makes the principal per request: the device of the session; no session in observe mode is `device/legacy/legacy` and counted (V0.130); `forward(p, …)` has no form without it. It drops `Cookie`, `Authorization`, `Tailscale-*`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded`, `Origin`, `Referer`, `Sec-Fetch-*` and the browser's `Host` (the host's own name goes instead), and sets `Fyr-Actor` and `Fyr-Origin` (the public origin the browser used). Its own calls are `kind=install`. A browser-facing listener answers `400` to `Fyr-Actor` and `Fyr-Origin`. (3) **The host** reads them on the in-process pipe (`PipeHandler`, no browser guard, no `/mcp`; missing or malformed is `401`) and on the tailnet listener (`PeerHandler`, no `/mcp`; a front of an older build sends none and is still served as `legacy` and counted by `LegacyFronts`). A preview's CSP takes the origin from `Fyr-Origin` there. The tailnet listener answers `403` to a request with `Origin` and to a `Host` that is not an address, a `ts.net` name or a name of this node (DNS rebinding). The host's loopback main port is unchanged and ignores both headers. (4) **Audit**: an owner command's `command` event carries `actor` (`device:<id>`, `install:<id>`) and `front`; `Input.Actor`, `Input.Front`. (5) **`serve.sock`** takes a connection only from root (tailscaled); config `serveSocketUsers` adds users (the harness's emulator). Nothing the owner uses stops working: the old ways still serve in observe mode, `owner.sock` is untouched, existing cookies are used as they are (decision 0030 5.1). *Tests:* `TestHeaderRoundTrip`, `TestParseRefuses`, `TestStrip`, `TestContext` (authn), `TestPeerHandlerRefusesABrowserAndAForeignHost`, `TestPeerHandlerServesALegacyFront`, `TestPipeHandlerNeedsFyrActor`, `TestPipeHandlerOrigin` (server), `TestHopCarriesThePrincipalOnEveryRoute` (front: every route of the mux, through loopback and the Serve emulator, against a recording host), `TestFyrActorOnABrowserListenerIs400`, `TestServeSocketClosedToAnotherUser`, `TestCommandEventNamesTheDevice`, `TestNewFrontFollowsAnOlderHost`, `TestNoSessionIsTheLegacyPrincipalOnTheHop`, and `TestMigrationKeepsTheOwnerSignedIn` step 1. |
| V0.157 | → V0.14, V0.19, V0.119, V0.125, V0.130, V0.155, V0.156, decision 0030 (slice 2). **Sessions decide: the browser way in has a stored mode, a tailnet node is looked up, a device is added by a link or a code, and Settings → Devices lists and revokes.** *Amends V0.130 (observe only).* (1) **Modes.** Each way in (`browser`, `peer`) has a stored mode, `observe` or `enforce`, in the database; no release changes one. In observe a request with no session is served as the `legacy` principal and counted; in enforce it is `401 {signedOut}`, a navigation gets the sign-in page, and only the client's files, `/auth/*`, `/.well-known/fyr` and `GET /api/previews/…` are served without one. `peer` is stored and cannot enforce before slice 5. In enforce a `Tailscale-*` header off serve.sock is `400` and a changing call needs `Origin`. (2) **Rule 3 (Q2).** The install moves `browser` to enforce by itself after 3 days in observe with no would-refuse and a sign-in of each way in use, never one that was set back to observe by hand; it pushes every device, writes an audit entry that names `fyr auth observe`, and Settings → Devices shows a notice for 7 days. A mode otherwise moves by a signed-in device or the owner socket. (3) **`fyr auth status|observe|enforce`**, **`fyr device add|approve|revoke|list`** over the owner socket, which no mode gates; `fyr device add` prints a QR code (`internal/qr`, byte mode, level M, versions 1–6; checked against the standard's published Reed-Solomon and format values, not by a scanner). (4) **Tailnet node.** whois of `X-Forwarded-For`: a tagged node, a node shared in and another user's get nothing; a blocked node is `403`; an approved node gets a session in silence; a new node gets a session in observe and its approval, in enforce a waiting session with a code, except the first node of an install with no device (Q4). A V0.130 session that comes through Serve in observe has its node approved (the phone keeps working when enforce comes). (5) **Links and codes.** A link is 128 bits, once, 60 s (`fyr open`) or 10 min (`fyr device add`, Add a device). A waiting browser asks for a code (`POST /auth/code`): 8 characters, 40 bits, 5 minutes, used once, five wrong tries in five minutes end every open code, ten requests a minute; approving needs a session older than the one approved, or the owner socket. (6) **Revoke** (a device or `fyr device revoke`): the session, the devices it added and theirs, their tailnet nodes blocked, their open streams closed within a second, their push subscriptions dropped; a revoked browser reloads into the sign-in page. (7) **Before the cookie**: an `Origin` that is not the front's own, and a credentialed `/api/` call that is not same-origin by `Sec-Fetch-Site`, are `403`. (8) **Settings → Devices** on the desktop and the phone, one page (`devices-ui.tsx`): the devices with how they got in and when last used, Revoke (Sign out for this device), the waiting ones with Allow and Deny, Approve a code, Add a device with a link and a QR image, and Require sign-in. | V0.158 | → V0.51, V0.123, V0.124, V0.130, V0.156, V0.157, decision 0030 (slice 3), 0026 (the key and id). **Installs link by keys: a signed roster, a pinned TLS link, and installs of one tailnet user pair by themselves.** *Amends V0.123 (a candidate is followed when its key is in the roster) and V0.124 (3) (a changed id is a different key, so a different install).* (1) **Roster** (`internal/roster`, `<data>/roster.json`): a grow-only set of statements `install-add`, `device-add`, `device-revoke`, each signed with a member's Ed25519 key; members are this install and everyone a member added, to a fixpoint (order does not matter; a statement by a non-member counts for nothing; a revoke wins for ever); the install is its own first member. (2) **The peer port** (config `peerListen`, NixOS `services.fyr.peerPort` and `openFirewall`; `internal/peerlink`): TLS 1.3 only, ALPN `fyr-peer/1`, a self-signed certificate over the install key both ways, no 0-RTT; the dialer requires the exact install id, the listener learns the key and asks the roster. Open to any key: `/.well-known/fyr` (`{fyr, id}`) and `/pair/tailnet`; for members: `/roster` and the host protocol with `Fyr-Actor` required (`Server.LinkHandler`), **no `/mcp`**. (3) **Condition 1** (`internal/peer`): the same tailnet user id, untagged on both sides, not shared in, key not expired, and `GET https://<MagicDNS>:<Serve port>/.well-known/fyr` over WebPKI returns the install id and peer port; the link is dialed pinned to that id; the other side runs the same check on this node and its Serve must name the key on the connection; both sign the other's add. Never: another user, a shared-in node, a tagged node, an expired node, a node with a peer port but no Serve. A member vouches by sync (a learns c through b). (4) **Devices spread**: every signed-in device is a `device-add`, a revoke is a `device-revoke`; the issuing front ends the session when the roster says revoked, and every host refuses the id in `Fyr-Actor` (`401`) whichever front forwards it. (5) **Fronts prefer the peer link** to a member and keep the whois link as the fallback when the peer link cannot be made; a host with no peer link yet is followed as before; the fallback is counted (`fyr host list`). (6) **The drive switch** (3.7): a member reads every host and drives it unless `fyr host drive ID off` on that host (local, never in the roster): a call that changes things from that install is `403`. (7) **Config** `peerEverySeconds`, `resolveFile` (host:port or host → address, read at each dial) and `rootCAs` (extra roots for the Serve probe), for a network with no MagicDNS and for the harness; `/.well-known/fyr` gains `peer`. *Tests:* `internal/roster` (`TestPairingIsMutual`, `TestAMemberVouches`, `TestMergeOrderDoesNotMatter`, `TestStrangersAndForgeriesCountForNothing`, `TestDeviceRevokeWinsForEver`, `TestRosterIsKeptAcrossARestart`, `TestSetAddressSpreads`), `internal/peerlink` (`TestLinkPinsTheServerAndLearnsTheClient`, `TestWrongKeyFailsTheHandshake`, `TestOnlyTLS13`), `internal/peer` `TestCondition1`, `internal/server` `TestLinkHandler`, `internal/front` `TestLinkTransportPrefersThePeerLinkAndFallsBack`, `internal/authtest`: `TestTwoInstallsOfOneUserPairAndFollowEachOther`, `TestNodesThatDoNotPair`, `TestARevokedDeviceIsRefusedAtAnotherInstallThroughAThird`, `TestPeerPortRefusesStrangersAndWrongKeys`, `TestTheDriveSwitch`, `TestMigrationKeepsTheOwnerSignedIn` step 3. |
| V0.159 | → V0.130, V0.157, V0.158, decision 0030 (slice 4), decision 0026 (the key and id). **The owner's invite pairs what a tailnet cannot (a tagged install); the owner's login travels in the roster; Settings → Hosts shows the installs, the nodes that are not paired and why, and Pair and Add.** (1) **Invite** (`internal/peer/invite.go`): `fyr1-<install id>-<10 characters>` (50-bit secret), 10 minutes, single use, five wrong tries end it, a new one ends the old; `fyr pair` (and Hosts → Pair on a signed-in device) opens it and shows the text and a QR code; `fyr pair CODE [-addr host:port]` (and Hosts → Add) uses it: the id is pinned from the code, the secret goes inside the pinned channel (`POST /pair/invite`), the inviter learns the joiner's key from the handshake, both sign each other's add; a relay with its own key fails the handshake and never sees the secret. (2) **Vouching**: a member's add is sync, so a third install learns the new member with no press, and says so (`Vouched for by <member>`; `fyr host list`). (3) **The owner's login in the roster** (`owner-login`): an untagged install says whose it is; a tagged install (which has none) serves no tailnet browser until paired and then takes it from the roster; config `ownerLogin` still wins. (4) **Hosts** (desktop and phone, one panel `peers-ui.tsx`): the installs with how each came in, the tailnet nodes that are not members with why (a tag says who may reach a node, not whose it is; another user's; shared in; expired; waiting for its Serve), Pair (invite as text, QR image) and Add (code, optional address); `fyr host list` shows the same. | V0.160 | → V0.157, V0.158, V0.159, decision 0030 (slice 5). **An install can be removed for ever, a key can be rotated, trust-widening needs an old session, the peer way in can enforce, and the old link ends.** (1) **Removal** (`install-remove`, `fyr host remove`, Hosts → Remove): the install is removed with every key it rotated to; what it signed counts for nothing; the members only it vouched for are re-signed by the remover in the same batch; the roster computes the same members in any merge order; the removed install's open requests close within a second, a front drops a host reached only through it, a removed install serves nothing and pairs with no one. (2) **Rotation** (`key-rotate`, `fyr rotate-key`): signed by the old key; the new key waits in `identity.key.next` and takes over at the restart (exit 75, as for a release); the new certificate carries the rotation chain, so a peer that was offline follows it and merges it; the old key is refused. A key that may be stolen is removed and paired again. (3) **Step-up** (`stepUpMinutes`, default 5): approve, Add a device, Pair and Add need a session that old; the owner socket never. (4) **`peer` enforceable**: by hand, by a signed-in device, or by itself after 3 clean days when every followed host is on a pinned link (`Front.AllOnPeerLink`) and a request has come over it; the whois listener and the loopback host port are then removed (not started), a device no member signed in and the legacy principal are 401 on the peer port, and `fyr auth observe peer` restores the listeners; the stored mode survives a restart. (5) **`fyrd selftest`** loads the install key, the roster and the device table. *Tests:* `internal/roster` (`TestRemovalVoidsWhatTheRemovedSignedAndTheRemoverKeepsTheRest`, `TestRemovalSpreadsInAnyOrder`, `TestRemovedThenPairedAgainWithANewKey`, `TestRotationIsFollowedByAPeerThatWasOffline`, `TestAForgedRotationMovesNobody`), `internal/auth` (`TestPeerCanBeMovedByHand`, `TestPeerEnforcesItselfOnlyWhenEveryHostIsOnThePinnedLink`), `internal/front` `TestAllOnPeerLinkAndDropPeers`, `internal/server` `TestLinkHandler`, `internal/authtest`: `TestRemovingACompromisedInstall`, `TestARotatedKeyIsFollowedByAPeerThatWasOffline`, `TestPeerEnforceRemovesTheOldListenersAndObserveRestoresThem`, `TestSelftestReadsTheKeyTheRosterAndTheDevices`, `TestAFreshSessionCannotWidenTrust`, `TestMigrationKeepsTheOwnerSignedIn` step 5; web: the layout screen `settings paired installs (V0.159)` now carries Remove. |
| V0.162 | → V0.104, V0.106, 13.1 (⇧E). **A bulk settle skips a pinned thread: no settle, no unpin, and the toast says how many it kept.** *Amends V0.104 (3) and V0.106 (3).* (1) **Paths.** Every settle of several threads skips the pinned ones: the desktop's ⇧E (the picked rows, else the section of the marked row), the phone's "Settle merged" in Seen, and the phone's selection bar "Settle N". Desktop and phone go through one function (`settleBulk`, with `bulkSettle` in `store.ts`). No other path settles several threads: the board's Done marks a change done and settles no thread, and the host has no settle sweep. (2) **Host guard.** `thread.settle` with `now: true` (the bulk form) on a pinned thread is refused with "A pinned thread settles only by its own Settle"; nothing changes, and a running pinned thread gets no "settle when done". (3) **Toast.** "Settled 7 threads; 1 pinned thread kept"; "Nothing settled; 1 pinned thread kept" when only pinned threads were picked. Undo covers the settled threads. (4) **Phone button.** "Settle merged" shows while at least one merged thread in Seen is not pinned. (5) **Unchanged.** The owner's Settle on the thread itself (button, `e`, row menu, swipe, the thread sheet) still settles a pinned thread and removes the pin; unpin still lets it go. |
| V0.163 | → V0.137, V0.142, V0.146, V0.148, V0.151, V0.152, V0.154, decision 0029. *Amended by V0.166: the phone shows the load too, a strip in the inbox header and a sheet.* **Heavy jobs are a share of the host, CPU and memory apart, not slots: a job starts when its CPU fits the free CPU and its memory the free memory, and everything the owner and the agents see is a percentage.** *Amends V0.137 (the section's name), V0.142 (7) (the row), V0.146 (1)–(3), (5) (one cost, the bar), V0.148 (2), (4)–(6) (costs, backfill, Settings), V0.152 (the meter) and V0.154 (one budget).* (1) **Two needs.** A job needs CPU (its class's learned demand, cores) and memory (its class's learned peak, bytes): the measures `slot-costs` already holds, used as they are. A class not learned, or learned from under 3 runs, needs at least the prior: 1/N of the host in each (N is the host's `slots` setting, default 2: half the host); a learned need is bounded 2 %–100 % of the host per resource. A running job needs at least what it uses now; an idle hold (V0.146) 5 % CPU and the memory it holds; the self-deploy the prior. (2) **Admission** (V0.146 (2), replaced): without measures (no provider or no capacity: a host that counts jobs) the fixed count N; memory headroom under the reserve or a critical stall holds every job; with no job admitted the head starts (one job always runs); the head's CPU must fit the CPU budget less the admitted jobs' CPU, and its memory the memory budget less their memory, and no more than the real headroom above the reserve; a job that is not cheap (above 12.5 % of the host in either resource) waits while new jobs run 1.5× slower. The reason names the resource: "needs ≈ 45 % CPU, 30 % free", "needs ≈ 2.6 GB memory, 1.8 GB free". EASY backfill (V0.148 (4)) and the owner's order (V0.137) are unchanged; a cheap job is one under 12.5 % in both. (3) **Budget per resource** (V0.154, amended): AIMD moves a CPU factor and a memory factor (1 is all the host gives its agents; between 1/N and 2). Memory short (headroom under the reserve, or a critical stall) cuts the memory factor by a quarter; new jobs running over 3× slower for 60 s cut the CPU factor; on a calm host with a job waiting and nothing admitted for 60 s, the factor of the resource that holds the head back grows by 12.5 %. S5's freeze (V0.151) is unchanged. (4) **API.** `GET /api/load` names the pool `heavy` (the old `slots` stays one release, the same object). Per host: `cpuPct`, `memPct` (in use), `cpuBudgetPct`, `memBudgetPct`, `cpuFreePct`, `memFreePct`, `usedPct` and `tight` (the tighter resource's use of its budget), `binding` (what holds the head back), `needPct`; a host that counts jobs sends `usedPct` only. Per job: `need {cpu, mem}`, `cpuPct`, `memPct`, `pct` (the larger). Per class: `cpuPct`, `memPct`. The old `used`, `budget` and `cost` (one number in units of N) stay one release. (5) **Agents.** `slot_acquire` and `slot_release` keep their names: prompts, the gate, hooks and habits name them, and two names for one action would double the list. Their answers speak percent: "Admitted: your test job ≈ 45 % CPU, 15 % memory of home-laptop; 20 % CPU and 60 % memory left. …", "Queued: your e2e job needs 45 % CPU, 15 % memory of home-laptop; … free (0 ahead). Waiting: needs ≈ 45 % CPU, 30 % free. …", "Released: … free for heavy jobs.". The prompt line, the gate's refusal ("Heavy job (make test) not admitted: call mcp__fyr__slot_acquire …; Fyr admits it when its share of home-laptop fits.") and the notices ("Your test job is admitted now", "uses ≈ 80 % of this host; Fyr expected ≈ 50 %", "Your admitted test job ran nothing heavy for 5 min …") say no slot. (6) **Shown** (desktop and phone, shared components). The rail card's meter (V0.152) is a bar of the tighter resource's use of its budget with its percentage under it ("62 %", "+2" in the hot colour while jobs wait); no pips or counts; its tooltip: "Heavy jobs: CPU 60 % · memory 62 % of this host in use; the budget is CPU 100 % · memory 100 %", each running job's CPU and memory, and why the next waits. A host that counts jobs shows its share of the count ("50 %"). A job's row: "make test-go ≈ 50 %" (or "build job ≈ 46 %"); the queue's head: "waiting: needs ≈ 45 % CPU, 40 % free"; the others "waiting (1 ahead) · needs ≈ 20 %". V0.137's section is named "Waiting to run" ("Waiting" on the phone strip), with "The next waits: …" in the same words. Settings → Heavy jobs: a **Budget** row ("CPU 100 % · memory 100 % of what this host gives its agents (6 cores, 13.0 GB)", and why AIMD moved it) and per class "≈ 50 % CPU · 15 % memory of this host · learned from 5 runs". The self-deploy's progress says "waiting for room to run the tests". |
| V0.164 | → V0.163, V0.151, V0.148 (5), V0.144, V0.142, decision 0029 section 5 (the option not chosen then). **A thread that runs heavy work without asking is seen from its cgroup, its agent is told once, its use counts against the host, and under real memory pressure it is paused, never killed.** *Amends decision 0029 Q2 (work that did not ask now counts), V0.151 (2) (what is paused first) and V0.142 (7) (Settings).* (1) **Idle level.** Per thread the host keeps the median of its quiet readings of the last 10 min (every 5 s: cores, and the working set, `memory.current` less `inactive_file`); a reading is quiet when the thread is not admitted and is under its idle level + half a threshold; the first reading seeds it. (2) **Flag.** Thresholds are host settings (the config file's `undeclared`, `undeclaredCpu`, `undeclaredMemory`, `undeclaredAfter`, and what the owner saves in Settings through `host.undeclaredSettings`, which wins), shares of what the host gives its agents: `undeclaredCpu` 0.25, `undeclaredMemory` 0.08, `undeclaredAfter` 45 s (30–120), `undeclared: "off"` turns it off. A thread that is not admitted is flagged when, over the window cut in three parts, its mean cores above the idle level reach the CPU threshold in every part, or its lowest working set above the idle level reaches the memory threshold. The episode ends when both are under half their threshold for the window, the thread is admitted or its CLI ends. (3) **Command.** The processes of the cgroup started in the episode whose parent is older (never the CLI or what it kept running) are the job's roots; the heaviest root's command is the thread's newest Bash command found in its command line, else that command line, cut to 300 characters. Ended episodes are kept (KV `undeclared`, 100). (4) **Suggestions.** A command that matches no heavy pattern becomes a suggestion (its program and first argument, escaped; seen how often, where, how large); the owner adds it (`host.heavySuggestion`, into the host setting `heavyAdded`, read with the patterns, so the gate refuses it from then on), ignores it for good, or removes an added one (`host.heavyRemove`). (5) **Told once.** The agent gets one notice per episode and at most one per run, at its next tool call, never a wake: "This thread runs heavy work Fyr did not admit: about 2.8 GB memory (20 % of desk) and 2.1 cores (52 %) for 45 s, from `…`. Before a heavy job call mcp__fyr__slot_acquire {…}, … Fyr counts this use against the host either way, so other jobs wait; nothing is stopped, but if memory runs short Fyr may pause it."; a subagent's first Bash call of the episode carries it too. (6) **Counted.** A flagged thread's use (mean cores over the window, working set now, both above the idle level) is added to the used CPU and memory of admission (V0.163), not to the number of running jobs; a thread's own use does not count against its own request; the wait reason names it ("needs ≈ 45 % CPU, 20 % free (Map perf uses 52 % undeclared)"); `GET /api/load` adds `heavy.undeclared` and the two shares. (7) **Paused.** Only while memory is short (V0.151 (2)'s condition): the newest flagged thread whose memory is over has its roots and their descendants moved to `agents/<thread>/undeclared/` and frozen, before any job that asked; one thing frozen per host, each episode once, not when no other heavy work runs, never the CLI; it thaws by V0.151 (3)'s rule or when the episode ends. Its agent is told as in V0.151. (8) **Shown** (desktop and phone, one component each): the row's line starts "undeclared · 2.8 GB · 2.1 cores" (or "undeclared · paused: memory") in the beam ink; the rail's meter draws the undeclared share hatched, with a count under it that opens the thread or a menu of them (desktop only: the phone shows no host load); the thread has a strip with the use, the command, "counted against desk's heavy jobs", when the agent was told, Add as heavy job… and Dismiss; Settings → Automation → Heavy jobs lists Undeclared now, Suggested heavy jobs, Heavy jobs you added and the thresholds. Dismiss (`host.undeclaredDismiss`, owner only) hides the mark for the episode; the use still counts and may still be paused. (9) **Without cgroups** (Linux without the tree, macOS): the use is the process tree under the CLI (`/proc`, `ps`); (1)–(6) and (8) hold; nothing is frozen. (`cgroup.TreeReader`; the CLI pids come from the agent manager.) (10) **Not flagged:** an admitted thread above its learned cost (V0.148 (5)); the self-deploy; the probe; fyrd's own Chrome. |
| V0.165 | → V0.19, V0.37, V0.47, V0.54, V0.18, V0.32. **Previews are usable at any size on desktop and phone: every one opens full screen, an image zooms, an HTML page and the live view zoom by steps; the app around them is static.** *Amends V0.19 (the viewer), V0.47 (the frame's own Full screen button and Open), V0.37 (the live view's header) and V0.18 (a sent image's viewer); V0.54 unchanged (everything still loads from the thread's host).* (1) **Full screen.** Every preview card and the live view has one Full screen button (⛶, 44 px on the phone). The browser's Fullscreen API is used where it exists (desktop, Android Chrome); without it (iPhone Safari, the iOS home-screen app) an in-app layer covers the screen. Either way the layer is one history entry: Escape, Close and Back (the phone's back gesture) leave it, and leaving the browser's full screen leaves the layer. (2) **Images** (preview images and images the owner sent): fitted and never cut off; pinch, the wheel and a trackpad pinch zoom about the fingers or the pointer (1 to 8 × fitted); a drag pans, clamped so no empty edge shows; a double tap or double click toggles fit and 100 % (the image's own pixels; 2.5 × when it is no bigger than fitted); swipe or ←/→ move between the images of the thread (of the message, for sent ones). One viewer for both, shared by desktop and phone. (3) **HTML previews and the live view** go full screen in place: the same frame, so the page does not reload and the live view's bridge and state stay. The frame keeps V0.47's height in the card (its own report, else 16:10, at most 600 px, Show all up to 2,000 px). In full screen the page zooms by steps (50 % to 300 %: it lays out at width ÷ zoom and is scaled), which leaves the sandbox (no allow-same-origin) and the preview CSP as they are; a page may also handle its own pinch. The frame's own Full screen button and the Open button are gone. (4) **Files.** A sent PDF or other file stays an Open chip (a new tab; the phone hands it to its own viewer); an inline PDF viewer would need the attachments' `sandbox` CSP loosened, so it is not done. (5) **Thumbnails** stay small and lazy (`loading=lazy`, `decoding=async`); the viewer loads the full image only when opened. (6) **Static app.** The viewport has `maximum-scale=1, user-scalable=no`; `html, body` have `touch-action: pan-x pan-y` (no pinch or double-tap zoom) and `overscroll-behavior: none`; iOS's `gesturestart`/`gesturechange` are cancelled (Safari ignores `user-scalable=no`); on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`) every field is at least 16 px, so iOS does not zoom on focus. Only the image viewer and the full-screen page zoom. A conversation entry that holds a full-screen card drops `content-visibility` (its paint containment would trap the fixed layer). **Accessibility:** the desktop's browser zoom (Ctrl +/−) still zooms the whole app, and the system text size applies where the browser honours it; pinch zoom of the app's chrome on a phone is given up, as the owner asked. (7) **Tests.** `zoom.test.ts` (zoom about a point, clamps, toggle, double tap, wheel, steps); layout sections `V0.165 fullscreen`, `image zoom`, `live view` (desktop and phone) and `static chrome phone`: each way out of full screen, with and without the Fullscreen API; pinch, wheel, ctrl+wheel, drag and double tap change the image's scale while the page's scale (read from the browser, not the scripted visualViewport) stays 1; the live view's frame is the same element in and after full screen; a pinch over the chrome does not zoom the page; no field under 16 px. |
| V0.166 | → V0.99, V0.152, V0.163, V0.164, V0.103, V0.113, V0.140. *Amended by V0.167: each host is two labelled meters ("ld", "job"), the same on both fronts; the rail's middle has no scrollbar and the rail is compact under 940 px.* **The phone shows each host's load: a strip in the inbox header and, on a tap, a sheet with what the desktop rail's tooltips say.** *Amends V0.99 (3) ("the phone shows no machine load"), V0.152 (6) ("the phone shows no load") and V0.163 (the heavy-jobs use is shown on the phone too).* (1) **Strip.** One button in the phone's inbox header, after the front's name and before the bell, 44 px high (touch): per host the rail's ring (V0.152: the load as a share of the cores, coloured by V0.99's and decision 0029's rule) at 26 px, with beside it the host's short name (V0.152 (3), with several hosts) and the heavy jobs' use (V0.163, the tighter of CPU and memory, "95%"), in the hot colour with "+n" while jobs wait. The same components and figures as the rail (`LoadRing`, `railMeter`, `railBudget`, `shortHostNames` in `web/src/load-ui.tsx` and `load.ts`); the ring's and the bar's CSS rules are shared. Two hosts show, those with a load first in the owner's host order (V0.132); more are "+n". An offline host shows as an empty ring and "off" when it fits, else in "+n"; the button's label names every host and the offline ones as offline. A host that reports no load while online (older than V0.80) is left out. With one host, its ring and its use, without a name. With several, the front's connection keeps its dot and writes its name only for the screen reader and on hover: a 360 px header has no room for both. The strip never wraps, scrolls or grows the header, and it is not zoomable (the app's UI is not). (2) **Sheet** ("Host load", a tap on the strip; Back closes it): per host, in the owner's order, the ring, the full name, "Load 59% of the cores" or "Offline since 14:07; no load until it is back", the heavy jobs' bar (V0.163, with V0.164's undeclared part hatched), then the rail's tooltip lines as text: the load averages, the slowdown, the pressures, memory, the agents' use, "Heavy jobs: CPU … · memory … of this host in use; the budget is …", the running jobs with their share, who waits and why, and the undeclared work (V0.164) with its threads. Under them, the provider quotas (`QuotaList`, as in New thread). (3) **Not changed:** the desktop rail; the section Waiting for a slot. Tests: `web/test/load.test.ts` (`stripHosts`: the owner's order, offline hosts, a host without load left out); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.166 phone at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640 with `?mock=rail&hosts=2`: the strip is a 44 px button inside the header with nothing past the edge, it shows desk and lab and "+1" for the offline host-b, the connection keeps its dot only, each card's figure and heavy-jobs % equal the desktop rail's for the same mock state, a tap opens the sheet with desk, host-b (Offline) and lab, the Heavy jobs, Running and waiting lines, the same figure as the rail and four quota lines inside the screen, and Back closes it. |
