| V0.167 | → V0.99, V0.141, V0.152, V0.163, V0.166. **Each host's load and heavy jobs are two labelled meters, the same on the desktop rail and the phone; the rail's middle shows no scrollbar and cuts nothing.** *Amends V0.99 (1) (the ring goes), V0.141 (3) (the compact rail starts under 940 px; the middle's layout), V0.152 (1)–(5) (the card and the quota grid) and V0.166 (1)–(2) (the strip's and the sheet's host).* What was wrong: a native scrollbar with arrows showed on the middle and ate its width, so the 2 × 2 quota grid was cut on both sides; the jobs bar's "45 %" sat under the bar, apart from it, and read like the ring's "14 %"; a hairline rule and uneven gaps. (1) **Two meters per host** (`HostMeters`, `web/src/load-ui.tsx`), one component on the desktop rail, the phone's strip and its sheet: the host's name (with several hosts; the short name of V0.152 (3), the full name on hover), then a row "ld" with a bar in the load colour (V0.99's and decision 0029's rule) and the load as a share of the cores ("130%", V0.152 (2)), and a row "job" with a blue bar and the heavy jobs' use (V0.163, the tighter of CPU and memory; V0.164's undeclared part hatched), red with "+n" while jobs wait. The labels stay quiet; only a hot value turns red. An offline host is its name and "offline". The tooltip (desktop) and the sheet (phone) start with the legend: "ld: the load, % of the cores (1 min) · job: heavy jobs' use of their budget (the tighter of CPU and memory)". The ring is gone. (2) **The rail's middle** stacks the hosts, 8 px apart, then the quotas 10 px below, with no rule. It shows no scrollbar (`scrollbar-width: none`); it scrolls by wheel or touch only when even the compact rail is short. The quotas (a provider initial and the value, red from 80 %, V0.152 (5)) wrap two to a row only where two fit, measured by the browser, so none is cut. (3) **Compact sooner:** the rail is compact under 940 px of height (860 before), so at 1280 × 900 two hosts and four providers show whole with no scroll. (4) **Phone:** the strip shows the same two meters per host (two hosts, then "+n"); the sheet shows the legend, then per host its name with its meters, and the rail's lines. Tests: `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.167 at 1280 × 900, light and dark, with `?mock=rail&hosts=2`: no element of the middle sticks out of the rail, no scrollbar is drawn and nothing scrolls, each host is "ld" and "job" with the legend first in its tooltip, the parts are at least 8 px apart with no rule; V0.80, V0.141, V0.152, V0.164 and V0.166 read the meters (V0.141: the hosts stack, and at 900 px the rail is compact and its middle shows everything). |
| V0.168 | → V0.126, #153, decision 0027. **A host reads `deployable` hourly, not every minute, and a Mac reads the releases with the same token file as a NixOS host.** *Amends #153's poll.* (1) **Poll.** The watcher reads `deployable` every `updateEveryMinutes` (default 60). A release it sees waits its quiet period (`updateQuietMinutes`, default 10) as before; the watcher wakes when that period ends instead of at the next poll, so a release is installed 10 minutes after it is first seen, at most an hour after it is published. A failed read is logged once, then hourly. (2) **Token.** `~/.config/fyr/github.env` (`GH_TOKEN=…`, mode 0600) holds a fine-grained token that may only read the repository's contents. A NixOS host loads it with `EnvironmentFile=-` on `fyr.service`; `install-macos.sh` starts the launchd job through `/bin/sh`, which loads the same file if it is there and then execs the launcher. Without it, `gh`'s own login is used. Tests: `internal/deploy` TestWatcherWakesWhenTheQuietEnds (hourly by default; due at the end of the quiet period, not before). |
| V0.169 | → V0.134, V0.138, V0.100, V0.152, V0.132. **The host filter is its own row: a switch with one segment per host, each turned off or on by itself; the project row follows from it.** *Amended by V0.204: a small pill per host, and a click on the last host on ends the filter.* *Amends V0.134 (1) (one host picked: now hosts turned off, any number), (2) (each group counted the threads the other let through: now row 1 counts every host's threads and row 2 only the threads on the hosts that are on), (3) (the empty state's words; Clear filter turns every host on) and (5) (one row: now two); V0.138 (1) (a failed host filter cleared: now the thread's host comes back on).* On the desktop and the phone, the one component (`FilterRow` in `web/src/inbox.tsx`; the rules in `web/src/filters.ts`). (1) **Row 1, hosts.** Under the search field (the desktop) or the section strip (the phone), a switch across the list with one segment per host in the owner's host order (V0.132): the host's dot from Settings → Hosts, its short name (V0.152) and how many of its threads are in view. Every host is on by default, which is no host filter. A click turns a host off: its threads leave the list, and its segment goes grey, its name struck through, its count replaced by "hidden"; a click turns it back on. The last host on cannot be turned off (its tooltip says to turn another one on first), so the list is never emptied by the hosts alone. Row 1 counts each host's threads in view whatever the project filter is: the rows are separate, and row 1 comes first. (2) **Row 2, projects,** as in V0.134, counted only over the threads on the hosts that are on: the projects whose threads in view are all on hosts turned off fall out of it. One project is picked at a time; a click on the picked one, or the × at the start of the row, clears it. (3) **Only when there is a choice.** A row shows only with more than one value in view, or while one of its values is selected: with one host, no row 1; with one project in view, no row 2 (V0.134 (2); folded sections are still not considered). A host turned off stays in row 1, off, when its last thread leaves or is folded away, and when the front no longer lists it (after the listed hosts, by name), as a picked project stays (V0.134 (3)). (4) **Empty state** (V0.134 (3)): "No threads in view for fyr.", "No threads in view with server hidden.", or "No threads in view for fyr with server hidden.", the folded count, and Clear filter, which turns every host on and clears the project. (5) **Opening a thread** (V0.138) clears per row: a thread on a host that is off turns that host back on, and the other hosts stay as they are; a project it is not in is cleared. A host turned off while a thread is open stays off. (6) **Remembered per device** as `fyr.hostsOff` (a list of names); V0.134's `fyr.hostFilter` (one host picked) is dropped at load, so every host is on after the update. (7) **Sizes.** Desktop segments 28 px high, the phone's 36 px (touch); the row scrolls sideways without a visible scrollbar when the hosts do not fit; the phone stays static (V0.165). The phone menu's "Filter by project…" sheet is unchanged. Tests: `web/test/filters.test.ts` (the toggles, the fall-out of row 2, kept selections, the last host on, the stored list, clearFor per row); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.169 desktop" and "V0.169 phone" (no row 1 with one host; the switch in order above row 2 and inside the screen, 36 px on the phone; host-b off: its threads gone, acme/ops out of row 2, remembered, desk the last host on; the empty state with desk hidden and Clear filter; the old key dropped) and "V0.138 desktop/phone" (a link to a thread on a host turned off turns it back on). |
| V0.170 | → V0.54, V0.139, V0.140, V0.152, V0.167, V0.169, V0.135, V0.132, V0.124. **Each host has a mark, a circle with the glyph of the machine it runs on in its own colour, and the mark shows the host wherever a host is shown: on every thread row, in the host switch, in the thread header, in the load meters, in the Host field and in Settings → Hosts.** *Amends V0.54 (the phone row names its host in words: now its mark, on both fronts; an offline host still says so in words), V0.135 (1) (the row's start: the host's mark, then the project's icon, with more than one host), V0.169 (1) (the segment's dot: now the mark) and V0.124 (Settings → Hosts' dot: now the mark).* This is slice 1: the mark is automatic. Slice 2 lets the owner set a host's icon and colour in Settings → Hosts. (1) **The machine.** Each host says what it runs on, in its state and through the front in `hosts[]`: `machine` is `laptop` (it has a battery that powers the system: Linux `/sys/class/power_supply/*` of type Battery and not scope Device; macOS `pmset -g batt` lists an InternalBattery), else `desktop` on macOS and Windows, else `server`; `os` is Go's name for its system. A host older than V0.170, or one not seen yet, has none and draws as a server. (2) **The mark.** A circle (hosts; projects are squares) with the Lucide glyph of the machine (laptop, monitor, server) in the host's colour on a tint of it. The colour is one of the project palette's ten hues (never amber), picked from the host's install id (its name without one): the hosts are taken in the order of their ids, and a host whose hue another has already got takes the next free one, so a front's hosts differ while hues are left, every front gives a host the same colour, and a renamed host keeps it. An offline host's mark has a small ring at its corner. Its tooltip is the host's full name, and "· offline" when it is. (3) **Rows.** With more than one host, every thread row, on the desktop and the phone (`web/src/row-ui.tsx`), shows its host's mark first, then the project's icon, inside the title's first-line indent: the title keeps its two lines. The phone's line 2 no longer says "desk ·" (the mark says it); "<host> offline" stays, since it is state. With one host there is no mark. (4) **Everywhere else, the same mark:** the host switch's segments (V0.169), the thread header's host chip and its offline chip (V0.139, both fronts), the rail's and the phone strip's load meters before the short name (V0.167), the Host field of New project and New thread beside the list (a native list draws no icons), and Settings → Hosts (24 px, in place of the dot; a changed id keeps its "Needs you" pill). On a front with one host, Settings → Hosts draws this install's mark from its own state's `machine`. Tests: `internal/machine/machine_test.go` (the kind from the OS and a battery; Linux power supplies, a mouse's battery not counted; macOS pmset); `internal/front/machine_test.go` (TestV170HostsCarryTheirMachine); `web/test/host-mark.test.ts` (glyphs, decoding, colours stable across fronts and renames and distinct, twelve hosts, this install's own machine); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.170 desktop/phone" (no mark with one host; every row's mark named and apart from the project icon with a two-line title; no host words on the phone, an offline row still says so; the switch's marks; the header chip; Settings → Hosts' glyphs and four colours; the load meters; the Host field), and V0.54 and V0.169 read the row's host from its mark. |
| V0.171 | → V0.170, V0.132, V0.124, decision 0031. **The owner sets a host's icon and colour in Settings → Hosts → the host; the mark shows them everywhere.** *Amends V0.170 (2) (the mark is automatic: now the owner's icon and colour come first).* Slice 2 of the host marks. (1) **Where.** A host's own page in Settings → Hosts has a Mark group after Identity (so a changed id and its Trust stay first), the same on the desktop and the phone: the mark at 32 px with "Automatic: a laptop (it has a battery), a colour from its id." or "Set by you."; Icon, a grid of Automatic and nine glyphs (laptop, monitor, server, house, cloud, cpu, database, terminal, box) and a field for one emoji (empty is Automatic again); Colour, Automatic ("A") and the project palette's ten hues. On the phone each control is at least 44 px. Each pick saves at once. A host without an install id (a Fyr older than V0.124) shows its automatic mark and "its mark cannot be set until its next update". (2) **Keyed by install id** (decision 0031): `front.hostMark {id, icon, color}` (owner only) sets one host's mark; empty icon and colour together give the automatic mark back. As the host order (V0.132), the marks are the owner's, not a front's: the front writes the whole set, stamped now, to every host it follows (`host.hostMarks {marks, at}`, kv `hostMarks`, newest wins, owner only with a receipt), keeps a copy (kv `front.hostMarks`), and every front reads the newest from its hosts; a host that was offline gets it when it is back. A front's state has `hostMarks` (`{id: {icon, color}}`) and its stream the event `hostMarks` when they change. An icon is a glyph name or one emoji (at most 8 code points); a colour a palette name; anything else is refused, and a client ignores a value it does not know. (3) **The mark** (V0.170) draws the owner's glyph or emoji on the owner's colour; the colours the owner set are taken first, so the automatic colours of the other hosts avoid them while hues are left. Tests: `internal/engine/hostmarks_test.go` (newest wins, bad marks refused, a restart); `internal/front/hostmarks_test.go` (TestV171OwnerSetsAHostMark: set on one front, kept on every host, shown on the other front, an offline host catches up, only a colour, cleared, refusals); `web/test/host-mark.test.ts` (the owner's glyph, emoji and colour, unknown values, owner colours kept free, the icon list); `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.171 desktop/phone" (the automatic mark; house, teal, 🍓 and Automatic each send front.hostMark with host-b's id and the mark follows; the owner's mark on rows and in the switch, no other host teal; old-nuc without an id cannot be set). |
| V0.172 | → decision 0031 (slice 1), V0.51, V0.54, V0.85, V0.121, V0.124, V0.127, V0.132, V0.134, V0.139, V0.140, V0.169, decision 0030 (3.2–3.4). **The front speaks both: a host's calls by its install id in the path, what belongs to no host under `/api/front/`, the old routes unchanged, marked and counted.** *No client change: the client still uses the old routes until slice 2.* (1) **`/api/h/<hostId>/<the host protocol's path after /api/>`**: one handler. It authenticates the browser as every route does, finds the host by install id (`hosts[].id`, never the name), strips the prefix and forwards the call with `Fyr-Actor` over that host's link; it does not read the body, buffers no upload and knows no host route by name, so a route added to the host protocol needs no change in the front. A preview's page and its relative files resolve under `/api/h/<id>/previews/<p>/`, so nothing is learned from context and a restart of the front changes nothing; like `/api/previews/`, they need no session (the id in the URL is the key). (2) **Answers**: an id the front does not follow is `404 {ok: false, code: "no-host"}` and reaches no host (a name is not an id); a host the front cannot reach is `503 {ok: false, code: "host-offline", link}`; while a host is offline its `state` (in the host's own shape: its threads with its own revs, unstamped), `threads/{id}` and the six settings reads (V0.127) come from the front's cache with `X-Fyr-Read-At`. (3) **`/api/front/…`**: `GET state` (`{now, user, buildId, front: {id, name}, hosts, projectOrder}`: no thread, project, profile or action), `GET stream`, `POST command` (`front.projectOrder`, `front.hostOrder {ids}`, `front.hostId {host: id}`, `front.hostMark {id, icon, color}` (V0.171); any other action is 400), `POST presence {host, threadId, device}` (the focus goes to the host named), the push routes, `auth/*` and `peer/*`. `hosts[]` gains `link: {state: up\|down, via: peer\|whois\|pipe, opened, since, error?: {kind, text}}`. (4) **One stream**: `/api/front/stream` carries each frame of a host with `host: <id>` (`thread` whole, never patched across hosts, `project`, `event`, `deploy` of every host, `stall`, `hostOrder`) and the front's own (`hello`, `hosts`, `projectOrder`); a thread patch names its host, and the stream keys what it has sent by host and thread id, so one id on two hosts is two threads. The host protocol's own stream is unchanged. (5) **Inside the front** a host's cache file is named by its install id (a rename keeps it; a file named by the host's name is read once and replaced). The pins and the host order stay by name inside the front until slice 5, because the order is kept on the hosts by name (the host protocol does not change). (6) **The old routes** (every `/api/` path outside the two prefixes) behave as before and each answer carries `Deprecation: true`; `oldRouteUses` counts them (`Front.OldRouteUses`). (7) **Tests**: `internal/front/hostprefix_test.go` (the same thread and project ids on two fake hosts; an unknown id reaches no host; offline from the cache; the stream; the focus; the cache file; the old routes counted; a preview after a restart), the hop table with the new routes, and `internal/authtest/neutral_test.go`: two real fyrds, role both, no `FYR_*`, paired; one script of flows (hosts, a project and a thread with an attachment on each host, events, Changes, a settings page, an unknown host, the host order) runs through A's front and through B's, each on a fresh pair, and the results are equal. |
| V0.173 | → V0.66, V0.98, V0.126, V0.168, #153. **Fyr spends less of the account's GitHub API limit, and shows what it spends.** *Amends V0.66 (2) (how often a deploy watch reads) and V0.168 (how deployable is read).* The account's REST limit (5000 calls an hour, shared by every agent, every host's Fyr and the owner's own gh) ran out twice on 17 Nov; the second time the updater could not read `deployable`. (1) **Measured.** Every gh call fyrd makes goes through one meter, which counts it by kind (`rest deployments`, `rest commits/:id/check-runs`, `graphql pr view`,...) and repo, and writes one line an hour to daemon.log: `gh calls 19:00–20:00: N (M counted REST); <kind> <repo> <n> (k free);...; account REST r of 5000 left, resets hh:mm`. Fyr reads `GET rate_limit` (which GitHub does not count) every 10 minutes. Settings → Automation has a **GitHub API** group per host (desktop and phone alike): the account's REST calls left and when they reset (marked when under a tenth), what this host's Fyr made this hour, and its six largest kinds of call. (2) **The updater reads `deployable` with `git ls-remote --tags https://github.com/<repo>.git refs/tags/deployable`**, gh as its credential helper since the repo is private (no API call; an annotated tag is its peeled `^{}` commit) and asks the API only when git cannot say. (3) **Deploy watches.** What a repo's first read found is kept per repo: a repo without any GitHub deployment (meros/fyr publishes releases) is believed for 24 hours, so a merge there arms a watch that ends at once with no call; a repo known to deploy skips the repo-wide read. A watch is read at each 2-minute poll in its first hour, every 10 minutes in its first day, then every 30 minutes. A deployment whose newest status is final (success, inactive, failure, error) is not read again. (4) **Conditional reads.** The deployment, status and check-run reads send the ETag of their last answer (`If-None-Match`); an answer of 304 costs nothing and gives back the stored body. The meter counts 304s as free. (5) Merged PRs are polled only while they settle (V0.82, V0.98); this row does not change that. |
| V0.174 | → V0.160, V0.157–V0.159, V0.172, decision 0030 Amendment 1 (slice 5b). **Two installs that trust each other keep one two-way link, and either of them can open it.** (1) **One TLS 1.3 connection per pair**, ALPN `fyr-peer/2` (a server offers `/2`, then `/1`; an older peer has no link and keeps the per-request route): after the hello line the dialer is the outer h2c client and opens one tunnel (`POST /link/tunnel`) inside which an inner h2c session runs with the roles reversed, so each side sends requests to the other over the same connection; keepalive pings every 15 s, no answer in 10 s ends it. (2) **A connector per roster member** on both sides; the smaller install id's dial wins a race, the loser drains for at most 5 s, a newer link from the same dialer replaces the old one; membership decides linking, following and syncing, the address only decides who is dialed; an install with no address, or one behind a block, is dialed by the other side. (3) **Trust unchanged**: pinned install keys, the signed roster, `Fyr-Actor` on every request, the link gate (a member, the drive switch, a revoked or unknown device refused in `peer` enforce, `kind=local` refused, `kind=install` carrying the proven id); a front never relays through a third install; removal closes the link and its open streams. (4) **The front** sends to a host over that host's link (mesh, else the old `/1` per-request route, else whois, counted), sync runs over the link, and `hosts[].link` says `state` (up, connecting, down), `via` (pipe, peer, whois), `opened` (here, them), `since` and why a link is down (`timeout`, `refused`, `key-mismatch`, `not-a-member`, `removed`, `older-version`, `no-address`, `no-route`, `closed`); Settings → Hosts → a host shows it in words (`fyr auth` shows it too), on desktop and phone with one component. (5) Pairing, the owner's modes, `owner.sock` and `fyr auth observe peer` are untouched; the stored modes never change. *Tests:* `internal/peerlink` (`TestOneLinkCarriesRequestsBothWays`, `TestClosingEitherEndEndsTheLink`, `TestOnlyOneTunnelPerLink`, `TestSimultaneousDialsLeaveOneLink`, `TestAFlapLeavesOneLink`), `internal/server` `TestLinkHandler`, `internal/authtest`: `TestEitherSideOpensTheLink`, `TestAnACLThatBlocksOneDirectionStillGivesBothFrontsTheirHost`, `TestBothDirectionsBlockedShowsTheReasonAndAFlapGivesOneLink`, `TestRemovalClosesTheLinkAndItsOpenStream`, `TestAStrangerGetsNoLinkAndWritesNothing`, `TestAnOlderPeerHasNoLink`, `TestMigrationKeepsTheOwnerSignedIn` step 5b; web: `web/test/peers.test.ts` (the link line and reasons), the layout screen for Settings → Hosts. |
| V0.175 | → V0.174, V0.160, V0.159, decision 0030 (slice 6, 3.5, 3.6). **Installs on a LAN with no tailscaled find each other, the address follows the key, and a phone with the owner's own certificate signs in over HTTPS.** (1) **mDNS** (`_fyr._tcp.local`, standard library only): an install with a peer port advertises `fyr-<short id>` with TXT `v=1`, `id=<id>`, its peer port and its addresses, and nothing else (no machine name, owner, build or tailnet name), and browses for the others'; an answer lives 2 minutes; `discovery: {mdns: false}` turns it off (advertising and browsing); where multicast does not pass it logs that and runs on. (2) **The address follows the key**: the mesh tries, for a member, the address it last reached it at, the roster's, then what mDNS says for its id; each dial is pinned to the id, so an answer at a wrong or spoofed address fails the handshake (`key-mismatch`), nothing is sent but a ClientHello, and the roster does not change; an id that is no member is shown `not paired` (`fyr host list`: id, address, "heard on the LAN") and never dialed; `fyr pair CODE` with no `-addr` dials what the LAN says for the id in the code first. (3) **Browsers (3.5)**: config `tls: {cert, key, listen, name}` serves the front over HTTPS on `listen` with the owner's certificate; sessions only (the observe mode is never applied to it, a `Tailscale-*` header is 400, a cross-origin or Origin-less change is refused, no loopback trust); `fyr device add` and Settings → Devices → Add make the QR/link on `https://<name>:<port>/auth/once#…`; the cookie is Secure. Without `tls` nothing is served off loopback (a `listen` that is not loopback stops fyrd at start), and a phone with neither Tailscale nor a certificate is told in the docs which of the rows of 3.5 to use. *Tests:* `internal/discovery` (`TestAnswerCarriesTheIdThePortAndNothingElse`, `TestAMalformedOrForeignAnswerIsDropped`, `TestAQueryIsRecognised`, `TestFileSourceReadsTheBus`, `TestOwnAnswerIsNotACandidate`, `TestTwoResponondersFindEachOtherOverRealMulticast` (skipped where the sandbox has no multicast)), `internal/authtest` (`TestTwoInstallsOnALANFindEachOtherAndPairByInvite`, `TestASpoofedAnswerIsNeverFollowed`, `TestAPairedIdIsFollowedAtANewAddress`, `TestNoPlainHTTPOffLoopback`, `TestAPhoneWithTheOwnersCertificateSignsInByQR`). |
| V0.176 | → V0.80, V0.164, V0.143, V0.90, V0.166, V0.167, V0.170, #236. **Fyr finds the processes a thread left running, shows them on the thread, the rail, the phone's load sheet and in Settings, and ends them by policy; a settled thread's scratch folder and a layout run's browser profile go by themselves; a settled thread's worktree is listed, and removed only when its work is pushed.** (1) **Leftover.** In a thread's cgroup (`agents/<thread>/` and its subgroups: `job/`, `undeclared/`), a process that is not the thread's CLI or a descendant of it; any process in the cgroup of a thread with no live CLI. One leftover is a root and its descendants (its memory is their RSS); its id is `<pid>@<start ticks>`, so a reused pid is never taken for it, and a pid whose start time changed is never signalled. A thread whose CLI is starting, or live with no known pid, is not looked at; `self-deploy/` and `probe` never. A root that runs under `timeout` (itself or a process in it, after `setsid`, `nohup`, `env`) has that end. A leftover is shown, and an orphan's ended, after a grace of 2 min. (2) **When Fyr looks.** Every minute (the first look 2 min after a start, so the runner's CLIs are adopted first), so a CLI that exits, is stopped or is killed (oomd, the watchdog of V0.143) is seen within a minute; and at once when a run ends and when a thread settles or is archived. (3) **Policy.** A settled or archived thread's leftovers end at once. A cgroup no thread of this host has (`orphan`): after the grace. A live thread's: never during a run; 30 min after the run ended (or after it was first seen, whichever is later), unless the agent declared it (`keep_process`) or its timeout has not passed. The owner's Keep (`host.leftoverKeep`, 24 h by default, at most a week) holds through a settle too; the agent's ends at the settle. The owner's End (`host.leftoverEnd`) ends one now. Ending is SIGTERM to each process, then SIGKILL after 5 s (a frozen one too); not `cgroup.kill`, which would also end a CLI that starts in the same cgroup meanwhile. Nothing outside `fyr.service/agents/` is read or signalled. (4) **The line.** Each end leaves a line on its thread (`leftover.ended`: "Fyr ended 2 leftover processes (the thread settled): chromium (1.9 GB), http.server"), shown in the conversation as a Fyr note; the agent hears it at its next run with "Clean up what you start, prefer a timeout, and for a process that must stay call mcp__fyr__keep_process" (a Next notice, never a wake). An orphan's end is in the log only. (5) **keep_process** `{pid or match, hours (1–24, default 4), why}`: the agent's declaration of a process that must outlive its run (a dev server or a tunnel the owner asked for); a pid names the root or a process inside it, a match at least 3 characters of the root's command line. Kept in the store (KV `leftover-keeps`). (6) **Temporary files.** The sweep removes a `<tmp>/fyr-layout-*` profile 30 min old that no process's command line names, and a settled or archived thread's scratch folder (`<tmp>/claude-<uid>/<project>/<session>/`) when it has no live CLI. Every 10 min it lists the worktrees of the threads that went to sleep in the last 14 days: the thread's folder when it is a linked worktree, and the repository's worktrees on one of its changes' branches. A worktree is never removed by the sweep: a settled thread can wake in its folder. The owner's Remove (`host.tempRemove`, `git worktree remove`, no force) removes one with no uncommitted file whose HEAD is on a remote branch, checked again at the press; one with uncommitted or unpushed work is only shown ("kept: 2 commits not pushed"). (7) **Shown** (`GET /api/load`: `leftovers`, `temps`). The open thread has a strip under its header, desktop and phone (one component): "2 leftover processes: chromium (1.9 GB), http.server · Fyr ends them in 24 min" with Keep and End (the phone's under the text, 44 px). The desktop rail shows "5 left" under the host's meters, its tooltip naming them; a press opens Settings → Automation at Leftover processes. The phone's load sheet has the same as a line (no rail, no hover on a phone). Settings → Automation (desktop and phone): Leftover processes (each with its thread, what holds it or when it ends, Open, Keep, End) and Worktrees of settled threads (Remove on a safe one). (8) **Without cgroups** (macOS, #236): the tracker reads the process table (`/proc`, else `ps -A -o pid=,ppid=,rss=,etime=,args=`) every minute and remembers each live CLI's descendants as its thread's; a remembered process that is no longer under its live CLI is that thread's leftover, and (3)–(7) hold. A process started and orphaned between two looks is not seen; the memory is lost at a restart. (9) **The prompt** asks the agent to clean up what it starts, prefer a timeout, and declare a process that must stay. (10) `leftovers: "off"` in the config turns (1)–(8) off on a host. *Tests:* `internal/cgroup` (`TestV176ALeftoverIsWhatIsNotTheCLIsTree`, `TestV176AThreadWithoutALiveCLILeavesEverythingAndAnUnknownPidLeavesNothing`, `TestV176BSettledThreadsLeftoversEndAtOnceAndLeaveALine`, `TestV176BLiveThreadKeepsLeftoversUntilIdleLimitUnlessKeptOrTimed`, `TestV176BOwnerKeepHoldsThroughSettleAndOwnerEndEndsAtOnce`, `TestV176BOrphanCgroupEndsAfterTheGraceAndPidReuseIsRefused`, `TestV176EProcessTreeModeFollowsTheCLIsDescendantsAfterItEnds`, `TestV176CLayoutProfilesWhoseChromeIsGoneAreRemovedOthersStay`, `TestV176CScratchOfAnAsleepThreadWithoutCLIGoesAndAnAwakeOneStays`, `TestV176CWorktreesOfSettledThreadsAreListedOnlyAPushedCleanOneCanGo`: real processes (a fake CLI with a child, orphans, a `timeout` orphan) in a scratch cgroupfs in a temp dir, real git repositories), `internal/engine` (`TestV176TheEndedLineIsOnTheThreadAndANoticeNotAWake`, `TestV176TheOwnersCommandsReachTheHostAndAreTheOwnersOnly`, `TestV176ARunsEndAndASettleLookAtOnce`, `TestV176KeepProcessDeclaresForTheCallingThread`), `internal/agent` (`TestV176PromptAsksToCleanUpAndDeclare`), `web/test/leftovers.test.ts`, the layout test's `V0.176 desktop` and `V0.176 phone`, light and dark. |
| V0.177 | → decision 0031 (slice 2a), V0.172, V0.51, V0.54, V0.85, V0.121, V0.124, V0.127, V0.132, V0.139, V0.147. **The client names the host in every call: one URL function, per-host snapshots, the one stream.** *No change to what the owner sees.* (1) **`web/src/urls.ts`** is the only module that writes an `/api/` path in a string: `hostPath(hostId, path)` (`/api/h/<id>/…`) and `frontPath(path)` (`/api/front/…`); a call with no host id throws, and a URL that is only shown (an image, a link) is empty without one. `test/urls.test.ts` fails on an `/api/` string anywhere else (the service worker's rule and the mock are the exceptions). (2) **Every `Api` method that is a host's takes the host's install id first** (`command`, `events`, `thread`, `changes`, `prDetail`, the folder picker, uploads, attachment and preview URLs, the settings reads, `commands`, `uploadProjectIcon`, `t3Candidates`); the front's own are `frontCommand` (`front.projectOrder`, `front.hostOrder {ids}`, `front.hostId {host: id}`, `front.hostMark`), `presence(host, threadId, device)`, push, `authState`/`authAct`, `peerState`/`peerAct`. `args.host` is gone from every command. Quota, load and profiles are read from each host that is online, one call each (`/api/h/<id>/quota`), and joined in the owner's host order. (3) **The start** is the stream `/api/front/stream`, then on `hello` `/api/front/state` (the hosts) and `/api/h/<id>/state` of every host with an install id, in parallel (a host that cannot answer is listed and has no records; one without an id is listed and cannot be addressed). `src/hostfeed.ts` makes the state the store reads from them: a thread and a project are stamped with the host whose state or frame they came in, `host` (the name, shown) and `hostId` (the id, sent), and `hostOffline` while that host is unreachable; actions are the first host's, profiles merge by id, a stall names its host, the deploy is the one of the host the front runs in (until slice 4). A `hosts` frame that changes which hosts are reachable reads every state again. The device's copy is still drawn at once; "since" is not used until the cache is per host (slice 2b), so each start reads every host's whole state. (4) **Pickers** (New thread, New project, Adopt, the folder picker, Settings) still choose a host by name; `hostIdByName` turns the name into the id once, at the call; no name is the first host in the owner's order, as the picker's default choice, which the call then names. The commands of threads and projects take the id from the record (`thread.hostId`, `project.hostId`); `thread.create` takes the picked host, else the project's, else the first. (5) **Front frames on the front's stream** (`hosts`, `projectOrder`, `hostMarks`) and `hostMarks` in `/api/front/state`: both were missing from slice 1. (6) **The mock** (`?mock=…`) stamps ids as the front does and maps them back to its fixtures' names; its command log records the host. Tests: `test/urls.test.ts`, `test/hostfeed.test.ts`; `internal/front/hostprefix_test.go` (TestFrontStreamCarriesTheFrontsOwnFrames); the layout and auth e2e run on the new calls. The store, the cache and the selectors stay one map keyed by id until slice 2b (so the same id on two hosts is tested there). |
| V0.178 | → V0.173, V0.66, V0.8, V0.72, decision 0027. **One budget for a host's GitHub calls: polls give way, PR polls are batched, and agents are told the limit is shared.** *Amends V0.8 (one gh pr view per PR per poll) and V0.173.* (1) **Priority.** Every gh call of fyrd goes through one budget per host. The updater, merges, re-runs and the PR tab (act) always run; the poller's calls (PRs, deploy watches, annotations: poll) give way. (2) **Low or limited.** The budget knows each limit (REST `core`, `graphql`) from the 10-minute read of `rate_limit` and from the rate headers of every `gh api -i` answer (`X-RateLimit-Remaining`, `-Reset`, `-Resource`). At 500 calls left or fewer, polls of that limit wait until its reset; after GitHub refuses a call for the limit, they wait until the reset, or 5 minutes for a secondary limit, which names none. One failed call, not a storm. A held poll sends nothing and logs nothing; the pause is logged once (`github: polls wait until hh:mm: <why>`) and Settings → Automation → GitHub API shows it as a row ("Polls wait until 20:51" / "PR reads wait…", the why, "Merges and updates still run"), desktop and phone alike. A deploy watch not read is due again at the next poll. (3) **Pace.** Poll REST calls keep to 30 a minute (a token bucket); a poll past it waits for the next one, with no notice. (4) **Batched PR polls.** The poller reads the PRs of one repo in one GraphQL query (`pullRequest(number:)` aliases, up to 20 a query) with the fields of its `gh pr view` and the head commit's check rollup (first 100 contexts); one PR alone is still a `gh pr view`. A PR the batch does not return (it does not exist, or the query failed) is read alone, so V0.72's two answers still end a missing PR. (5) **The agent prompt** says the limit is shared: batch gh reads, write no loop that polls gh (`until`/`while` around `gh run list`, `gh run view`, `gh api`, or `gh run watch`), use mcp__fyr__watch and change_list, and if a shell must wait on CI, one foreground `gh pr checks <n> --watch --interval 60`. (6) The meter names a compare (`a...b`) as `rest compare/:id`. |
| V0.181 | → V0.147, V0.51, V0.54, V0.129, decision 0031. **A stalled host is an offline host: the front treats the host in its own process as a remote one, and the exit is a last resort.** *Amends V0.147 (6): the exit comes after 10 minutes, not 2.* (#267, from the fyrd architecture review, 17 Nov: "a stuck laptop host hides the server and the Mac from the phone too.") (1) **Why.** With the engine's lock held, the front kept its own host online as long as the host's stream was open (the stream takes no engine lock): every call to it hung until its deadline, and after 2 minutes the watchdog ended fyrd (code 70), and with it the front, every browser's stream and every peer link. (2) **The signal.** The deadline's 503 says `"code": "host-stalled"` (`api.StalledCode`). When a call the front forwards to a host gets it, or the host's stream sends a `stall` frame, the front asks that host for its state (one ask at a time per host, bounded by the host's read deadline plus 5 s). An answer changes nothing. No answer marks the host offline, as V0.51 does for a host that went away: its threads carry `hostOffline`, `hosts[].error` is `stalled: …` (link error kind `stalled`; a pipe's link stays `up`, since the pipe is), its state, threads and settings reads come from the front's cache (decision 0031), and its commands are refused at once with `host-offline` instead of waiting for the deadline. The front asks again every 5 s; at the first answer it loads that state (a newer thread taken from the stream meanwhile is kept) and the host is online. The stream, the other hosts and the peer links are not touched. Desktop and phone show it as any offline host, from the same stamps (`offlineLine`, the host chip, the pickers' notes); no client change. (3) **The last resort.** The engine's lock held past **10 minutes** (`lockwatch.ExitAfter`) still writes the dump and exits 70 for the launcher to restart fyrd; the runner keeps the agents (V0.90). daemon.log says so twice: when the engine's lock passes the stall limit ("the front shows this host offline; fyrd exits for a restart if it is still held after 10m0s") and at the exit ("escalation, the last resort"). Tests: `internal/front/stall_test.go` (TestV181StalledLocalHostIsOfflineAndTheFrontStaysUp: a real host over the pipe whose calls hang as with its lock held, beside a second host; by a 503 and by a stall frame it goes offline, its state is served from the cache, its command is refused, the other host takes a command, the browser's stream stays open and carries the offline stamp and its removal; a stall the host answers through leaves it online); `internal/lockwatch` (TestV181ExitIsALastResort: no exit at 2 and 5 minutes, an exit at 10, both lines logged). |
| V0.180 | → V0.13, V0.90, V0.159, V0.174, decision 0023, decision 0030 (Amendment 1, 4), #264, #261. **fyrd says when its config changed under it, `systemctl reload fyr` applies it with the agents kept, and a pairing that is refused says who refused and why.** (Seen 17 Nov: a NixOS switch set `peerListen`, which fyrd never read; the laptop then could not join home-server and the CLI printed `Fyr refused: 409 Conflict bad request`.) (1) **Config drift.** fyrd remembers the bytes of its config file at start (a SHA-256), stats it every 30 s and hashes it when the stat moved; a file that differs from what was read (or appeared, or went) is logged once (`config: … changed since fyrd read it; restart fyrd to apply`), and back to the read bytes is logged too. While it differs, `/healthz` stays 200 and adds `warning: config changed; restart fyrd to apply` (a `deploy.Warning`: the launcher trusts a build by the status only), `GET /api/front/peer/state` and the owner socket's `GET /host/list` carry `configChanged`, and Settings → Hosts shows it at the top of the installs panel, desktop and phone alike (one component): "Config changed; restart fyrd to apply. Fyr reads its config file at start. On NixOS, systemctl reload fyr restarts fyrd on the new file and the agents keep running." (2) **A safe reload.** The launcher forwards SIGHUP to fyrd and keeps running; fyrd exits 75 on it (as since V0.160's key rotation), so the launcher starts it again on the new config while its runner keeps the agents (decision 0023); a next build that waits is switched to, as on any exit 75. SIGINT and SIGTERM stop as before. The NixOS unit gets `ExecReload = kill -HUP $MAINPID` and `reloadTriggers = [ <config.json> ]`: a switch that changes only the config reloads the unit (switch-to-configuration reloads a unit whose `X-Reload-Triggers` alone changed, even with `restartIfChanged = false`), and a switch that changes the package still restarts nothing. (3) **A joiner with no peer port** (port 0: no `peerListen`, or a host that only dials out) is paired by the inviter as a member that dials out only: the mesh is two-way since V0.174, so its connector opens the link, and the inviter shows it "no address for it yet; it may open the link itself" until it does (decision 0030, Amendment 1, 4). (4) **Refusals name the cause.** The inviter's `/pair/invite` answers 400 with the bad field: "the join request is not JSON", "port: 70000 is not a port (1 to 65535, or 0 …)", "secret: missing". The joiner says "the install that made the invite refused (<status>): <its words>"; an inviter older than V0.180 that answers port 0 with a bare "bad request" is explained ("this install has no peer port, and that install is older than V0.180 …: set peerListen here and restart fyrd, or update that install"). The owner socket's `POST /pair/join` passes the inviter's 4xx through (its 5xx as 502), and 409 stays for a failure on this side; `fyr` prints a refusal's `error` field alone, and the status and body only when there is none. *Tests:* `cmd/fyrd` (`TestConfigWatchSaysWhenTheFileChanged`, `TestConfigWatchSeesAFileThatAppearsOrGoes`, `TestHealthzNotesAChangedConfigAndStaysGreen`), `internal/authtest` (`TestARunningFyrdSaysItsConfigChanged`: a real fyrd), `internal/peerwire` (`TestJoinStatusPassesTheInvitersThrough`), `internal/deploy` (`TestHealthWarningStaysGreen`, `TestLauncherReloadsOnSIGHUP`), `internal/peer` (`TestAJoinerWithoutAPeerPortIsPairedAsDialOutOnly`, `TestJoinInviteNamesTheBadField`, `TestARefusedJoinCarriesTheInvitersStatusAndWords`, `TestAnOlderInviterRefusingPortZeroIsExplained`), `cmd/fyr` (`TestOwnerCallPrintsTheErrorField`, `TestOwnerCallWithoutAnErrorFieldNamesTheStatus`), `web/test/peers.test.ts`, the layout test's `V0.125 desktop` and `V0.125 phone` (`config changed`), the flake's module check (`reloadTriggers`, `ExecReload`). |
| V0.182 | → V0.147, V0.86, decision 0029 (S2–S5), V0.163, V0.164, V0.51, #265. **fyrd's wiring moves into packages: the slot admission policy, the engine's dependencies as typed groups, one listener helper.** *No change to what the owner sees.* (1) **Admission.** `internal/admission` is the slot policy that was closures in fyrd's main.go: `Admission{Host, Jobs, Policy, N, Pool}` predicts a job's need (its class's learned demand and peak, the prior of 1/N until learned), costs a holder (the prior for a priority hold, a little CPU and the memory it holds for an idle one, else at least its live use), admits the head (`slots.DecideWith`, with undeclared work beside the holders), passes a later waiter by EASY backfill, says when a holder is over its prediction by an eighth of the host, and when the host thrashes. `Install` makes it the pool's `Host`, `Cost`, `Admit` and `Backfill`. Its locks: it runs under the pool's lock and calls the sampler and the job tracker, never the pool or the engine. (2) **The engine's dependencies** are six typed groups (`AgentDeps`, `OutsideDeps`, `DeployDeps`, `SlotDeps`, `UndeclaredDeps`, `LeftoverDeps`, `internal/engine/deps.go`), each set once by fyrd as one value, embedded in the engine so their fields read as before. The engine calls every func in them without `e.mu` (V0.147's rule); `guardDeps` fails a test that sees one called under it, and a full run of the engine's tests fails while any func of the groups is reached by no guarded test. They are not arguments of `engine.New`: the agent manager and the job tracker are made from the engine, so they exist only after it. (3) **Listeners.** One helper serves and shuts down fyrd's HTTP servers (role host, role both, front only and the old host listeners), and role both and front only make and run the front, its peer side and discovery through one function. Tests: `internal/admission/admission_test.go`, `internal/engine/deps_test.go` with `TestV182DepsAreCalledWithoutTheEngineLock`, `cmd/fyrd/servers_test.go`. |
| V0.183 | → decision 0030 (3.5, 3.6, 5.1, Amendment 1), V0.158–V0.175, V0.130, V0.14, #266. **The peer wiring moves out of fyrd, and the front stops importing the host's server.** *No change to what the owner sees.* (1) **`internal/peerwire`** is fyrd's peer side: `Start(ctx, Deps{Config, Name, Key, Env, Dial, Port, Front, Host, Auth, Old, Self})` runs the mesh, the peer port and mDNS, the peer way in's mode (enforce stops the old host listeners, observe starts them; a request on them or on the whois listener is one the mode would refuse), a front following each member over its link and dropping a removed one, and the devices kept in step with the roster. `Front`, `Host`, `Auth` and `Old` are small interfaces, nil when the install has none. `OwnerRoutes(Owner{…})` is the owner socket's `host list`, `pair invite`, `pair join`, `host remove`, `host rotate` and `host drive`, with the same status codes; the restart after a rotation is injected. fyrd only builds the deps and calls them. (2) **`internal/httpguard`** holds the request guards a host and a front share: `Browser` (the loopback and tailnet guard with gzip), `Guard`, `Gzip`, `ViaServe`, `ServeSocket`, `ViaLAN`, `IsViaServe`, `IsViaLAN`, `TailnetHost`, `ValidHost`. The front imports it, not `internal/server`. (3) **The legacy-front count** (decision 0030 slice 1) is the host server's own (`Server.LegacyFronts`), given to the front as `front.Options.LegacyFronts`; a front only shows 0, as before. Tests: `internal/peerwire/peerwire_test.go`, `internal/httpguard/guard_test.go`, `internal/front/imports_test.go` (TestV183TheFrontDoesNotImportTheServer). |
| V0.186 | → V0.109, V0.48, V0.138, V0.91, V0.104, V0.137. **A section folds and unfolds only by the owner's hand, and the header of a folded section shows that the current thread is inside it.** *Amends V0.109 (a thread in a closed section no longer opens it; its header is the mark).* (1) **What changes a fold.** A click, tap, Enter or Space on the section's header, and naming the section in the rail, with g1–g5 or in the phone's strip, which is a request to go to that section. Nothing else unfolds one: opening a thread by any path (a row, a link, a notification, `?open=`, Back and Forward), a thread moving between sections, j/k, a filter clearing (V0.138), a reload. V0.48 stays: a Settled the owner opened from its header closes after its 30 s. (2) **The folds are kept** per device (`fyr.sectionOpen`), so a reload, also the one a new build causes, does not undo them; an open Settled is not kept, its 30 s do not survive a reload. (3) **The mark.** When the current thread (the open one, else the last one open, so also on the phone after Back) is in a folded section, that section's header has the selected row's background and shows the thread's title, cut with an ellipsis, after the name and the count, in place of the section's hint. It says so to a screen reader: `aria-current`, and a name with the section, its count and the title. The click still only toggles. Unfolded, the row is the only mark on the desktop. The mark moves with the thread. A pinned or queued thread has a row that is always drawn, so no header is marked for it. (4) **Scrolling** to the current thread lands on the header when the row is folded away, and unfolds nothing. A row past an *open* section's limit still shows the whole section (V0.109): that is not a fold. (5) **j/k walk the drawn rows and skip folded sections.** From a thread inside a folded section the step starts at that section's place: j goes to the next drawn row below its header, k to the one above. The row chosen after a thread in the list leaves (settle, archive) follows the same rule. (6) **Filters.** A thread that fails a filter still clears it (V0.138); a folded section does not count as filtered out. (7) Both fronts share the header's behaviour (`sectionHead`, `HeldTitle` in `row-ui.tsx`) and differ only in sizes (the phone's 44 px touch height). Headers are buttons for the keyboard and screen readers (`aria-expanded`). |
| V0.179 | → decision 0031 (slice 2b), V0.177, V0.121, V0.38, V0.54. **A record is named by its host and its id: the client keys threads and projects by `<hostId>/<recordId>`, so the same id on two hosts is two records.** *No change to what the owner sees, except what is listed below.* (1) **Refs.** `web/src/hostfeed.ts` gives every thread and project its ref as its client id (`id`), keeps the host's own id in `rid`, and makes the ids inside a record that point at another record of the same host refs too (a thread's `projectId` and `parent`, a change's `threads`); a timeline event's `subject`, a thread patch, the load (threads, slots' owners, undeclared, leftovers, temps), a deploy's `blocking` and the reply of `thread.create`, `project.*` and `host.slotOrder` carry refs. Everything the client keys by id (the store, the selection, the open thread, drafts, the events cache) then cannot mix two hosts. (2) **Calls send the host's own id**: `bare(host, ref)` for a thread or project in a path, `unrefDeep` over a command's arguments (only that host's refs are taken off). `front.projectOrder` takes refs; an order saved before names the host's own ids and still places the projects (`rid`). (3) **A link names the host's own id** (`?open=t_12`, `?project=`, Settings → Projects/`p_12`): once the records are here it becomes the ref of the one record that has the id, and is left as it is (refused) when two have it (`resolveRef`). (4) **The device's copy is per host**: the stored state is read again under a new key (the old copy is dropped), and each host is asked `?since=` its own newest rev (a rev is its host's clock), a partial answer merged into that host's stored threads. (5) **Not yet (slice 3)**: `localStorage` keys that name a record (drafts, the project filter, New thread's remembered project) and a push tap still hold the host's own ids until their migration; a draft written before this release is not found. Tests: `test/refs.test.ts` (one thread id and one project id on two hosts stay two records through the snapshot and a frame; bare and ref; events, load and slots; links; the old project order), `test/hostfeed.test.ts`; the layout e2e with the hosts=2 scenario reading refs. |
| V0.184 | → V0.40, V0.141, V0.169, V0.14. **The owner sets the thread list's width on the desktop.** *Amends V0.169 on the desktop only: the project chips wrap instead of scrolling sideways.* (1) **The handle** is on the edge between the list and the right-hand pane. A drag (pointer events: mouse, pen or finger) changes the width live; while it lasts no text is selected and no iframe (a live view, a preview) takes the pointer. A press that does not move stores nothing. (2) **Limits.** Default 372 px, minimum 280, maximum 640, and at most what leaves the conversation 480 px beside the 76 px rail; where the window has no room for both the list keeps 280 and the conversation gives way. The window limits what is applied, not what is kept: a narrower window clamps the width, a wider one brings it back. (3) **Reset.** A double click or double tap, or Enter on the handle, goes back to the default and removes the stored width. (4) **Keyboard.** The handle is a focusable `role="separator"` with `aria-orientation="vertical"` and `aria-valuenow/min/max`: Left and Right move it 16 px, 64 px with Shift, Home and End go to the limits. (5) **Kept per browser** in `localStorage` (`fyr.listWidth`, read and written in try/catch). It names no record, so it has no host in it (decision 0031). (6) **Row layout.** At every width titles are cut within their row; the project chips wrap (three lines, then they scroll) and the host segments shrink, because a mouse has no sideways swipe. (7) **The phone** has no handle and keeps its one row of chips: it shows the list full screen with no pane beside it, and a finger swipes the row. *Tests:* `web/test/sidelist.test.ts`, the layout test's `V0.184 desktop` (drag over an iframe, clamping, persistence, reset, keys, touch, the list and the rail at both limits and two window heights) and `V0.184 phone`. |
| V0.185 | → 5.3, 6.2, 7.1, 7.2, 7.3, 7.5, V0.67, V0.104, decision 0031. **A thread can be snoozed until its work is done.** (1) **The choice.** `thread.snoozeWhenDone` (no args, bulk) is the last entry of the one snooze list on desktop and phone, "Until work is done, at most 24 h", so the keys of the timed choices stay. (2) **What counts as work going on** (`WorkGoing`): a live run or background task; a turn about to start (an unread message, a queued notice that wakes the agent, a handover, a wait for the machine); or something the agent set up: a watch or settle condition of its own that is armed, a place in the slot queue, a merge ask held for CI, CI pending on an open PR of the thread. A thread whose run ended but that still has one of these stays snoozed. (3) **It wakes** when no work goes on, when the thread is settled or archived, or at once when it needs the owner (`NeedsOwner`): a question, an approval or merge ask, a failure, an unresponsive agent, a change that is ready or stopped on a failure, a Run card. This holds whatever the request's age, so this snooze never hides one; attention reads the same rule, so the thread shows even before the Rule fires. (4) **Safety net.** The snooze ends after 24 h (`SnoozeDoneMax`) however the work goes, so a watch that never fires hides nothing for long. The menu shows the limit; `view.snoozeUntil` carries the time and `view.snoozeWhenDone` says which snooze it is. (5) **No silent hiding.** With no work going, or when the thread needs the owner, the action is unavailable with the reason ("Nothing is working", "It needs you now"): the option is off on both fronts and the host refuses it. For several threads it goes to those that can take it. (6) **The Rule.** The View's snooze Rule with `any(workOver, at(armed + 24 h))`. It is evaluated last in `Reduce`, after merge asks are gated and notices queued, and not on a `feed.take`, so a firing watch or a notice on its way to the agent does not count as the end. It is stored with the View, so it survives a restart: a run the restart keeps goes on snoozed, and a run it loses is a failure, which wakes the thread. (7) **The slot queue** is the pool's; `slot.queued` copies a thread's place to its record (`slotQueued`), from the slot tools and on the tick. (8) As every snooze it removes the pin (V0.104) and is undone by Unsnooze or a message; the wake is an attention change like any other, so it notifies by the same rules. The row and the Snoozed section say "until work is done" rather than a time. |
| V0.187 | → V0.41. **A message's Copy shows again on the desktop.** Entries of the conversation use `content-visibility: auto`, which clips what leaves the entry's box, and the desktop's Copy hung below the message, outside it. The entry now holds that strip as padding and gives it back as a negative margin, so the spacing between entries does not change. The phone keeps Copy in the flow (no hover). |
| V0.188 | → V0.164 (7), V0.151, decision 0032 (slice 1). **The memory guard freezes a long episode of undeclared work in time slices, releases a freeze that holds nothing, and logs every decision.** *Amends V0.164 (7): "each episode once" and "never when it would help nobody".* (1) **Somebody to help.** Undeclared work is frozen under V0.151 (2)'s condition when an admitted job runs, or another thread is flagged, or any other thread runs (its agent starts slowly in a thrashing cgroup); not when none of them holds. (2) **Time slices.** An episode that was frozen and thawed is frozen again once the work has run 60 s and memory is short again, at most 8 times in the episode; then it runs, and the log says so. (3) **A freeze that holds nothing.** When the frozen cgroup has no process any more (`populated 0`, or the cgroup is gone) it is thawed at the next tick and no longer blocks the next freeze. (4) **Log.** `fyrd` writes one line starting `guard:` for: memory becoming short and recovering (headroom, stall, slowdown, flagged, held, running, what is frozen); each freeze (thread, its number, the episode's age, command, memory, cores, signals, how many threads run); each thaw (after how long and why: memory fine for 30 s, its processes are gone, the 10 min limit, the episode or hold ended); and each reason not to freeze or that it failed, at most once a minute per thread and text. (5) **History.** `GET /api/load` adds to the heavy jobs `guard`: `short` and `shortSince` (memory is short now), `max` (8) and `refreezeSec` (60), `events` (the last 60 decisions: `short`, `recovered`, `freeze`, `thaw`, with the thread, work `undeclared` or `job`, the freeze's number, the thaw's reason `memory`, `gone`, `limit` or `ended`, the headroom and stall) and per thread `threads` (`n`, `frozen`, `frozenAt`, `nextAt`, `capped`). It is a published copy, so the pool reads it under its lock and waits for nothing. "
"(6) **Shown** (desktop and phone, one component): a frozen thread's row says \"undeclared · paused 3/8 · 2 min\" in place of \"paused: memory\" (a thread between two pauses adds \"runs · next pause possible in 24 s\", one at the cap \"let run after 8 pauses\"); Settings → Automation → Memory guard, when the host sends a guard, shows one line (\"Memory is short (8 min) · 1 thread frozen\"), a chart of the last 15 minutes (a shaded band for each time memory was short; a lane per thread the guard froze, a hatched stretch for each freeze with its number; plain SVG that scales to the width, with a text alternative) and the last 8 decisions, newest first, with the time. "
"(7) **Not in this slice:** a freeze per job and rotation by a measured slice; a memory budget per thread; both are in decision 0032. Tests: `internal/engine/undeclared_test.go` F6, F7/F8, F13–F17; `internal/cgroup/undeclared_freeze_test.go`; `web/test/guard.test.ts` G1–G10; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.188 at 1280 and 390 wide with `?mock=rail&guard=1`. |
| V0.189 | → V0.101, V0.91, V0.137, V0.162, V0.59 (8), 6.3, 6.4, 13.1 (U11, `Shift+E`), decision 0031. **A finished thread the owner has not looked at is Unread, its own section directly above Seen; Seen holds only what the owner has seen.** *Amends V0.101 (the unread dot in Seen), V0.162 (1) and V0.59 (8).* (1) **The order** is Needs you, Working, Waiting to run, Unread, Seen, Snoozed, Settled. A finished thread does not go back to Needs you: V0.101 stands, nothing is asked of the owner. (2) **The client derives it.** The host is unchanged: `state: "done"` in `section: "seen"` is Unread (`sectionOf`), so a front follows hosts of any version since V0.101 (decision 0031). A pinned unread thread is in the pinned block as any pinned thread. (3) **It moves by the read watermark** (6.4): opening the thread to its end or Mark seen moves it to Seen, Mark unseen moves it back, new activity after a run brings it back. Both fronts' menus offer Mark seen on an unread thread and Mark unseen on a seen one (`seenToggle`); the desktop menu used to offer Mark unseen on an unread thread, and the phone only ever offered Mark unseen. (4) **It is a section like the others:** its own count, rail and strip item, fold state (open by default), row limit (200) and place in j/k. `g6` jumps to it; g1 to g5 keep their meaning, so g3 is still Seen. The rail's compact form now starts under 1010 px (940 before), so the extra item does not make it scroll. (5) **A sweep leaves Unread alone; the owner's own picks do not.** Until now ⇧E on a row in Seen also settled the unread threads there, and Select all with Settle N on the phone did too. The sweeping actions now skip them: ⇧E with nothing picked, on the marked row's section (from a row in Unread it settles nothing), and the phone's Select all, which leaves unread rows unpicked. The toast counts them ("Settled 3 threads; 2 unread threads kept"). Threads the owner picks one by one (`x` on the desktop, a tap in the phone's selection, also after Select all) are an explicit choice and settle even when unread, as does Settle on the one thread (`e`, the row menu, the swipe). ⇧E on the other sections is otherwise unchanged. (6) **The change chip says "change open", not "working".** The phase `working` means the change is not finished; the chip shows on threads whose agent has stopped, where "working" read as a running agent. An agent's own label, a failure, "needs you" and the other phases read as before. The Changes tab keeps the phase names. |
| V0.195 | → V0.16, V0.143, V0.133, V0.95 (3), V0.164. **A thread waiting for a background command says which one, and when the command sits while the thread uses no CPU, the agent is woken to check it.** (18 Nov: a hand-written wait outlived the experiment it waited for; the thread read as waiting for 17 min until the owner asked why nothing moved.) (1) **Shown** (desktop and phone, the same components): the Waiting reason is "Background: <description>" for one command and "N background tasks" for several; the row adds "no CPU use since 09:19" when quiet; after the turn the status line above the composer reads "Background: <description> · 35 min", with the quiet note in the signal ink; the timeline gets one line when the agent is told. Agents keep "1 agent". (2) **Which tasks.** `is_backgrounded` (the CLI also reports every foreground Bash as a task) and the starting tool (it reports a Monitor as `local_bash`). A Monitor never counts as stuck: it is quiet by design and has a timeout. (3) **Quiet** is measured on the thread's cgroup, which also holds what the agent detached: the average over each minute under 0.05 cores (idle threads measured 0.004 to 0.03) for 5 min after the turn ended, with no turn, unread message, decision, running subagent, machine wait or unresponsive state. A frozen thread, or a gap over 3 min between readings, starts the count again. CPU use clears the mark. Without cgroups nothing is detected; (1) still holds. (4) **Told once per task**, as a budgeted wake (`task.quiet`). The notice allows that the wait is right: Fyr cannot see what a command waits for. (5) **Prevention.** The system prompt says to run the job itself in the background and never to detach and poll; a background Bash with an `until`/`while` … `sleep` loop and no `timeout` gets a note at the gate. Tests: `internal/engine/quietwait_test.go`, `internal/core/feed_test.go` (`task.quiet`), `internal/core/unresponsive_test.go`, `web/test/tasks.test.ts`, `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` V0.195. |
| V0.196 | → V0.48, V0.186. **Snoozed and Settled both close by themselves after 10 s, on the desktop and the phone.** *Amends V0.48 (30 s, Settled only, desktop only) and V0.186 (1), (2) (an open Snoozed is no longer kept over a reload, like an open Settled).* (1) **When.** Each section opened from its header runs on its own 10 s and shows the small clock in its header. (2) **What restarts the time.** Using the section: pointing at it, pressing in it or a key in it, and on the phone, which has no pointing, scrolling while it is on screen. (3) **What keeps it open**, as in V0.48: the current thread is in it, or the owner named it in the rail, with g4/g5 or in the phone's strip. Tests: `web/test/autohide.test.ts`, `web/test/selection.test.ts`; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` section "V0.196 auto-close", desktop and phone. |
| V0.190 | → V0.188, decision 0032, decision 0031. **Settings → Automation shows on a host whose memory guard has done nothing yet.** The host sent `events: null` until the guard's first decision, and the page failed with "e.events is not iterable". The host now sends `[]`, and the client reads `null` as empty, so it also works with hosts of V0.188 and V0.189. |
| V0.191 | → V0.188, V0.164 (7), decision 0032 (slice 1). **The second freeze of an episode works.** A thaw leaves the work in `undeclared/`, and the freeze only looked for processes in the thread's own cgroup, so every freeze after the first found nothing to move and logged `could not freeze`: V0.188's time slices never happened on a real cgroup, while its engine tests, which fake the freeze, passed. A freeze now also holds work already in `undeclared/`; with nothing there and nothing to move it still does nothing. Tests: `internal/cgroup/undeclared_freeze_test.go` F18, and `TestRealRefreeze` against the kernel (`FYR_REAL_CGROUP=1` in a delegated scope). |
| V0.192 | → V0.109, V0.114, V0.134, V0.169, V0.186. **The current thread punches through whatever hides it in the list: one row, in its own place, marked as a visitor.** *Replaces V0.138 (opening a thread no longer clears a filter) and V0.186 (3) (a folded header no longer carries the mark); V0.186 (1), (2) stand.* (1) **Which thread.** The current thread (the open one, else the last one open, so also on the phone after Back), when a host or project filter, quiet hours or a folded section would hide it. (2) **Where.** In its section at its sorted place, or in the pinned block or the queue when it belongs there; under a folded header it is the only row, and the section stays folded. j/k step on from that place. It leaves as soon as another thread is current. (3) **The mark.** A dashed outline on the row and, first on its second line, a tag with an eye-off icon: "Outside filter", "Quiet project" or "In folded section"; the same on both fronts. On the desktop it is also the selected row. (4) **Nothing else changes.** The filters stay as the owner set them, and every count (rail, strip, section headers, chips, the empty state) still counts only what they let through. (5) **What still hides it.** A typed search it does not match, and a quiet thread that has not passed the hold (V0.114). Tests: `web/test/selection.test.ts`; `web/test/layout.e2e.mjs` sections "V0.192 folded" and "V0.192 filter", desktop and phone. |
| V0.193 | → V0.13, V0.90, #167. **A second `fyr` on a data folder that already has a launcher says so and stops.** (18 Nov 08:28: a launcher with no runner started beside the live service; its fyrd failed on the port six times, into the live install's log, before it gave up.) The launcher ends with an error that names the data folder and `fyr open` when another live runner holds the socket; it no longer starts a fyrd. A socket nobody answers is still taken over. Test: `internal/runner/runner_test.go` `TestListenRefusesALiveRunner`. |
| V0.199 | → V0.97, V0.136, V0.178. **A merge ask is judged on a fresh read of the PR.** An agent asks to merge right after it pushes, while the poller's last read, up to 2 minutes old, can still hold a conflict the push resolved and the old head. Before `change_request` with `merge` or `effect_request` with `pr.merge` is judged, Fyr reads the thread's PRs and waits up to 10 s for the answer; if none comes, the ask goes by the last read, as before. Tests: `TestMergeAskReadsThePRFirst`, `TestRefreshWaitsForTheRead`. |
| V0.194 | → V0.188, V0.191, V0.164 (7), decision 0032 (slice 2). **Undeclared work with several heavy parts takes turns instead of stopping whole.** (1) **The parts:** from each root of the episode down to where the memory fans out, a child counting when it holds at least 10 % of its parent's subtree; a part keeps being one while its memory changes. (2) **The freeze:** with two or more parts one runs and the others are frozen, each in `agents/<thread>/undeclared/u<pid>/`; the runner above them keeps running. With one part, or when the cgroups refuse, the work is frozen whole as before. (3) **The turn** lasts 3 × the next part's memory ÷ the read-back rate measured in the last turn (`pgmajfault` of the thread's cgroup), at least 30 s and at most 2 min, and moves at once when its part ended; it is applied at every tick, so a part started since is frozen too. (4) The rules of V0.188 stay: the freeze is one of the episode's 8, memory fine for 30 s or 10 minutes ends it, and the row still says paused. (5) **Logged:** `guard: taking turns` and `guard: turn`. Tests: `internal/cgroup/rotate_test.go` R1–R8, `internal/engine/rotate_test.go` T1–T7, `TestRealTurns` against the kernel (`FYR_REAL_CGROUP=1` in a delegated scope), `internal/engine/turns_real_test.go` under real memory pressure. |
| V0.198 | → V0.194, V0.188, decision 0029 S3, decision 0032 (slice 3). **A thread can have a memory limit of its own, a slot raises it, and the log says what the limit does, or would do while it is off.** (1) `cgroups.threadMemoryHighPercent` (module option of the same name, default 0, off): each thread's `memory.high` is that share of `agentsMemoryHigh`, set at each sampler tick; `self-deploy` and `probe` are left alone; off sets every thread back to `max` without a restart. (2) **A slot** raises the limit by 1.25 × the job class's learned peak; at the whole budget or for a job of unknown size the thread has no limit of its own; the release lowers it. (3) **Swap valve:** with the agents' swap at 90 % of its cap all thread limits are lifted, since `memory.high` without swap room is a livelock. (4) **The log**, for tuning: `guard: budget limit`, `budget over` (once a minute per thread, working memory over the limit with swap, read-back rate, high events, memory PSI, slot), `budget summary`, `budget limits lifted/back`. While the share is off `budget over` runs with `mode=shadow` against 12 % and nothing is limited. (5) `/api/load` gives each thread's `memoryHigh`. **Not shown:** that the share protects other threads; two runs did not (decision 0032). Tests: `internal/cgroup/threadbudget_test.go` B1–B12, `threadbudget_real_test.go` against the kernel. |

Not in v0 as first planned: phone, tailnet, pairing, push, hours, governor, policy file, async effects, handover, away strip, palette. This line describes the first plan; the rows above built many of them since.

## 1. Concepts

The core has ten concepts.

| Concept | Kind | Definition |
|---|---|---|
| **Host** | data | A daemon on one machine. It owns one event log, the entities that reside on it, checkouts and processes. |
| **Entity** | data | A durable thing with an id, a kind, one residence host, a residence epoch, and its own event sequence. |
| **Ref** | data | A typed pointer to a thing that has its own state. Every Entity is also a Ref. |
| **Link** | data | A typed relation from a thread to a Ref. |
| **Document** | data | An immutable, typed, structured record. |
| **Event** | mechanic | An append-only record of one change. All state is a projection of events. |
| **Action** | mechanic | A named operation with typed parameters. It is the only way a principal changes state. |
| **Rule** | mechanic | `when Condition then ActionCall`, owned by an Entity. It is the only way time or outside state causes an Action. |
| **Job** | mechanic | A durable, resumable unit of side-effect work with a resource class. |
| **Attention** | derived | A pure function from an entity's projection to its display state and sort position. |

**Entity kinds.**
- The core defines three built-in kinds:
  - **Thread:** one unit of work.
  - **Decision:** a question to the user that blocks or steers work.
  - **Coordinator:** an agent that oversees threads within a signed grant.
- Modules register more kinds, for example `workflow`, `schedule` and `budget`.

**Conversation.** A component that Thread and Coordinator share. It holds the Agent sessions, the runs, usage, and handover. A **Session** is one agent session. A **Run** is one agent turn.

**Brief.** The Document schema `brief` (3.9).

**Module.** The unit of packaging (12). A module adds behaviour only through extension points.

**User.** A person with an account on a host (3.12). v1 has exactly one.

**View.** The state that belongs to one user's view of one entity: snooze, pin, read watermark, failure acknowledgement (3.12). It is not part of the entity's work state.

**Principal:**
- `user(userId, deviceId)`;
- `agent(entityId)`: the agent of a thread;
- `coordinator(entityId)`;
- `rule(ruleId)`;
- `system(component)`: the core, a Job or a module.

**Lineage root.** For a thread, the first entity in its `lineage` chain (itself if it has none). Quotas (5.2) and mode ceilings (5.2) are kept on the lineage root.

## 2. Architecture

- **Host daemon.** One per machine. It runs the core and the configured modules.
- **Clients.** A client connects to each host separately and shows each entity from its residence. Clients do not use the mesh.
- **Client origin.** The web client is served from one origin, `clientOrigin`, which is the same in every host's config. Hosts accept client connections only from that origin. Passkeys are registered for that origin.
- **Reachability.** A tailnet is optional. Installs link over mutual TLS pinned to install keys (decision 0030), and the client keeps one uplink to its front (3.17). There is no `tsnet` (16).
- **Host list.**
  - Each device stores `(hostId, url, deviceToken)` for each host.
  - The export contains `hostId` and `url` only. An import requires pairing again.
- **Offline host.**
  - Clients keep its last known state.
  - If no host is reachable, the client shows one banner for all hosts, not a marker per row.
  - Actions on an offline host's entities are unavailable with the reason "host offline". Captures go to the client outbox (13.1 U23).
- **Clocks.**
  - Each host stamps events with a hybrid logical clock (HLC) value `at`. `at` is used for display and for sorting across hosts.
  - Order within one entity uses `eseq` (3.2), never `at`.
  - A Rule's time Conditions use the wall clock of its owner's residence.

**Mesh (D3, post-v1).**
- **Pairing.** Every host pairs with every other host (`host.pairPeer`, 11.3).
- **Replication.** Each host subscribes to the `summary` stream of each peer and keeps a read-only replica of it. The stream holds:
  - the projections of replicated entities (without messages);
  - Links, Ref states and Document headers;
  - `level.evaluated` events;
  - the bodies of `grant` and `policy` Documents, pushed eagerly.
- **Document bodies.** A host fetches other Document bodies on demand with `document.get` from the Document's host.
- **Liveness.** Every peer sends a heartbeat on the summary stream every 10 s. A replica with no heartbeat for 30 s is **stale**. A Condition that depends on a stale replica is `unknown` (7.2).
- **Peer trust.** A peer is trusted to report its own residents' state truthfully. A peer is not trusted to assert a principal: a command from a peer carries the original principal and its proof (a grant signature, an approval proof, or a Job id whose `job.requested` event the receiving host can verify in the sender's replica). A host rejects a `coordinator(id)` command when its replica of the coordinator's residence is stale or the grant is not the head of its `supersedes` chain (K24).
- **Scale.** The target is N ≤ 6 hosts.

## 3. Data model

This section states the model as built. `internal/core` is the reference: when this text and the code disagree, the code is right and this text has a bug. Each subsection is built unless it is marked **Post-v1**. A post-v1 part is designed and kept here, and no code does it yet. Section numbers are stable IDs (`PROCESS.md`), so new subsections come after 3.17.

### Records

The state is a set of typed records (decision 0020, part 1). A record has one owner host (decision 0016): no record has two writers. Every change to a record in this table goes through the one path of 5.1, with the exceptions named below. Messages are in the event log and the conversation. They are not part of a record.

| Record | Holds | Stored | Key | Section |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thread | one agent conversation and its state that is not messages | `threads` (JSON) | thread id | 3.4 |
| View | one user's state of one thread | `views` | user and thread id | 3.4 |
| Change | one change a thread makes or asks for | inside its Thread | change id, key | 3.13 |
| Host change record | one observed git branch on the host | `changes` | change id; one per repo and branch | 3.14 |
| Decision | a question or approval for the owner | inside its Thread | decision id | 3.5 |
| Rule | an armed watch | inside its Thread, or the View (snooze) | rule id | 7.1 |
| Effect | work Fyr runs after a reduce | `effects` (durable effects only) | effect id | 5.10 |
| Receipt | the result of one command | `receipts` | command id | 5.1 |
| Project | a name, a colour and one folder | `projects` | project id | 3.15 |
| Provider | an agent CLI, an auth, an endpoint and models | `providers` | provider id | 3.16 |

**Events.** One table `events {seq, at, subject, type, data}` per host. `seq` increases per host; `subject` is the thread id (or the change id of a host record). A reduce appends its events in the same transaction as the record (5.1).

**Stored data keeps loading, through one versioned migration (V0.versioned-store).** The key-value table holds the store's schema version (`store.schema`, the number of migrations the store has run). `store.Open` runs the migrations after it, in order (`internal/store/migrate.go`), each in one transaction with the version it reaches: a migration that fails leaves the old version, and the next open runs it again. A store a newer build migrated opens as it is (a rollback). Migration 1 is the load passes of V0.98 (7), V0.105 (2), V0.changes-code-only (4) and V0.structure-cost (2) (`core.Migrate`, `core.MigrateRecord`). Shapes stored before a build of 16 Nov 2026 (V0.100) are no longer read: a store with no version that still holds one (the thread's PR, settle fields, actions' old places, `settle.when`, `deployWatch`, a view's `snoozeUntil`, a project's `sources` or `auto`) is refused at open with the records named, and is opened with an older build or moved aside.

**Outside the one path (V0.spec-from-code).** Four kinds of state change without an Input of 5.1:
1. **The front's own state and the owner's preferences** (`internal/front`, 3.17). The front has its own vocabulary, the `front.*` commands sent to `POST /api/front/command` (`front.hostOrder`, `front.projectOrder`, `front.hostMark`, `front.hostId`, `front.projectAdd`, `front.projectLink`, `front.projectUnlink`, `front.projectShared`, `front.threadHandover`), and keeps the result in its own key-value store, beside the device data: sessions, push subscriptions and their settings, presence, the peer roster. A `front.*` command that changes a host's record sends that host an ordinary command (`project.link`, `project.shared`, `project.create`), which takes 5.1 there.
2. **The guard's runtime state** (held by the engine as a `guard.State`). The policy is `internal/guard` (`guard.Tick` over `guard.State`, `guard.Admission`, `Sweep`, `JobLimit`, `Budget`), its vocabulary `internal/guard/resource`, the queue `internal/slots`, the books `internal/heavy`, and the host's `guard.Mechanism` `internal/cgroup` (`cgroup.Mechanism`, or `ProcMechanism` on a host without cgroups). Heavy-job admission (the queue, the slots, the learned costs and the budget), the memory guard's freezes and thaws, and the leftover processes it ends or an agent keeps (`keep_process`) live in the engine's memory and on the host, not in records. What a thread must hear of it lands as an Input (`leftover.ended`), which takes 5.1.
